# RaisinDB read/index/resolve plan

**Status (2026-10-03):** Phases 0, 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 DONE (committed in ffebb7be); Phase 0b DONE (uncommitted) — the oracle found and fixed eleven bugs across the shipped phases and the replication path, and a review pass fixed seven more (items 12–18), and lists six expected failures with their owning phases. Phase 10 DONE (uncommitted; read rule and backfill). Phase 4 DONE (uncommitted; `sql.batched_fetch` rollback flag, measured 7–14 % on harness C in a debug build, not the hoped 2×). Phase 7 DONE (uncommitted): one delta writer for PROPERTY_INDEX (UNIQUE, REFERENCE and ORDERED_CHILDREN deltas beside it), `index.skip_unchanged` gated per branch on this node's `property_index` rebuild, harness H 42 → 6 entries per update. Phase 7b DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-04): commit-order-independent index deltas — a per-node, in-process commit lock (`indexing::node_lock`, the existing `KeyedMutex`) taken by every local write funnel and by the replication applicator, under which each commit re-validates its staged delta against the versions stored at that moment; `index.skip_unchanged` is now ON by default, still gated per branch, with the `property_index` rebuild queued automatically per branch in the background; the Phase 7 known gap (two overlapping writers committing out of revision order) is closed. Phase 10b DONE (uncommitted): by owner decision there is no release N / N+1 split — the gate is gone and every writer (transaction, repository, merge, replication, backup import) stores the one record format; downgrading below this release is unsupported. Phase 8 DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-04): one compound writer, a revision-bounded newest-per-`(tuple, node)` reader, derived tombstones, and COMPOUND + UNIQUE entries written by the replication apply path and merge resolutions from a definitions cache; replica compound queries are index-served again, and the oracle's compound expected failure is pruned. Phase 9 DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-04): run-collapse GC as the admin-triggered `collapse_runs` repair, default off (`history_gc_collapse_runs`), single node only — it refuses in cluster mode because no trustworthy causal-stability watermark exists — plus the merge-base pin in retention GC. Phase 11 DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-04): one revision-correct translation reader and one atomic writer, overlays replicate as the one op `UpsertTranslationOverlay` (no legacy op, no emission flag — owner decision: no cluster is in use, so no old-op compatibility is carried), `OpType::Unknown` skip-and-warn, the `resync_translations` repair, the per-branch `translation_history_complete_from` floor, and merge apply writing translations at M — three oracle expected failures pruned. Phase 11b DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-04): the owner decision is confirmed in code (no emission flag, no legacy translation ops; a saved legacy op decodes as `Unknown` and is skipped, now tested end to end), every Phase 11 review finding is verified fixed, and the remaining legacy replication code is listed for an owner decision; its review then replaced the write-time node-delete translation tombstones (racy on the origin, between concurrent peer batches and across checkpoint ingest) with a read rule in the one reader plus a history-GC materialization, stored transaction translation metas, and stopped legacy `SetProperty` resurrecting deleted nodes. Phase 11c DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-04): a node delete ends its BLOCK overlays too — the node-delete read rule now covers `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS`, the delete funnel, the one writer (late versions) and history GC store the delete's `T` for them, and the automatically queued `block_overlay_tombstones` repair cleans up existing data. Phase 12 (with 12.0) DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-04): the localized name index — on by default (owner decision; `RAISIN_LOCALIZED_NAME_INDEX=0` turns it off), one `sync` writer riding every record, overlay and delete funnel, a fingerprinted and generation-guarded state per (branch, workspace) flipped in the config change's own batch, a pinned streaming rebuild on the repair machinery queued automatically per branch, verified lookups with an always-correct row-level fallback, and SQL (`RESOLVE_PATH`, `__node_name`, `__localized_path`, `LocalizedPathLookup`), HTTP, WS and JS SDK surfaces; a new oracle template guards it. A localized name is the TRANSLATED NODE NAME (`/__node_name` overlay) and nothing else (owner decision, 2026-10-04: no `url_{locale}` source, no "slug" naming); a review pass then fixed thirteen findings (see the Phase 12 "Review fixes"). Phase 11d DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-04): the pre-v2 granular node ops are gone — the promotion prune (their last emitter) rides in its `ApplyRevision` as a `Delete` change, the thirteen `OpType` variants, their apply arms, builders, the oplog compactor, the path-less keep-path record writer and the defaulted-workspace scan fallback are deleted, and a saved entry of any of them decodes as `Unknown` and is skipped (tested end to end on the applied state). Phase 13a DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-04): a promotion stages everything it ENDS (displaced occupants, stale placements, given-up UNIQUE claims) before any put, a mover is never taken for a displaced occupant, the commit-time correction and every claim-ending tombstone spare a claim another node holds (in the commit or committed), and replicated deletes and cold replicated upserts end the claims the node owns, found from the index rather than the definitions cache. Phase 13b DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-05): release-build profiling of the SQL point lookup, RESOLVE and the localized lookup — the optimizer's Debug-string change detection, a per-statement function registry and JSON round trips in the projection were 65 % of a point lookup; with those fixed and a prepared-statement cache (analysis + logical plan per catalog and SQL text, never the physical plan or the caller) a point lookup by path is 10 µs (`SELECT id`, 1.7× the storage get) / 20 µs (`SELECT *`, 3.3×) instead of 54 / 111 µs, and a localized SQL lookup 38 µs instead of 118 µs at depth 3. Phase 13c DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-05): localized-name sibling uniqueness on every local translation write path — the transaction overlay write now checks at staging (a package install rejects the one colliding overlay, not its batch) and the commit judges every sibling, stored or staged in the same transaction, by its final view; replication, merge and promotion never refuse. Phase 13d DONE (uncommitted, 2026-10-05): native speed — one optimizer round (verified in every debug build), a parameterized plan cache keyed on the text with placeholders (pgwire Parse/Bind/Execute, HTTP, WS and functions share it), a physical plan cache re-checking index availability, RESOLVE over stored values instead of JSON, no `has_children` probe on SQL point reads, and the localized lookup on one snapshot-pinned iterator set with a projected overlay decode; point lookups 1.5–2.5× of the storage get, prepared statements with a new value 2–4×, RESOLVE 1.5–1.7× of its floor (the cold-text and RESOLVE targets were not fully reached; evidence in the phase). Everything else PLANNED. Phase 2 still owes its operational gate: a prod-snapshot dry run of both repairs (the replica compound latency measurement of item 5 is moot now that Phase 8 step 3 keeps replica indexes `Ready`). The binding amendments from `read-index-resolve-plan-critique.md` (three critics: replication, ops/migration, history/branch) are folded into the phases below; the critique file stays as the record of why.

This document combines nine research dossiers: propindex, haschildren, readpath, translation, linkrewrite, coreindex, resolve, invariants and sota. I re-read the code wherever two dossiers disagreed.

## 0. Conflicts I resolved by reading the code

| Question | Dossiers disagreed | Verified fact | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| Does HTTP compute `has_children` itself, so that storage `get` can stop? | readpath said yes (via `util.rs`); haschildren said `util.rs` is dead | `raisin-transport-http/src/util.rs::populate_has_children{,_batch}` has **no callers**. HTTP and WS responses serialize whatever storage `get` returned. | **Superseded by the owner (D4).** `has_children` stays populated on all storage reads. Phase 1 made it cheap instead (a limit-1 probe), so the ~33 internal callers no longer pay O(children × revisions) and no consumer has to change. |
| Does the delete tombstone of ORDERED_CHILDREN mask the live entry? | Only haschildren flagged it | `core_tombstones.rs:76-89` keys the tombstone by `node.parent`. `definition.rs:232-243` documents `parent` as "ONLY the parent node's name". The index is keyed by parent **id**. | Confirmed bug. Fixed in Phase 2. |
| Merge PATH_INDEX tombstone value | Only coreindex flagged it | `merge/apply.rs:29`: `const INDEX_TOMBSTONE: &[u8] = b"\x00";`. Every reader compares against `b"T"`. | Confirmed bug. Fixed in Phase 2 by making readers accept both, with the data repair as cleanup only. |
| Localized lookup: full-path index (sota #7) or per-parent segment index (translation, coreindex)? | | A full-path index needs O(subtree × locales) rewrites on every move, rename or ancestor name edit, and it would mirror about 30 PATH_INDEX write sites. | **Segment index** (one localized segment per node, per parent). The full-path index is rejected. |
| How RESOLVE should "know what is there before descending" | sota: stamp `has_refs` on the node; coreindex: probe the forward reference index; resolve: frontier walk | The resolver must decode every target anyway in order to inline it. | **No new stored state.** Compute each target's outgoing references once, at fetch time, with the one walker, and recurse only along that frontier. RLS and existence are decided per target *before* its references are collected. A path-only RLS check runs before the blob is even read, except under graph RLS. |
| Property-index cleanup: compaction filter or history_gc? | sota #5 and propindex (c): filter; propindex (d): run-collapse | A compaction filter cannot see lookahead or a causal-stability watermark. | **Run-collapse inside history_gc**, but only below a cluster-wide watermark and only after the Phase 2 repairs (see Phase 9; the earlier "lossless, pins irrelevant" claim was wrong). The lossy filter is deferred. |
| Rewriting links on plain reads | | Studio and the CLI read properties over SQL and write them back, so read-time rewriting would permanently replace internal links with public URLs. | **Rejected.** Use an explicit `REWRITE_LINKS()` only. |

## Guiding rules

These come from CLAUDE.md, project memory and the critique, and every phase follows them:

- **One writer, one reader, one walker per concern.** The largest bug class in this codebase is mirrored paths that drift apart, and several phases below exist only to collapse duplicates. Merge apply is one of those mirrored funnels (Phase 2.10).
- **Nothing derived replicates as an op, but checkpoints ship every CF.** Every new or changed index is maintained inline by the replication apply path and rebuilt locally on each node. Checkpoint bootstrap, however, is a put-merge of *every* CF from the peer, derived indexes and INDEX_STATUS included, and emits no events. So for every repair and every new CF the phase must say what checkpoint transfer does to it.
- **Rebuild leases.** Local index builds must not hold a *cluster-wide* lease: a lease held by the node that does not need the build leaves the node that does need it unbuilt. The compound build handler takes one today; Phase 2.11 scopes it per node. (The earlier rule "rebuild jobs take no lease" contradicted that existing code.)
- **New derived state ships behind a fail-closed state record**, with a row-level fallback that keeps results correct during the migration window (the spatial/compound template). State records use **tenant-first** keys that include repo, branch and workspace, and are revalidated after checkpoint ingest.
- **No change to the on-disk key format of an existing CF.** Every phase leaves existing data readable as-is. Migrations are additive (new CF, or new tombstones written at historically correct revisions).
- **New CFs ship registration-first.** A release that only declares the CF (empty, in `all_column_families`) ships one release before any writer, and the checkpoint ingestor skips and warns on unknown CFs. Downgrading below the registration release is unsupported.
- **Historical-revision writers.** Any writer that inserts below existing entries (repairs, rebuilds, out-of-order replication, `versionable=false`) must be accounted for by every reader and by Phase 9. A derived-index writer tombstones at `max(R, newest existing entry revision for that group)`, or allocates a fresh revision when the group's newest entry is above R.
- **Concurrency.** Cargo runs from the coordinator only, one crate at a time, using the narrow filters listed in the invariants dossier. Every new integration test is a module under `tests/all/`, never a new binary.

### Repair and rebuild discipline (applies to every repair, backfill and rebuild job below)

- **Reader-tolerant first.** A repair is cleanup, never the fix. Either readers tolerate the corrupt data permanently, or the repair is re-triggered by the checkpoint ingest hook (the place that already calls `invalidate_all_derived_caches`). Otherwise a later checkpoint from an unrepaired peer silently undoes it.
- **Detect from the data, not from "done" flags.** Every repair is idempotent and finds its targets by inspecting the data, so it is correct after a checkpoint ingest and after a crash.
- **Streaming and bounded.** Never the existing rebuild routine that loads a workspace into memory and commits one WriteBatch. Stream the CF, commit bounded batches (4–16 MB), persist a resumable cursor, rate-limit (or low IO priority), report progress per branch.
- **Disk precheck.** Refuse to start without headroom of at least 2× the affected CF on the data volume. Compaction afterwards runs by key range in bounded slices with a rate limiter, outside the backup window. Reclaimed space only appears after backup retention rotates, because checkpoints hard-link SSTs (prod holds ~207 GB of backups beside the data dir). The dry run reports the bytes it expects to write or reclaim.
- **Admin-triggered, never at boot, never on all nodes at once (D11).** One exception, by owner decision (2026-10-03): the Phase 10b `node_path` backfill queues itself after the job system starts, one branch at a time, on every branch where it has not completed on that node.
- **Fan-out mechanism.** Admin-triggered repairs and rebuilds use **(b) an admin endpoint that fans out to every peer** and reports per-node status; each node writes a per-node state record (`{tenant}\0{repo}\0{branch}\0repair_state\0{repair}\0{node_id}`) so the console can show which nodes have not repaired. Job dedup is per-process and there is no replicated "rebuild requested" op, so without this the result is N-1 nodes that stay broken and report nothing. Rebuilds that are *caused by a replicated config change* (Phase 12 default language) are instead enqueued by the `UpdateRepository` apply arm on every node, local and remote, i.e. on the "local + remote" side of `node_handlers.rs:133/162`.

## Phase 0 — Measurement harness — DONE

**Goal.** Give every later phase a reproducible before/after number and an operation-count assertion, so improvements are measured rather than reasoned.

**Files.**
- New `crates/raisin-sql-execution/tests/all/index_read_bench.rs`, added with `mod index_read_bench;` in `tests/all/main.rs`, all `#[ignore]`. It reuses `bootstrap()` and `engine()` from `throughput_sql.rs:102/210`.
- New `crates/raisin-rocksdb/tests/all/perf_counters.rs`, a helper that wraps a closure in RocksDB `PerfContext` (`set_perf_stats(EnableCount)`) and returns `{seek, next, get, internal_key_skipped, block_cache_hit/miss, bytes_written}`.
- `crates/raisin-rocksdb/benches/rocksdb_benchmarks.rs`: add `get_by_id/{revs}`, `get_at_old_revision/{revs}`, `get_by_path/{children}`, `append_children/{N}` (exposes the O(N²) parent lookup) and `update_unchanged_props/{P}` (write amplification: count PROPERTY_INDEX keys per update).
- A counting `Storage` wrapper in a test helper that counts `nodes().get*` calls (RESOLVE fetch counts).
- `QueryEngine` phase timers (parse / analyze / plan / execute) behind a `RAISIN_SQL_PHASE_TIMING` env flag in `raisin-sql-execution/src/engine/mod.rs`. This is the only product-code touch; it is a no-op when the flag is unset.

**Scenarios.** Results are written as JSON lines to `target/bench/*.jsonl`, so before and after can be diffed.
- **A.** Point lookup by an unchanged text property (`properties->>'slug'::String = $1`) on a node edited N ∈ {1, 10, 100, 1000} times, among M = 10k distractor nodes.
- **B.** The same query at `__revision = early`.
- **C.** `SELECT RESOLVE(properties, 2)` over a header/footer/settings fixture (about 25 references, 3 shared), as one statement and as N = 50 rows that share targets.
- **D.** `SELECT *` with embeddings configured.
- **E.** `get_by_path` on a parent with 1k children, each edited 10 times.
- **F.** `CHILD_OF` / `DESCENDANT_OF` over 3 levels × 20 children.
- **G.** Append 1k children under one parent, through the repository path and the transaction path.
- **H.** Update one property on a node with P = 30 properties; count index puts and bytes.
- **I.** Baseline for localized lookup: `properties->>'url_fr'::String = $1`, plus a per-segment walk. *(Obsolete since Phase 12 removed the `url_{locale}` source; replaced by Phase 13b's scenario L.)*
- **J.** Parse/plan share of a sub-millisecond point query (decides whether Phase 14 is worth doing).

**Index advisor (report-only).** One `debug!` counter per predicate shape that falls back to a table scan, aggregated in the harness output. This is the only adaptive-indexing work justified now; see §6.

**Migration / replication / branch.** None.
**Rollback.** Delete the files.
**Expected gain.** None directly. It is the baseline for everything else.
**Risk.** None. Tests are ignored and run on demand.

## Phase 0b — MVCC index oracle (the guard for Phases 5–9) — DONE (uncommitted)

**Goal.** One property test that turns any index or MVCC regression into a shrunk, minimal failing history.

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-03).** `crates/raisin-sql-execution/tests/all/mvcc_index_oracle/` (one module of the `all` test target; every file under ~300 lines):
- `model.rs` — the independent reference model (parent **ids**, explicit sibling `Vec`s, timestamps as the op sequence that stamped them); `ops.rs` — the generated op log (abstract selectors resolved against the model at apply time, so shrinking stays meaningful); `driver.rs`, `write_ops.rs`, `content_ops.rs`, `copy_restore.rs`, `merge.rs`, `merge_model.rs` — each op through a real funnel (transaction `put_node`/`add_node`, repository `update`/`delete`/`move_node_tree`/`rename_node`, SQL `UPDATE` and `RESTORE`, `copy_node_tree`, `store_translation`, reorder, fork → edits on both sides → `merge_branches` + `resolve_merge_with_resolutions`) while advancing the model, recording a snapshot at every revision a branch HEAD reached.
- `checks/` — the templates, asked at every recorded revision of every branch (`__revision = …`), and at each branch's newest snapshot a second time in the **HEAD form** (no `__revision`; the planner then picks other readers): `cf::NODES` decoded straight off `storage.db()` (live set, identity, properties) and `Node.order_key == ORDERED_CHILDREN label`; `get_by_path` (id, name, **path**, `has_children`) plus every vacated path resolving to nothing; `list_by_parent` + `has_children`; `list_ordered_children_page` both directions; `CHILD_OF ORDER BY __order` (both directions); `DESCENDANT_OF ORDER BY __tree_order` in keyset pages of 2; property equality (string and number); `node_type =`; `REFERENCES` (including a probe of a node the model says nothing references); the compound index (`CHILD_OF … AND node_type = … ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 3` — a bare `CHILD_OF` plans as a PrefixScan, the known-failing `compound_index_hierarchy` test); `ORDER BY created_at/updated_at ASC/DESC LIMIT 4` as tie-groups; timestamp ranges between wall-clock instants recorded between ops; `COUNT(*)` (all, by type, by property); `RESOLVE(properties, 2)`; a `locale = 'fr'` read; and, at HEAD, that the next appended label sorts after every live child. EXPLAIN of every shape is checked by `debug::oracle_explain_plans`.
- `stages.rs` — **stage 1** `oracle_histories` (random histories); **stage 2** `oracle_history_gc` (`gc.rs`: a tag pin on a random `main` snapshot, `run_history_gc` with `keep_revisions` 1..12, every snapshot at or above its branch's reported cutoff plus the pinned one re-asked); **stage 3** `oracle_replication_replay` (`replay.rs`: two origins with replication capture, sharing a bootstrap — A creates `/a` and `/b`, B receives them through the applicator — each running its own history beneath its own folder, alternating op by op so their HLCs interleave; both oplogs then reach a third storage either in a random interleaving that keeps each origin's order, or **fully shuffled through the production `CausalDeliveryBuffer`**; the replica is asked every template at every revision either origin recorded, against the union of the two models); `expected_failures_still_fail`; `regression_witnesses_pass` and `replay_witnesses_pass` (every fixed bug's witness history, `witnesses_fixed.rs`, and one stage-3 witness per op kind, `replay_witnesses.rs`).
- `PROPTEST_CASES` defaults to 16 (the default run of all stages takes ~22 s in debug); `ORACLE_OPS=min..max` sets history length (default 20..40, the full 20..60 is supported and swept clean). Triage aids, all `#[ignore]`d, in `debug.rs`: `ORACLE_SWEEP` (random sweeps that do not stop at the first failure), `ORACLE_ONLY=<template>` (shrink one finding), `ORACLE_WITNESS`/`ORACLE_REPLAY_WITNESS`, `ORACLE_DUMP` (raw CF dumps).
- Phase 6's templates (property and pseudo-property equality, `ORDER BY created_at/updated_at`, timestamp ranges, `COUNT(*)` at a past revision) are asserted outright — none is on the expected-failure list.
- Product-code touches for the oracle itself: `raisin_rocksdb::decode_node_blob` (`mvcc_read/node_decode.rs`, the same format order as the one decoder, for reading `cf::NODES` directly) and `NodeRepositoryImpl::last_order_label_for_append` (`ordering/queries.rs`); `raisin-replication` became a dev-dependency of `raisin-sql-execution`.

**Bugs the oracle found and that are fixed** (each has a witness in `witnesses_fixed.rs` or `replay_witnesses.rs`):
1. Transaction `add_node`/`put_node` wrote the node blob BEFORE minting the ORDERED_CHILDREN label, so every transaction-written record stored `order_key = ""` (`Node.order_key == label` broken). The ordering step now runs first.
2. `copy_node_tree`: the copied ROOT reused the source's label (landing at the source's position) or, under the root, passed a FULL label to `inc` (fails on `::`, fell back to a duplicate suffix-less `first()`), and every copy's blob kept the SOURCE's `order_key`. The root now appends through `next_append_label`; descendants keep their labels; `order_key` is the label. Copies also never got compound-index entries (now added).
3. Reorder, rebalance and the repository tree move re-stamp `updated_at` (and a move can change `name`) but wrote only the NEW property-index entries; the timestamp reader met the stale entry first and dropped the node from `ORDER BY updated_at` and ranges. New shared `rewrite_node_record_to_batch` tombstones what the old record indexed.
4. Descending ORDERED_CHILDREN scans visited a label group oldest-revision first, resurrecting entries beneath a newer tombstone (every reorder leaves one): `ORDER BY __order DESC` listed reordered nodes at their old position. The group is now buffered and resolved newest-per-child (`ordering/paged_desc.rs`).
5. `get_last_order_label`'s fallback returned the most recently WRITTEN label, not the last in editorial order, so after a reorder-to-front the next append landed before every sibling; it now returns the greatest fractional label (stale labels counted, which only over-shoots — Phase 7 item 6's newest-wins live-only refinement has since landed: `last_live_order_label`). Renames in place (repository and transaction) no longer cache their middle label as LAST, and a branch copy (merge) now invalidates the target's LAST cache for every parent it touched.
6. The transaction `move_node_tree` was a mirrored funnel: `inc` on a full label (duplicate `first()`), no property-index maintenance for re-stamped records, no compound re-key, and it replicated only the moved root (replicas kept every descendant at its old path). All four fixed; descendants are now tracked (`ChangeTracker::track_path_change`).
7. Readers materialized NODE_PATH at the BLOB's revision instead of the read's, returning the pre-move path for any node whose ancestor moved after its last write (`scan_descendants_ordered`, list-all, two `mvcc.rs` time-travel readers, and the transaction `get_node` — which made `move_node_tree` miss the old parent and leave the node listed under both). They now read the path as of the read.
8. `REFERENCES()` at `__revision = N` ignored N (HEAD index state): new `ReferenceIndexRepository::find_referencing_nodes_at` (default = HEAD for the memory backend), used by the executor. The delete-safety check had its own reverse-index scan that counted ANY live entry, so a reference removed long ago blocked deleting its target forever; both now read through one `live_referrers`.
9. Compound index scans returned `ORDER BY created_at DESC` oldest-first: the planner passed the query direction while `Timestamp` columns are stored newest-first. `ascending` is now the INDEX direction (EXPLAIN prints `index-order`/`reverse-index-order`).
10. `RESTORE NODE id=…` looked the historical node up by its CURRENT path ("not found" whenever it or an ancestor had moved since); it now reads by id.
11. Replication: a replicated reorder left the old label live (only moves tombstoned it); replicas now tombstone on any relabel and stamp `order_key` from the replicated label. `versionable=false` updates never replicated (no transaction revision ⇒ capture skipped); they are now captured at the revision they overwrote, and the replica's baseline includes the version AT that revision so its stale index values are tombstoned.

**Review pass (2026-10-03, two reviewers' findings; uncommitted).** Fixed, each with a regression test named after the failure:
12. A transaction's captured `ApplyRevision` carried EVERY change at one revision (the max), while `versionable=false` updates sit at their own nodes' revisions: the replica wrote a volatile node at a revision the origin never wrote it at, and the next in-place refresh landed beneath it. Capture now emits one op per distinct revision (`capture.rs::group_changes_by_revision`; `capture::tests::two_volatile_updates_in_one_transaction_replicate_at_their_own_revisions`, replay witnesses `volatile_pair_in_one_tx`, `volatile_beside_versioned_in_one_tx`).
13. A merge re-copied every source entry up to the source HEAD — pre-fork keys included — over the target's, reverting a target-side in-place write made after the fork (record AND property entries). The copy now never overwrites a key the target already holds (one merge-join cursor, `branches/copy_existing.rs`), and the LAST-cache invalidation covers only parents that received new entries, in bounded batches, and none on a fresh fork (`main_in_place_write_survives_merge`; fork edits gained a main-side `Edit::Volatile`).
14. The transactional move minted its append label from committed state alone, so a create and a move into one parent in one transaction minted byte-identical labels. One transactional minter, `create/append_label.rs::next_append_label_tx` (transaction cache first), used by both create paths and the move, over one shared `mint_append_label` (`create_and_move_into_one_parent_in_one_tx`, `move_and_create_into_one_parent_in_one_tx`; the oracle gained `Op::Tx`, several writes in one transaction).
15. The replica's relabel tombstone took the old label from the stored blob, which is `""` for every pre-fix transaction-written node (and the SOURCE's label for legacy copies); it now reads the label from ORDERED_CHILDREN as of the applied revision (`stored_order_label_at`, the one stored-label reader), only relabels on an origin-supplied label, and capture reads the label as of the change's revision (`replicated_reorder_of_a_node_with_an_empty_stored_order_key_tombstones_its_old_label`).
16. `put_ordered_child` repopulated an ABSENT LAST cache with whatever label it was handed (typically an existing child's middle label, from a replicated update or a merge resolution); it now advances only a present cache, by fractional order (`replicated_middle_label_does_not_repopulate_an_invalidated_last_cache`).
17. RESTORE keeps the node's current name, parent and label along with its path (`restore_after_move`, `restore_after_rename`; the model now allows any earlier revision as the target).
18. Out-of-order apply (new stage-3 `Delivery::Unbuffered`: shuffled, no causal buffer; every replay witness runs it over four seeds in `replay_witnesses_pass_out_of_order`) found four apply-path defects, all fixed: two in-place writes at one revision applied oldest-last (now `updated_at` decides, `newer_version::stale_in_place`); the out-of-order placement check resolved the newer version's parent by path at HEAD (now: is this very entry live at the newer revision); an old path was tombstoned even when another node had since taken it (`path_owned_at`); the old parent was resolved by path at HEAD (now as of the old version's revision, `parent_index_id`). Also: copying a node whose newest translation overlay is a tombstone failed the copy (now skipped).

Oracle changes from the same pass: the fork window's `main` snapshots are asserted STRICTLY just before the merge (`merge.rs::check_window_before_merge`; afterwards they are `retro`), so main-side edits inside a fork are checked at their own revisions; the historical compound tolerance is narrowed from the whole template to answers that differ ONLY in rows a later move, rename, retype or delete re-keyed, with every other row exact and in order (`checks/order.rs::rekeyed_rows_only`, tag `[re-keyed rows]`).

Found, NOT fixed (deferred, not modelled — the generator avoids them and says so in `tx_ops.rs`):
- The transactional `move_node_tree` reads the moved subtree from COMMITTED state: moving a child out and then its old parent elsewhere in ONE transaction rewrites the child's path as if it had stayed (and a node moved INTO a subtree that the same transaction then moves keeps its old path).
- Within one transaction, a write after a move resolves reference paths against committed state, not the move.
- Unbuffered replay of random histories (`ORACLE_DELIVERY=unbuffered`, opt-in): a version whose old parent's own create has not arrived yet cannot be placed (a replicated change names only the NEW parent), so its old ORDERED_CHILDREN entry stays live (~3 in 16 histories); and a past-revision compound read on a replica can still list a node that moved out (~1 in 16). The fix for the first is carrying the old parent id (or label) in `ReplicatedNodeChange` — a wire change, so it ships with a release-ordering note.
- The transactional move's compound re-key prefix-scans the whole workspace compound CF per moved node (LOW); the repository move and reorder/rebalance re-stamp `updated_at` without re-keying compound entries carrying it (LOW). Both belong with Phase 8. *(Both fixed by Phase 8: the re-key is derived, and `rewrite_node_record_to_batch` re-keys compound entries.)*

**Named expected-failure list** (`expected.rs`; each entry's witness must still produce the listed wrong result, so a fixed entry fails `expected_failures_still_fail` until it is pruned):
- ~~`locale_read_at_past_revision`~~ — overlays are read as of HEAD, not as of the revision. **Pruned by Phase 11** (revision-correct overlay reads); the witness runs in `regression_witnesses_pass`.
- `main_revision_inside_merged_fork` — merge replays the source's entries into the target at their ORIGINAL revisions, so target revisions taken between fork and merge show the fork's changes retroactively. Tolerated only AFTER the merge: the same revisions are asserted strictly just before it, and HEAD and all other revisions always. **No phase owns this yet** — the fix is merge apply writing source changes at the merge revision.
- ~~`locale_read_after_resolution_over_delete`~~ — a resolution that KEEPS a node the other side deleted loses its translation overlays (Phase 2.10's documented known gap). **Pruned by Phase 11** (node resolutions carry the kept side's overlays to M).
- ~~`compound_child_of_order_by_created_at_past_revision`~~ — **pruned by Phase 8**: the compound template is asserted exactly at every revision (the `[re-keyed rows]` tolerance and `rekeyed_after` are gone), and the witness `compound_at_past_revision` runs in `regression_witnesses_pass`.
- ~~`locale_read_on_replica`~~ — overlays do not replicate. **Pruned by Phase 11** (overlay replication); the replay witness `translate` runs in `replay_witnesses_pass` (causal and out of order).
- `nodes_cf` on a replica, never tolerated in random runs (stage 3 generates no `versionable=false` nodes until it is fixed): an in-place rewrite AFTER an ancestor moved diverges, because a move replicates a snapshot of every moved node — a NEW version on the replica that the origin never wrote — and the origin's later in-place write reuses the OLD revision, landing beneath it. No phase owns this yet.

**Deviations from the design above.**
- The localized-lookup template is not present: Phase 12's index does not exist yet. Add it with Phase 12.
- `versionable=false` "no history" is applied per node and per snapshot: a node rewritten in place AFTER a snapshot is excluded, by name, from that snapshot's content, path and `has_children` assertions (`Snapshot.tainted`); untainted volatile nodes are asserted exactly, and presence/COUNT are always asserted. Inside a fork only `main` edits volatile nodes (`Edit::Volatile`; conflict detection cannot see in-place writes, documented behaviour).
- Copies mint ids the op log cannot know: the root's id comes from the copy's return value and each descendant's from `get_node_id_by_path` of the MODEL-predicted path, read once at the copy revision (a predicted path that does not resolve is reported, not skipped). Everything else about copies is model-derived.
- The model follows two write-path behaviours it cannot derive by itself, both verified: a write re-resolves every reference's `raisin:path` to its target's current path, and a reorder to the position a node already holds is a no-op (no revision, no re-stamp). Reorders and creates inside a fork are confined per parent to one side, and names stay unique across the merge, so merged sibling order needs no label emulation; a node a resolution resurrects returns to its base position.
- Stage 3 covers one branch: forks/merges and an origin forwarding a PEER's oplog (multi-hop) are not replayed. Stage 3's two origins never create `versionable=false` nodes (see the last expected failure), and never write the SAME nodes (each owns its subtree; concurrent same-node writes need a last-writer-wins model of the union). Same-node out-of-order application is covered by `Delivery::Unbuffered` (witnesses always; random histories on request).

**Design (as planned).**
- Generate random histories of 20–60 operations: create, update (string, number, reference, nested Element/Composite), delete (with and without cascade), move subtree, rename, reorder, copy tree, set translation, fork followed by edits, **merge with resolutions (KeepOurs / KeepTheirs)**, **`versionable=false` updates**, and restore.
- Record HEAD after every commit.
- **The oracle is an independent in-memory reference model** driven by the generated operation sequence. It tracks tree and order from its own op log (parent **ids** and explicit sibling order), never from `cf::NODES`, NODE_PATH or ORDERED_CHILDREN. (The earlier design reconstructed the tree from `Node.parent`, which is the parent NAME and therefore ambiguous; read paths from NODE_PATH, which Phase 10 puts under test; and computed `__order` from ORDERED_CHILDREN, checking editorial order against itself. A NODES-derived oracle is also blind to bugs that distort NODES history itself, such as merge replaying source NODES at original revisions.)
- Compare **both** `cf::NODES` (decoded directly through `storage.db()`) and every index template against the model. Never call a repository read API from the model side.
- Assert the invariant `Node.order_key == ORDERED_CHILDREN label` for every live child.
- `versionable=false` is modelled explicitly as "no history": those nodes are excluded from historical-revision assertions rather than making them pass vacuously. Merge conflict detection does not see `versionable=false` edits (no revision is minted); the model encodes that as documented behaviour.
- For every recorded revision and every template, assert that the index-backed result equals the model. Templates: `get_by_path`, `list_by_parent` + `has_children`, `CHILD_OF ORDER BY __order`, `DESCENDANT_OF ORDER BY __tree_order` (keyset pages), property equality, `node_type =`, `REFERENCES`, compound `CHILD_OF + ORDER BY`, `ORDER BY created_at/updated_at ASC/DESC LIMIT k`, timestamp ranges, `COUNT(*)`, `RESOLVE(properties, 2)`, **`get_last_order_label` and the next appended label** (Phase 7 prerequisite), and the localized lookup (Phase 12).
- Stage 2: after `run_history_gc` with a random cutoff plus a tag pin, results at every retained revision are unchanged.
- Stage 3: replay into a second storage and check parity at HEAD **and** at every revision. Stage 3 uses **shuffled, interleaved two-origin oplogs with permuted apply order**, not a single-origin in-order replay, because `apply_replicated_upsert` has no newer-than-stored guard and its own comment names out-of-order catch-up.
- Case count comes from `PROPTEST_CASES` (default 16).

**Expected outcome.** Several templates **fail today** (see Phases 2 and 5). They go on a **named expected-failure list** whose expected (wrong) results are asserted, and the build **fails if a listed template unexpectedly passes**, so the list is pruned the day its fixing phase lands. `#[should_panic]` is not used: it passes on any panic and would mask new regressions in exactly those templates.

**Risk.** Low (test only). The reference model makes it 4–5 days rather than 2–3.

---

## Phase 1 — Stop paying for work nobody reads (has_children, logging, revision seeks) — DONE

**Goal.** Remove the largest per-row and per-lookup waste with no change to on-disk state or to results.

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-03).**
1. **has_children stays on all reads (owner decision, supersedes the earlier "None on the storage path" text and D4).** Instead of moving population to the API boundary, the computation was made cheap. `util.rs`'s dead helpers and the per-caller `populate` flags are therefore not needed.
2. **has_children is a limit-1 existence probe** (`ordering/child_probe.rs::probe_has_children`). It takes the caller's path, reads ORDERED_CHILDREN with limit 1 bounded by `max_revision`, and confirms each hit against NODES liveness. After **64 dead hits** it answers `true` and warns once, so a parent with many stale entries (Studio per-run job nodes) costs O(64), not O(deleted children), until the Phase 2.1 repair runs. `delete_without_cascade` (`cascade/single.rs`) uses the same probe.
3. **Seek instead of walk for time travel.** `get_revision_at_or_before` (`crud/helpers.rs`), `materialize_path` and the `lookup.rs` time-travel loop seek to `prefix ++ encode_descending(target)`. The seek helper **advances past a key whose revision fails to parse** (today's skip-and-continue at `crud/helpers.rs:141-153`), never returns None on it. The two `materialize_path` copies are merged into one.
4. **Log levels.** Hot-path `info!` sites became `debug!`/`trace!`; the `first_bytes` allocation moved into the error branch.
5. **Embedding column.** `warn!` once per query; "newest ≤ max_revision" instead of exact revision equality; one seek per `(embedder_hash, kind)` partition instead of the whole-workspace v2 fallback.

**Remaining items (critique, not yet done).**
6. **Moved-child check behind a stale entry.** NODES liveness alone cannot detect a child that is live but was MOVED to another parent behind a stale ORDERED_CHILDREN entry. Confirm with NODE_PATH (or a head decode) that the child's parent at ≤ rev equals the probed parent, and pass the service's `max_revision` through `GetOptions` / `ListOptions::for_api` so `rev/{n}` API reads are bounded. Test: `has_children_false_when_only_child_moved_behind_stale_entry`.
7. **package_builder's has_children consumer.** `jobs/handlers/package_create_from_selection/package_builder.rs:109` picks the folder layout (`.node.yaml`) vs the file layout from `node.has_children`. It is correct while D4 keeps has_children populated, but it is a behavioural consumer of a derived field: switch it to an explicit probe (or collected-set membership) so a future change to D4 cannot silently change the archive layout. Grep every `.has_children` read, including the functions callbacks (`raisin-functions/.../callbacks/nodes.rs` uses `storage.nodes()` directly) and MCP surfaces, and record which keep it.
8. **Golden package-export test** for a non-folder node with children: `package_export_non_folder_with_children_golden`.
9. **Path rule** for the merged `materialize_path`: adopt the Phase 10 rule (newer of NODE_PATH and embedded blob path, by revision) when Phase 10 lands. *Done with Phase 10* (`mvcc_read/node_path.rs`).

**Files.** `ordering/child_probe.rs`, `queries/lookup.rs`, `queries/listing.rs`, `crud/batch/has_children.rs`, `trait_impl/has_children_ext.rs`, `crud/helpers.rs`, `crud/read/path_materialization.rs`, `transaction/context/nodes/read.rs`, `crud/cascade/single.rs`, the scan executors, `embedding_storage/storage.rs`, `node_to_row/embedding.rs`; remaining: `package_builder.rs`.

**Migration.** None.

**Replication / branch / copy / move.** None; these are read-side changes. The probe reads the same CF that fork, merge and replication already maintain.

**Tests.**
- `has_children_consistency_test` in raisin-rocksdb `tests/all`: false→true→false on first create and last delete, at HEAD and every revision in between; move-out and move-in; copy and cross-branch copy; fork and merge; replication apply; ignores a client-supplied `Some(true)`; root `/`; unchanged after history GC; **dead-hit cap answers true and warns once**.
- A seek-vs-walk unit test over random HLCs, including encodings with `0x00` and `0xFF` bytes **and an unparseable-revision key the seek must advance past**.
- Remaining: items 6–8 above.
- Existing guards: `ordered_children_keyset_test`, `child_listing_keyset_test`, `integration_tests::{node_repository,mvcc_time_travel}`, `editorial_order_tests`.

**Rollback.** Revert the commit. No data is involved.

**Expected gain** (to be confirmed by harness E, A, C and G).
- `get_by_path` drops from about 7 seeks plus an O(children × revisions) scan to about 3 seeks plus an O(1) probe.
- An SQL index-scan row loses one node reload, one BRANCHES read and one child-range scan.
- Child appends through the repository path go from O(N²) to O(N).
- Time-travel reads go from O(newer revisions) to O(1) seeks.

**Risk.** Low. No API-visible change; the probe's dead-hit cap trades an occasional false `true` (warned) for bounded cost.

## Phase 2 — Correctness bugs the research proved, plus their repairs — DONE

**Goal.** Fix verified defects that would otherwise corrupt every number taken afterwards. Each item is small and independently revertible. **Order inside the phase: writer fixes (items 1, 2, 7, 10) first, then the repairs.** Repairing while a funnel still produces the corruption means the repair has to run forever.

**Prerequisites.** Prod-snapshot dry run of every repair (see Operational test gates).

1. **ORDERED_CHILDREN delete tombstone keyed by parent NAME** (`tombstones/core_tombstones.rs:76-89`).
   - Change the tombstoner signature to take an explicit `parent_index_id: Option<&str>`. The replicated delete passes its `parent_id` (`crdt_ops.rs:457-461`) **unconditionally**; today the shared tombstoner is fed `node.parent` (the NAME) whenever the peer's node carries one, so the fix would never reach replicas.
   - Callers with no id resolve it: `/` for root children, otherwise a PATH_INDEX point read of `node.parent_path()`.
   - Tombstone the **stored** label from `get_order_label_for_child`, falling back to `node.order_key`.
   - Fix the "insurance" lookup in `delete/tombstone.rs:68-70` the same way. `cross_branch/prune.rs:153-157` is the reference implementation.
   - **Repair** (streaming, per the discipline above; not an addition to `rebuild_order_indexes`, which loads a workspace into memory):
     - *NODES-tombstone pass.* Iterate **every** NODES tombstone of a child, not only the latest, so a node deleted and re-created more than once gets a tombstone per delete revision. Resolve the parent id and the label from the node version **just before each delete revision** (PATH_INDEX and the ordered entry at that revision), never at HEAD: in a cascade delete the parent is gone, or its path now belongs to another node. Write the tombstone at the child's delete revision on the exact stored `(label, child)` key, which can differ from the deleted node's `order_key` (legacy drift, merge verbatim copies).
     - *ORDERED_CHILDREN pass.* Iterate ORDERED_CHILDREN per parent and verify each `(label, child)` against the child's NODES state at HEAD. For a live entry whose child has no live NODES version ≤ HEAD, tombstone at the child's delete revision if one is known, otherwise at the GC cutoff, and report it. *As built:* the repair has no reliable GC cutoff to read, so a child with no NODES version left is tombstoned at the successor of the entry's own revision (`successor(entry_rev)`) — just after the entry, which hides it at every revision where the child had no readable version anyway — and counted as `without_delete_revision`. A known delete revision older than the entry is likewise replaced by `successor(entry_rev)`, so the tombstone always lands above the entry it masks. This catches entries whose NODES versions `history_gc` with `drop_orphan_tombstones` (`history_gc/mod.rs:226-235`) already removed.
     - Covers root parents, which the existing job skips (`async_indexing/rebuild.rs:843-845`).
     - Runs per branch, including forks taken after the delete that copied the untombstoned entry.
     - Reader-tolerant already: the Phase 1 probe confirms liveness, so the repair is cleanup. Checkpoint: an ingest from an unrepaired peer re-imports stale entries; the repair is data-detected and re-run from the ingest hook.
2. **Merge PATH_INDEX tombstone** `b"\x00"` → `b"T"` (`branches/merge/apply.rs:29`).
   - **The fix is in the readers:** one shared `is_tombstone(value)` that treats both `b"T"` and a one-byte `b"\x00"` as a tombstone, used by **every** PATH_INDEX reader permanently. A checkpoint from an unrepaired or older peer re-imports `\x00` values, and nothing would re-run a one-shot repair.
   - **Repair (cleanup only):** rewrite exact one-byte `\x00` values in PATH_INDEX (and any CF that merge wrote to) as `T` at the same key. Idempotent, streaming.
3. **Bulk descendants at a past revision** (`bulk_descendants.rs:127-130` vs `:163-171`): apply the `max_revision` filter before recording tombstones.
4. **`find_child_id_by_name`** (`ordering/queries.rs:254-288`): add per-(label, child) newest-wins deduplication, the same way the label lookup does.
5. **Replica compound fail-closed.** Until Phase 8 step 3 writes compound and unique entries on replicas, the apply path marks a workspace's compound state `NotBuilt` whenever it applies an upsert to a workspace that has compound indexes.
   - Find the indexes via `list_for_workspace` (the state records), **never** via a NodeType read in the apply path (the deadlock rule).
   - The marker carries a **monotonic generation** (the applying revision). The rebuild CASes `Ready` only if no newer marker exists; otherwise a rebuild's final `Ready` overwrites a `NotBuilt` set by an upsert it never saw.
   - **Consequence, stated plainly:** every replicated upsert after a rebuild flips the replica back to `NotBuilt`, so **replica compound queries are scan-only until Phase 8 step 3**. Measure replica query latency under this item. Preferred alternative: pull Phase 8 step 3 forward and ship it with this phase rather than a permanent replica scan fallback.
   - Merge apply writes no compound entries either, so the same marker applies there until Phase 2.10.
   - Checkpoint: state records arrive from the peer; revalidate after ingest (a peer's `Ready` says nothing about this node's apply history).
   - **Retired by Phase 8 step 3** for every write whose node-type definitions are cached: the apply path writes the compound entries itself and no longer marks. The mark (with its generation) remains for cold definitions, the legacy apply arms and a cold commit-time correction, each of which also requests a local build.
6. **Tenant wipe of INDEX_STATUS** (`storage/tenant_wipe.rs`): reuse `repo_purge`'s `MixedLayout` logic so that `spatial_index\0{t}`, `compound_index\0{t}` and `prop_index\0{t}` records are deleted.
7. **One reference walker.** Replace `tombstones/helpers.rs:25-76`, `async_indexing/helpers.rs:327-365`, `repositories/reference_index.rs:22-60` and the dead `transaction/types.rs:17-50` with `walk_references`. That fixes Composite references never being tombstoned on delete, and Element/Composite references being dropped by REBUILD. Add the Composite arm to the write-side `collect_references` (`transaction/.../create/references.rs:121-169`), with a log-only mode for dangling path-form references in the first release.
8. **Replication `load_latest_node`** (`replication/.../db_lookups.rs:13-56`). **Lands before Phase 7 and before Phase 10.**
   - *Seek:* a workspace-scoped seek of `{t}\0{r}\0{b}\0{ws}\0nodes\0{id}\0` instead of scanning the whole branch NODES CF.
   - *Explicit vs defaulted workspace:* the workspace is always present today because it is defaulted, so a "slow path for callers with no workspace" would never run, and a node whose op carries no workspace (older peers in a mixed-version cluster) would get `old_node = None`, leaving stale path/property/spatial entries untombstoned. Distinguish an explicit workspace from a defaulted one; on a scoped miss, fall back to the branch scan with a `warn!` counter (a miss cannot be told apart from a wrong workspace). Remove the fallback only after a release in which the counter stayed at zero cluster-wide.
   - *Decode:* `load_latest_node` decodes NODES blobs as `Node`; repository-written blobs are `StorageNode`, which has no `path`, so `path` silently becomes `""`. Route it through the shared `deserialize_node_with_path` (materializing NODE_PATH at the baseline's revision). That function becomes the **only** baseline reader for Phase 7, Phase 8 and Phase 12's `sync_node`.
   - *Bounded variant:* add `load_node_before(id, rev)` (newest version strictly below `rev`), the baseline Phase 7 and Phase 12 need.
9. **Copy, promote and deep-create write spatial entries.** `crud/batch/write_batch.rs::add_node_to_batch_with_parent_id` calls the shared spatial writer. Also, `spatial_reconcile.rs:62-67` uses `indexed_geometry_paths` instead of top-level iteration.
10. **Merge apply is a mirrored write funnel; route it through the shared writers** (new; lands before any repair). `write_resolved_node` and `write_resolved_deletion` (`merge/apply.rs:90-230`) write no ORDERED_CHILDREN entries or tombstones, no NODE_PATH tombstone on delete, and no UNIQUE, COMPOUND or translation entries, and do not call `tombstones::tombstone_node`. A merge-created or merge-moved node is missing from CHILD_OF; a merge-deleted node stays listed and keeps `has_children` true.
    - Route merge deletion through `tombstones::tombstone_node`.
    - Route merge upsert and move through the shared ordered-children and NODE_PATH writers.
    - At the merge revision M, a resolution does a **full put of every index, ORDERED_CHILDREN included**, and tombstones the **union** of the values indexed by base, target head and source head. Today's `add_stale_*` calls tombstone only target-old vs new, which leaves source-side values live (an existing bug).
    - **Known gap — translations.** Merge apply writes no TRANSLATION_DATA / TRANSLATION_INDEX entries: the overlays the copy brings over stay as each side left them, with no union tombstone at M. Resolving this needs the revision-correct translation reader and the translation write substrate of Phase 11, and lands there. *(Phase 11: closed — a translation resolution writes its overlay at M, a node resolution carries the kept side's overlays to M with union tombstones.)* COMPOUND entries are likewise not written at M; item 5's `NotBuilt` marker covers them until Phase 8 step 3. *(Phase 8: written at M now, union tombstones included.)*
11. **Compound build lease** (new). The compound build handler takes a cluster-wide lease and skips with "being built elsewhere", so a replica marked `NotBuilt` by item 5 may never rebuild locally. Remove the cluster lease, or scope its key per node (include the node id).
12. **Cross-branch stage leaks removed values** (new, existing bug). `stage_cross_branch_entry` (`stage.rs:114-145,163-185`) is an upsert with `old_dst` but tombstones only compound and unique entries from it; PROPERTY_INDEX and REFERENCE_INDEX values removed on the source stay live on the publish branch. Tombstone them from `old_dst`. (Phase 7 then passes `old_dst` as the baseline.)

**Files.** As listed above, plus a new streaming repair module `management/async_indexing/repair/{cursor,ordered_children,path_tombstone}.rs` with the admin fan-out endpoint and per-node state record, registered in the job registry.

**Migration.** Two idempotent, streaming repair jobs (order tombstones, merge `\x00` cleanup) and a REFERENCE REBUILD for Composite and Element references. All admin-triggered, fanned out per the discipline above, each node running its own build under a per-node (not cluster) lease.

**Replication / branch.** Repairs run per node and per branch. Delete-path fixes reach the replicated delete through the explicit `parent_index_id`. Checkpoint: readers tolerate both corruptions (items 1 and 2), so an ingest from an unrepaired peer cannot make results wrong, only re-dirty the data; the ingest hook re-enqueues the data-detected repairs.

**Tests to add.**
- `has_children_after_last_nested_child_deleted`: false at HEAD, true at the pre-delete revision, and `list_ordered_children_page` empty.
- `replicated_delete_tombstones_by_parent_id`.
- Order repair: `order_repair_cascade_delete`, `order_repair_delete_then_recreate`, `order_repair_after_gc_dropped_nodes_versions`, `order_repair_fork_after_delete`.
- `merge_resolution_vacates_path`: `get_node_id_by_path` returns None after a merge delete or rename resolution.
- `path_reader_treats_nul_byte_as_tombstone`.
- `merge_resolution_keeps_child_listing_consistent` (create, move and delete).
- `merge_keep_ours_source_only_value_not_matched`.
- `bulk_descendants_at_past_revision_includes_later_deleted`.
- `find_child_by_name_ignores_renamed_entry`.
- `replica_compound_query_falls_back_until_rebuilt`, `compound_ready_cas_loses_to_newer_marker`.
- `compound_build_runs_on_replica_while_origin_holds_lease`.
- `tenant_wipe_clears_index_state`.
- `reference_walkers_agree` (writer, delete tombstoner and REBUILD produce the same `(path, ref)` set for Element and Composite input).
- `copied_geometry_is_spatially_indexed`.
- `promote_removed_property_not_matched_on_publish_branch`.
- `replicated_upsert_finds_previous_version_without_branch_scan` (a perf-counter assertion), `replicated_upsert_without_workspace_falls_back_and_counts`, `load_latest_node_materializes_path_for_storage_node_blob`.
- `repair_streams_within_memory_bound` over a large synthetic workspace; `repair_resumes_after_crash` (see Operational test gates).

**Rollback.** Per item. The repair jobs only add tombstones at correct revisions (or rewrite `\x00` to `T`, which readers already treat identically), so they never need undoing.

**Expected gain.** Correctness. Replication apply cost per op goes from O(branch NODES) to O(1) seeks.

**Risk.** Medium (was "low"): item 10 changes the merge write path, and item 5 makes replica compound queries scan-only until Phase 8 step 3. Item 7 can surface previously silent dangling path references in Composite blocks, which is why it starts in log-only mode.

---

## Phase 3 — RESOLVE: correct, unified, and "know before descending" — DONE

This is the core of the user's request. It only touches code; batching is Phase 4.

**Goal.** Make `RESOLVE()` the single resolution engine. It must be RLS-safe, read at a single snapshot revision, deduplicate across all rows of a statement, and decide for each target whether it exists and is readable before it walks into that target.

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-03).**
1. **Dead resolver copies deleted.** `reference_resolver.rs` is split into `reference_resolver/{mod, walk, frontier, fetch, memo, budget}.rs`.
2. **One snapshot.** The resolver takes `at: HLC`, which is `ctx.max_revision` or else HEAD read **once per statement**. Node reads, the translation overlay **and graph RLS** (`filter_node_with_graph(..., &self.snapshot)`, `fetch.rs:113-120`) all use it, so `rls_filter_node_graph`'s `HLC::now` default no longer tears the snapshot. Path references are HEAD-bounded, matching id references.
3. **RLS always enforced on targets** (decision D1; no legacy flag). Every target passes through the RLS filter. A denied, missing or hidden-in-locale target produces identical output: the bare reference. The path-only pre-check (`can_read_in_path` before the blob is read) is **disabled when `auth.uses_graph_rls()`** (`fetch.rs:76`), because a path-only check cannot see RELATES.
4. **Per-statement memo** keyed `(ws, locator, snapshot, locale, fields_sig)`, held on `ExecutionContext`, never shared across statements.
5. **Frontier BFS.** Level N+1 consists only of references found in nodes first fetched at level N, minus anything already in the memo. One final substitution pass with depth tracking.
6. **Translation presence probe.** One iterator pass per node over its overlays; one resolver per statement.
7. **Budget.** 5,000 distinct targets, 50k inlined occurrences, 32 MB per statement; fails loudly.
8. **Embedding hidden from `SELECT *`** (D5): fetched only when named.

**Known limitation (critique).** Historical-translation behaviour under D2 is only trustworthy on the origin. On replicas, overlays do not replicate until Phase 11, and Phase 11's resync must preserve overlay history; until **Phase 11.1** (revision-correct overlay reader), 11.4 (overlay replication) and 11.5 (history-preserving resync) land, a locale-scoped `rev/{n}` RESOLVE can differ between origin and replica. Do not advertise historical translations in RESOLVE before then. *(Phase 11 DONE: all three have landed; below a replica's `translation_history_complete_from` a locale-scoped read fails loudly.)*

**Remaining.** Verify, then fix if confirmed: `RESOLVE(...)->>'k'` fails because `JsonExtractText` is not in the async dispatch (`async_eval.rs:44-74`).

**Files.** `raisin-core/src/services/reference_resolver/*`, `raisin-core/src/services/translation_resolver/mod.rs`, `raisin-core/src/services/rls_filter/mod.rs`, `raisin-sql-execution/src/physical_plan/eval/async_eval.rs`, `executor/context.rs`, `scan_executors/helpers.rs`.

**Migration.** None.

**Replication / branch.** None; this is read-only. On a fork, RESOLVE reads the fork.

**Tests** (in `raisin-sql-execution/tests/all/resolve_json_tests.rs` and `resolve_rls_tests.rs`):
- `resolve_respects_rls_on_referenced_nodes`, `resolve_literal_jsonb_ref_cannot_read_unauthorized_node`.
- `resolve_graph_rls_target_denied` (RELATES rule denies a target; the pre-check must not let the blob through, and the snapshot must be the statement's).
- `resolve_at_revision_inlines_historical_target`.
- `resolve_same_path_ref_in_two_workspaces_does_not_collide`.
- `resolve_cycle_and_shared_dag_output_unchanged` (golden output).
- `resolve_hidden_in_locale_is_bare_ref`.
- `resolve_budget_errors_loudly`.
- `resolve_json_extract_works` (remaining).
- A fetch-count assertion through the counting wrapper: 50 rows sharing 3 targets perform 3 fetches.
- Existing guards: `translation_roundtrip`, `resolve_inlines_nested_and_shared_references`, `resolve_fields_trims_inlined_nodes`.

**Rollback.** Revert. There is deliberately no legacy-semantics flag: RLS on targets was a security hole, not a behaviour preference.

**Expected gain** (harness C). A target shared by N rows is fetched once instead of N times; translation cost per node drops from 1 + |chain| seeks to 1; about 5× fewer storage ops on the chrome query.

**Risk.** Medium, because two behaviour changes are deliberate: RLS is enforced and historical targets are returned.

## Phase 4 — Batched snapshot reads (`get_many_for_read`), shared by RESOLVE and scans — DONE (uncommitted)

**Goal.** Turn per-id awaits into one blocking batch per level or chunk.

**Prerequisites.** **Phase 10 lands before or together with this phase**, because the path rule below depends on Phase 10's backfill; adopting a NODE_PATH-only rule first returns stale paths for put_node-written nodes.

**Changes.**
- New trait method `NodeRepository::get_many_for_read(branch_scope, items: &[(ws, Locator{Id|Path})], snapshot, ReadOpts{properties: Load|Skip|Only(&[..]), has_children})`. The trait default loops `get`, which keeps the deprecated memory backend correct.
- RocksDB implementation in the new file `crud/read/batch_get.rs`. Inside **one `spawn_blocking`**:
  - **one RocksDB snapshot held for the whole statement**, alongside the single HEAD read, not one per chunk. A per-chunk snapshot can see a `versionable=false` in-place overwrite at R ≤ HEAD in one chunk and not the next; the HLC bound does not protect in-place rewrites;
  - sort the items;
  - use one raw iterator each on PATH_INDEX, NODES and NODE_PATH, seeking per item to the newest entry ≤ snapshot;
  - honour tombstones through the shared `is_tombstone` (Phase 2.2);
  - **path rule** (shared with Phase 1's `materialize_path`): take the newer of (NODE_PATH newest ≤ snapshot) and (the embedded blob path at the blob's revision), comparing revisions. NODE_PATH wins only when its revision is ≥ the blob's, which covers ancestor moves. (The earlier "read NODE_PATH at the snapshot, never at the blob's revision" rule returned the stale path for a repo-created node later renamed through put_node, because a full Node blob also parses as StorageNode and NODE_PATH then wins.)
  - decode with a projected decode when RLS needs no property conditions.
- The RESOLVE frontier calls it once per level, across a chunk of rows. `project.rs` and `batch_execution/project/mod.rs` buffer 64 rows when a projection contains RESOLVE or REWRITE_LINKS; the chunk size is 1 under `LIMIT 1` or a point lookup.
- `PropertyIndexScan`, `CompoundScan` and `ReferenceScan` fetch rows in chunks of 256 through the same primitive. One locale clone per row is removed from `property_index_scan.rs:167`, and `node_to_row` moves properties instead of cloning them (`fields.rs:162-167`).

**Files.** `raisin-storage/src/traits/node/mod.rs`, `node_operations.rs`, `raisin-rocksdb/.../trait_impl/mod.rs`, new `crud/read/batch_get.rs`, `storage_node.rs` (projected decode), `physical_plan/project.rs`, `batch_execution/project/mod.rs`, the scan executors, `node_to_row/{mod,fields}.rs`, `executor/context.rs` (statement snapshot).

**Migration.** None.

**Replication / branch.** None; this is read-only.

**Tests to add.**
- `batch_get_honours_tombstones_above_head_and_legacy_blobs`.
- `batch_get_equals_get_for_random_ids_and_revisions`, extended into the 0b oracle.
- `move_then_descendant_of_returns_new_path` (if it fails, fix `scanning/mod.rs:412-413`).
- `repo_create_then_put_node_rename_reads_new_path_at_r2` (shared with Phase 10).
- `statement_snapshot_spans_chunks_with_versionable_false_overwrite`.
- A perf assertion: RESOLVE seeks ≤ 2·distinct + c.

**Rollback.** A config flag `sql.batched_fetch=false` falls back to the per-row path for one release.

**Expected gain.** Iterator and superversion setup is amortized, and tokio workers are no longer blocked on cold reads. More than 2× on harness C/A at N = 50 rows on top of Phase 3 (to be measured).

**Risk.** Medium (a new storage primitive, and a statement-long snapshot pins memtables/SSTs for long statements; the RESOLVE budget bounds that). The mitigation is the equivalence property test.

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-03).**
- **Trait** (`raisin-storage/src/traits/node/batch_read.rs`): `NodeRepository::get_many_for_read(BranchScope, &[BatchReadItem{workspace, locator: NodeLocator::{Id|Path}}], at, Option<&ReadSnapshot>, ReadOpts{properties: Load|Skip, has_children})` and `open_read_snapshot() -> Option<ReadSnapshot>` (an opaque, clonable, backend-owned view). Item `i` answers `items[i]` exactly as `get`/`get_by_path` at `at`; `has_children` only when asked (`None` otherwise — neither the SQL row nor RESOLVE's inlined JSON carries it). The default body is `get_many_by_loop` (one `get`/`get_by_path` per item, in order), which keeps the deprecated memory backend correct and IS the `sql.batched_fetch = false` path — one reference behaviour, not two.
- **RocksDB** (`crud/read/batch_get.rs`, `crud/read/read_snapshot.rs`): one `spawn_blocking` per call; items sorted; path items resolve through PATH_INDEX (newest ≤ `at`, the shared `is_tombstone`, so merge's legacy `\x00` too); each distinct `(workspace, id)` read once; one raw, total-order, snapshot-pinned iterator per CF (`mvcc_read::SnapshotRead`) reused for every item. Decoding is THE decoder with THE path rule (`mvcc_read::deserialize_node_with_path_in` → `materialize_path_in`, the Phase 10 rule including its PATH_INDEX tie-break), generic over `VersionedRead` so the single-read path (`DbRead`) and the batch path are one body. `RocksReadSnapshot` owns its `Arc<DB>` (snapshot dropped first) and counts the seeks issued through it (`raisin_rocksdb::read_snapshot_seeks`, `#[doc(hidden)]`, for the perf assertion — batched reads run on blocking threads where a thread-local `PerfContext` cannot see them).
- **One snapshot per statement.** `StatementState` holds the statement's `ReadSnapshot` (a `OnceLock`), opened LAZILY at the statement's first batched read (`ExecutionContext::statement_read_snapshot`) — after `statement_snapshot()` fixed the revision, so the view contains everything at or below it and the HLC bound filters anything newer. (The first cut pinned it eagerly right after the HEAD read in both query paths; review: that took the DB mutex and held a snapshot — blocking compaction of superseded versions for as long as the result stream lived — for every table scan, point lookup and EXPLAIN that never reads in batches.) Every chunk and every RESOLVE level of the statement reads through it.
- **The view decides CONTENT, the live database decides RECENCY** (review fix). The statement's other readers (table/prefix/tree pages, the index reads that name candidates) read live, later than the pin. A commit at or below the statement's revision can land after the pin — an out-of-order local commit (A takes r1, B commits r2 = HEAD, A commits; HEAD's monotonic guard keeps r2, A's data lands at r1) or a replicated peer's ops below the local HEAD — and the live readers return its rows while a view-only batched read did not: RESOLVE left a bare reference next to the row naming the target, and a `CompoundIndexScan`/`ReferenceIndexScan` candidate decoded to the older version that no longer matched (neither re-checks). So when the database moved since the pin (`RocksReadSnapshot::moved_since_pin`, a sequence-number compare — free when nothing was written), each item is also probed LIVE, keys only (`mvcc_read::node_record_revision_in`: newest NODES/NODE_PATH revision ≤ R; the PATH_INDEX entry for a path item), and an item whose live record is NEWER is read live. A `versionable=false` in-place rewrite keeps its keys and revision, so the view still answers for it — the guarantee the view exists for. Cost when the DB moved: two extra seeks per node (one per path item), counted in the view's seek total.
- **One reader per concern** (review fix). The batched reader had its own copies of `resolve_path_index` (PATH_INDEX seek + tombstone test) and of `get_at_revision_impl_as`'s seek-and-decode. Both are now ONE body over `VersionedRead` (`mvcc_read/point.rs`: `path_index_entry_in`, `node_version_in`), called with `DbRead` by `get_by_path`/`get_node_id_by_path`/`get(.., Some(rev))` and with the batch's `SnapshotRead` by `get_many_for_read`; the copies (`resolve_path_in`, `read_one_in`) and the now-unused `get_versioned_at_or_before` are gone.
- **RESOLVE** (`raisin-core/.../reference_resolver/{fetch,frontier,mod}.rs`): `read_targets` reads a whole frontier level with ONE `get_many_for_read` through the statement's snapshot (path-only RLS pre-check first, per target; translate + RLS after, per target, unchanged). `resolve_json_many` runs ONE frontier walk for several documents (a BFS from many sources reaches the union of each source's ball), then inlines each document on its own. SQL (`physical_plan/project_resolve.rs`, `eval/resolve_eval.rs::eval_resolve_rows`): a projection expression that IS a `RESOLVE(...)` call is evaluated a chunk of rows at a time, grouped by `(locale, depth, fields)`; the chunk grows 1, 2, 4, … 64 rows (see deviations). The columnar `batch_execution/project` does the same in chunks of 64.
- **Scans** (`scan_executors/batch_fetch.rs`): `PropertyIndexScan`, `CompoundIndexScan` and `ReferenceIndexScan` read candidates in chunks of 256 through `fetch_nodes` (the rows still owed under a LIMIT, at least 1, so `LIMIT 1` reads one; a chunk that came up short is followed by one at least twice its size — review fix, so `LIMIT 1` behind 10,000 RLS-denied candidates is ~log₂ batched reads, not 10,000 blocking hops), at the statement's revision through the statement's snapshot. `CompoundIndexScan` and `ReferenceIndexScan` now read at `statement_snapshot()` (they read at `ctx.max_revision`, `None` = unbounded, before). The per-row locale clone is gone (`per_locale`: the last locale takes the node), and `node_to_row_owned` MOVES the property map into the `properties` column (`node_to_row/{mod,fields}.rs`, one `convert` body behind both entry points).
- **Rollback flag `sql.batched_fetch`**: `QueryEngine::with_batched_fetch(bool)`, defaulting from `RAISIN_SQL_BATCHED_FETCH` (on unless `0`/`false`/`off`/`no`), carried on `ExecutionContext::batched_fetch` and `ReferenceResolver::with_batched_fetch`. Every SQL `ExecutionContext` is built by `QueryEngine::new_statement_context`, which sets it (review fix: the scalar no-FROM path, `SELECT RESOLVE('{..}'::jsonb)`, built its own and kept the env default). Off: scans call `get_many_by_loop`, RESOLVE reads one target at a time, the projection evaluates RESOLVE per row, and no statement snapshot is pinned. **Rollout:** this is a rollback switch, not a two-release rollout — the batched path is ON by default in the release that ships it; set `RAISIN_SQL_BATCHED_FETCH=0` on a node to return it to the per-row path for one release, after which the flag is removed.

**Tests (all green).**
- raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/batch_get_test.rs`: `batch_get_honours_tombstones_above_head_and_legacy_blobs` (delete tombstone; a tombstone and a resurrecting blob + PATH_INDEX entry stranded ABOVE HEAD — invisible at HEAD, visible when read at that revision; merge's legacy `\x00` PATH_INDEX marker at HEAD; a legacy full-`Node` blob; a missing id, a missing path, a duplicate, an unknown workspace), `repo_create_then_put_node_rename_reads_new_path_at_r2` (the Phase 10 witness read in batches at r1..r4, by id and by path), `batch_get_snapshot_hides_in_place_overwrite_between_chunks` (a real `versionable=false` NodeType: the pinned view keeps the old record across "chunks", a fresh view at the same revision bound reads the rewrite). Every comparison runs `assert_equals_get`: batched == `get_many_by_loop` with and without the statement snapshot, `Load` and `Skip`, with and without `has_children`.
- raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/batch_get_equivalence_test.rs`: `batch_get_equals_get_for_random_ids_and_revisions` — seeded random histories (repository create, transaction create/rename/update, legacy-format renames, delete, `move_node_tree` of subtrees), every recorded revision × every id and path ever used (shuffled, duplicates, missing), plus the final paths against the history's own map. `BATCH_GET_SEEDS` (default 4; 40 run green).
- **Extended into the 0b oracle:** template `batch_get_equals_get` (`mvcc_index_oracle/checks/batch_get.rs`) asks the same equivalence at every snapshot of every stage (histories, GC, replication replay) for every model id, live path and previously-used path. Compared against `get`, not the model, so a known `get` defect on the expected list cannot resurface under a second name.
- raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/batch_get_resolve_test.rs` — **the perf assertion**: `resolve_seeks_bounded_by_two_per_distinct_target` (15 distinct targets over two levels, a repeated reference: seeks ≤ 2·distinct + 2, and the unbatched resolver produces the identical document); `resolve_chunk_of_rows_reads_each_target_once` (50 documents, each with its own target plus three shared: 53 reads, ≤ 2·53 + 2 seeks, each document equal to the row-by-row unbatched answer).
- raisin-sql-execution `tests/all/batched_fetch_tests.rs`: `statement_snapshot_spans_chunks_with_versionable_false_overwrite` (300 `versionable=false` rows through a `PropertyIndexScan` — asserted from EXPLAIN; after the first row is pulled — the first 256-node chunk read — a row of the SECOND chunk is rewritten in place; the running statement still reads `old`, and the same procedure under `with_batched_fetch(false)` reads `new`, which proves the window is real), `move_then_descendant_of_returns_new_path` (repository and transaction subtree moves; `DESCENDANT_OF` of the new parent lists the moved paths, the old parent lists none at HEAD and the old paths at the pre-move `__revision` — it passed without touching `scanning/mod.rs`), `batched_fetch_off_returns_the_same_rows` (property equality with and without `LIMIT 1`, `REFERENCES` with and without `LIMIT`, the compound `CHILD_OF … ORDER BY created_at LIMIT`, `RESOLVE` at depth 1 and 2 — each scan kind asserted from EXPLAIN — identical rows with the flag on and off).
- Unit: `scan_executors::batch_fetch::tests` (chunk sizing, `per_locale`), `project_resolve::tests::chunks_grow_from_one_row`.
- Review fixes: raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/batch_get_out_of_order_test.rs` — `batch_get_reads_out_of_order_commit_landed_after_pin` (A takes r1, B commits r2 = HEAD, the view is pinned, A commits below HEAD: a target A created resolves by id and path, and `p` decodes to A's version, == one `get` per item) and `resolve_inlines_target_committed_out_of_order_after_pin` (the reviewer's scenario: the live listing returns `p → t`, RESOLVE through the pinned view inlines `t`); both FAIL with the live probe disabled. raisin-sql-execution: `batched_fetch_tests::property_named_properties_does_not_replace_the_map_column`, `batch_fetch::tests::limit_one_with_rejected_candidates_grows_chunks`, `engine::statement_context_tests::scalar_query_context_honours_batched_fetch_off`. Re-run: raisin-rocksdb `--test all` 501 passed / 13 ignored, `--lib mvcc_read` 11; raisin-sql-execution `--lib` 935, `--test all` 237 passed with only the 8 known failures (oracle's `batch_get_equals_get` green).
- Regression runs: raisin-rocksdb `--test all` (499 passed, 13 ignored) and `--lib mvcc_read`; raisin-sql-execution `--lib` (933 passed) and `--test all` (236 passed, only the 8 known failures; the MVCC oracle green with the new template); raisin-core `--lib reference_resolver`.

**Measured (harness C, `index_read_bench_resolve.rs`, debug build — relative numbers only; median of 50; two alternating runs each, stable to ±1 %).** "before" = the same binary with `RAISIN_SQL_BATCHED_FETCH=0` (the per-row path), "after" = default.

| scenario | before | after | change |
|---|---|---|---|
| C — chrome, one row (25 refs, 23 distinct, depth 2) | 1.48 ms | 1.38 ms | −7 % |
| C — chrome, 50 rows sharing it (depth 3) | 18.0 ms | 16.2 ms | −10 % |
| C3 — 50-row listing, 100 distinct targets (depth 1; new) | 6.97 ms | 6.00 ms | −14 % |

The plan's "> 2× on harness C at N = 50" did NOT materialize in this harness. Everything sits in memtables and the block cache, so the per-key iterator/superversion setup that batching amortizes is cheap here, and the remaining cost is RESOLVE's own work (JSON conversion, inlining 50 copies of a 25-reference chrome at depth 3, the per-target translate/RLS step). The gain should grow with cold reads (SST-resident blobs, where the old path also blocked a tokio worker per target) and in a release build; both are still owed: the same bench on a copy of the real database, in a release build (operational gate).

**Deviations.**
- `ReadOpts.properties` is `Load | Skip`, not `Only(&[..])`; nothing calls `Skip` yet. The plan's "projected decode when RLS needs no property conditions" is NOT wired into the scans: a scan cannot see whether a filter or projection above it reads `properties`, so it always loads. The primitive (`StorageNodeHead`) is in place for when the planner can say so.
- The RESOLVE chunk GROWS (1, 2, 4, … 64) instead of being "64, or 1 under `LIMIT 1` or a point lookup": the projection cannot see a `LIMIT` above it, and growing from one row keeps a point query at one row and any limited query at most ~2× the rows it consumes. Only a projection expression that IS a RESOLVE call is chunked; one nested in another expression (`RESOLVE(...)->>'k'`) is still evaluated per row (its frontier is still one batch per level).
- Only batched reads go through the statement snapshot. Readers that are not batched (table scans, prefix/`CHILD_OF`/`DESCENDANT_OF` traversals, point lookups, the property/compound/reference INDEX reads that produce the candidate ids) still read the live database at the statement's revision. The two views can disagree only about a commit at or below the revision that landed after the pin, and the batched reader resolves that toward the live side (the newer record wins, see above), so the batched reads never show an OLDER world than the rows that feed them. Passing the `ReadSnapshot` to every reader (the "full fix") remains open. The `has_children` probe inside a batched read (only when `ReadOpts::has_children`, which no caller sets) also reads live.
- A property literally named `properties` no longer overwrites the `properties` column under `SELECT *` (review fix): the borrowed `node_to_row` let it win and the owned `node_to_row_owned` did not, so the answer depended on the scan the planner picked.
