The column is the map in both, as it already was with a projection.
- **Open (review, LOW, deferred):** (1) the chunked RESOLVE projection's read-ahead (1, 2, 4, … rows past what a `LIMIT` above consumes) is charged against the statement-wide RESOLVE budget — a query that fit with per-row evaluation can fail with "budget exceeded". The frontier admits targets while it reads and each document is charged as it is inlined; charging per YIELDED row means splitting `resolve_json_many` into an uncharged walk and a per-row charged inline, or pushing the LIMIT into `project_chunked`. (2) `SnapshotRead`'s iterators have no `iterate_upper_bound`, so a missing item followed by a long run of delete markers walks the run; a per-workspace bound helps little (GC'd runs sit in the same workspace), and a per-item bound is a fresh iterator, i.e. the per-key cost batching removes.
- `sql.batched_fetch` is a `QueryEngine` builder plus an env var, not a server config-file key (no `raisin-server` plumbing), like Phase 10's gate was.
- The bench's "before" is the per-row fallback in the same binary, not the pre-Phase-4 commit (no checkout allowed). The fallback still benefits from the scan-side changes that are not behind the flag (no locale clone, properties moved), so it slightly understates the gain.

---

## Phase 5 — Tree readers: know before recursing — DONE

**Goal.** Deep readers stop scanning ORDERED_CHILDREN for nodes the bulk set already shows to be leaves.

**Changes.**
- `deep/nested.rs` and `deep/array.rs` build `children_by_parent` once from `get_descendants_bulk_impl`.
  - A node with 0 children is a leaf: `has_children = false` and no scan.
  - A node with 1 child needs no ordering scan.
  - A node with 2 or more children gets an editorial-order scan (never sort by `order_key`, because of legacy drift).
- Fix `array.rs`'s double scan (`:99` and `:114`).
- `flat.rs` prunes ids with no descendants.
- Apply the same treatment to `count_scan`'s per-node child traversal and `NodeService::list_all`.
- At the `max_depth` boundary, use the Phase 1 probe.

**Files.** `repositories/nodes/queries/deep/{nested,array,flat}.rs`, `count_scan.rs`, `node_service` list_all.

**Migration / replication.** None.

**Tests to add.** Deep nested and array output at HEAD and at a past revision is unchanged (golden tests); `subtree_document_order_test` and `editorial_order_tests` must still pass, so DESCENDANT_OF stays pre-order.

**Rollback.** Revert.

**Expected gain** (harness F). Eliminates one iterator per leaf, which is the majority of nodes in content trees.

**Risk.** Low.

---

## Phase 6 — One revision-bounded PROPERTY_INDEX reader (correctness + speed) — DONE

**Goal.** Replace the five reader loops in `property_index/query.rs`, `scan.rs` and `listing.rs` with one primitive.

**Prerequisites.** Prod-snapshot dry run of the orphan detector (item below) before readers switch.

**The primitive.**
- Seek to `value_prefix ++ encode_desc(at)`, where `at` is `ctx.max_revision`, or else the branch HEAD (which also excludes entries stranded above HEAD).
- Decide per `(value, node_id)`: the first entry seen is the newest ≤ `at`. A live entry is a match; `T` is not.
- Stop early under LIMIT.
- Remove the node-wide cross-value tombstone set.

**Keeping orphans masked (critique).** Removing the cross-value tombstone set exposes orphan live entries left by historically buggy writers (the replica writer missing membership entries, merge apply, the old in-place writer). Custom JSON properties stay masked by the residual filter, but pseudo-properties (`node_type`, `__name`, `created_at`/`updated_at`) are exempt from it, so their orphans would become phantom rows at HEAD and in COUNT. Therefore:
- **Residual pseudo-property re-check stays** on the new reader: each candidate row's pseudo-property is re-checked against the decoded node until the per-workspace PROPERTY_INDEX state record says a rebuild or verify has reported clean. Only then may the reader drop the re-check for that workspace.
- **Report-only orphan detector** (`management/async_indexing/property_orphan_report.rs`): compares the HEAD index against node blobs for pseudo-properties and reports counts per workspace. Run it on a prod snapshot before switching readers. It writes nothing.

**Bugs this fixes** (each gets a failing-first test):
- (a) `ORDER BY updated_at ASC` drops updated nodes.
- (b) `created_at`/`updated_at` range scans. Use one encoder for bound and key (8-byte big-endian micros, `index_keys.rs:86`). Parse the value by its fixed width instead of stopping at `0x00`. Shadow older live rows.
- (c) `list_by_type` returns nodes whose type changed; re-check `node_type`.
- (d) A `__revision = N` query with a property or pseudo-property equality misses or invents rows.
- `COUNT` pushdown (`count_scan.rs:150`) uses the same primitive and the same residual re-check.

**Files.** `raisin-storage` `PropertyIndexRepository` trait (gains `at: &HLC`; memory backend stubbed), `raisin-rocksdb/.../property_index/{query,scan,helpers}.rs`, `queries/listing.rs`, executors `property_index_scan.rs`, `property_range_scan.rs`, `property_order_scan/index_order.rs`, `count_scan.rs`, planner `scan_planning/build_scan.rs` (bound encoding at `:696-704`; keep the custom JSON residual), new `property_orphan_report.rs`.

**Migration.** None. The key format is unchanged, and existing bloated data reads correctly. Orphans are masked by the residual filter (custom JSON) and the residual pseudo-property re-check.

**Replication / branch.** None. Fork-copied history reads the same way. Checkpoint: the per-workspace "clean" state record is revalidated after ingest, since ingest can import a peer's orphans.

**Tests to add.**
- `order_by_updated_at_asc_includes_updated_nodes`.
- `updated_at_range_lower_and_upper_bounds`.
- `list_by_type_after_type_change`.
- `property_eq_at_historical_revision`, `name_eq_at_historical_revision`, `count_at_historical_revision`.
- `pseudo_property_orphan_not_returned_at_head_or_counted`.
- `orphan_report_counts_pseudo_property_orphans`.
- Remove the matching templates from the 0b expected-failure list.
- Existing guards: `dml_index_and_predicate_maintenance`, `limit_pushdown_tests`, `pagination_navigation_tests`, `references_compose_tests`.

**Rollback.** Revert. No data is involved.

**Expected gain** (harness A/B). Lookup of an unchanged value on a node edited N times goes from O(N) to O(1) per node. Early exit under LIMIT. Four correctness bugs closed. The pseudo-property re-check costs one decode per candidate until a workspace is certified clean.

**Risk.** Medium, because the trait signature change touches six executors. It is guarded by the oracle and the residual re-check.

## Phase 7 — One delta writer: skip unchanged index writes (the PostgreSQL HOT idea) — DONE (uncommitted)

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-04).**
- **The one writer** — `crates/raisin-rocksdb/src/indexing/property_delta/` (`entries.rs`, `mod.rs`, `baseline.rs`, `in_place.rs`). `entries_of(node)` is the ONE derivation of a version's PROPERTY_INDEX entries (custom properties hashed, `__node_type`, `__name`, `__archetype`, `__created_by`, `__updated_by`, `__created_at` / `__updated_at` in micros, one entry per IS_A / HAS_MIXIN member); diffs are taken over these `(tag, name, value)` keys, never over `PropertyValue`s (two values that hash alike count as unchanged). `write_property_index_delta(batch, target, ctx, baseline, new, rev, in_place)` tombstones `old − new`, then puts `new − old` under `Baseline::Predecessor`, or every entry under `Full` / `OutOfOrder` / `NoPrior`. It replaces the transaction writer (`create/indexing.rs::index_node_properties` + `tombstone_stale_property_indexes`), the repository writers (`crud/indexing/property_indexes.rs`, file deleted) and the replication writer (`index_writers.rs::write_property_indexes`, deleted); `tombstone_superseded_entries` (out-of-order apply, merge union tombstones) and `tombstone_all_entries` (the delete tombstoner, `tombstones/index_tombstones.rs`) are the same derivation. The replica therefore now writes IS_A / HAS_MIXIN membership.
- **Baselines** (`property_delta/baseline.rs::resolve_baseline`, called through `NodeRepositoryImpl::delta_baseline`). Through the one baseline reader, now a shared function `mvcc_read/baseline.rs` (`node_version_at_or_before`, `node_version_before`, `node_versions_above`; the applicator's `load_node_before` / `load_node_replaced_by` delegate to it): newest stored version below `rev` and live → `Predecessor`; none, or a tombstone → `NoPrior`; a version AT `rev` → `Full(newest strictly below)`; versions ABOVE `rev` → `OutOfOrder { prior, successors }`. Without the skip gate the answer is `Full(caller's prior)` and nothing is read. `OutOfOrder` (added beyond the plan text, see deviations) is a full put that then ends this version's values at the first successor's revision and re-puts every successor's entries at its own revision — a successor written with skips keeps unchanged entries BELOW `rev`, and a tombstone at `rev` would otherwise mask them.
- **Funnel matrix.** `put_node`: `NoPrior` on create; `Full(existing)` when the write is in place (`versionable=false`) or the transaction already wrote or moved the node (`ReadCache.nodes` / `moved_nodes`: its entries at R are in this very batch); otherwise `delta_baseline`. `add_node`, copy (`copy/tree.rs`), deep create, backup import: `NoPrior` (`PropertyWrite::CREATE`). `update_impl`: in place → `Full(old)`, else `delta_baseline`. Re-stamps (repository move, reorder, rebalance via `rewrite_node_record_to_batch`; the transactional `move_node_tree`): `Full(old)`. Replication apply (`crdt_ops.rs` → `write_all_node_indexes`): `Full(replaced)` unconditionally, in place when a version is stored at its revision. Merge resolution (`merge/apply.rs`): union tombstones, then `Full(None)` — never skips. Cross-branch stage (`stage.rs`): `delta_baseline(target, old_dst)` — an upsert against `old_dst`. Restore: SQL `RESTORE` → `NodeService::update_node` → `put_node`.
- **UNIQUE** (`crud/indexing/unique_delta.rs::write_unique_delta`, sync; `add_unique_delta_to_batch` / the transaction's `write_unique_properties` resolve both sides' unique names first): tombstone claims `(node_type, property, hash)` that changed, and re-put EVERY claim the new version holds. **Claims are never skipped** (review fix): nothing rebuilds or verifies UNIQUE_INDEX, the replica apply path writes no claims, and a schema change adding `unique: true` makes a missing claim look "unchanged" — a skipped claim was never written and a duplicate value was accepted (`unique_claim_reput_after_replicated_create`). Used by `put_node`, `add_node`, `update_impl`, stage.
- **REFERENCE** (`reference_indexes.rs::add_reference_index_entries`): every reference is re-put at every version's revision; removed references are tombstoned as before. **Not skipped** (review fix): a reference kept below the revision by a skip is masked by the stale-reference tombstone of any write committed below that version (an out-of-order commit, a replicated op older than a local version), and nothing re-asserted it (`out_of_order_write_keeps_successor_reference_and_unique_claim`). The `_skipping` variant is gone.
- **ORDERED_CHILDREN.** Item 6: `ordering/label_lookup.rs::last_live_order_label` — newest entry per `(label, child)` decides, tombstones honoured, the greatest LIVE label in editorial order — is the one fallback behind `get_last_order_label` AND the replication applicator's `scan_last_order_label_for_parent` (which took the highest-revision label, counted tombstoned labels and split keys on `\0`). Item 7: `current_order_label(…, hint = prior.order_key)` verifies the hint by one `(parent, label)` prefix seek filtered by child id (`live_entry_under_label`), falling back to the sibling scan. The entry is re-put at every revision even when parent, label and name are unchanged (**not skipped**, review fix — same masking argument as REFERENCE; one entry per write). The replica's "still in the same place at the newer revision" check reads the pair's newest entry AT OR BELOW the newer revision (`live_entry_under_label(…, at_or_below)`), not the exact key. The stage fallback that called `inc()` on a FULL last label now mints through `next_append_label`.
- **So PROPERTY_INDEX is the only index that skips.** That is where the saving is (harness H), and the only one with a rebuild and a verify certifying it.
- **`versionable=false` (item 8).** In-place writes pass `InPlace::Reused`: a PROPERTY_INDEX write may land at `max(R, newest entry of its (entry, node) group above R)`. The group lookup walks the value's entries of ALL nodes newer than R, so (review fix) it is **opt-in with `index.skip_unchanged`** (flag off: every in-place write lands at R, exactly as before Phase 7, zero reads), **capped** at `in_place::MAX_KEYS_PER_GROUP` = 256 keys per group (then R, counted in `in_place_scans_capped()`), and **resolved before** the transaction's batch mutex is taken (`InPlaceTargets::resolve` in `put_node` / `update_impl`). The replica apply path lands in-place writes at R. UNIQUE claims land on the claim's newest entry when it is above R and this node's own (one seek). Merge conflict detection does not see in-place writes (no revision is minted, `RevisionMeta.changed_nodes` never lists them), and both branches' in-place writes land on the same R0 keys — documented by `versionable_false_with_merge`.
- **The skip gate: `index.skip_unchanged`, default OFF** — *default ON since Phase 7b, which also makes the rebuild below queue itself per branch* (`repositories/nodes/index_writes.rs`). `RocksDBConfig::index_skip_unchanged` (server TOML `[storage] index_skip_unchanged = true`), or `RAISIN_INDEX_SKIP_UNCHANGED=1`, or `NodeRepositoryImpl::set_index_skip_unchanged` at runtime. Even with the flag on, a write skips only on a branch whose `property_index` rebuild state record is `done` UNDER THIS NODE'S ID (`property_index_rebuilt`); every other branch keeps full puts — the "built by the pre-Phase-7 writer" rule. `skipped_unchanged_entries()` counts skipped entries since process start (the operational signal).
- **The rebuild** (`repair/property_index.rs`, `RepairKind::PropertyIndex`, slug `property_index`): streams the branch's NODES, takes each node's newest version and puts every entry it indexes AT THAT VERSION'S REVISION (never at HEAD — an entry above the version's revision is one a later in-place write cannot mask). Data-detected (an exact key already live is not rewritten), bounded batches with the per-node state record and resumable cursor, disk precheck, rate limit, dry run, admin endpoint with peer fan-out (`POST /api/management/{repo}/repairs/property_index` — the existing handler takes any slug). Completing it is what flips the branch's gate.
- **The verify job** (`RepairKind::PropertyIndexVerify`, slug `property_index_verify`; `RepairOptions::sample_every`, default 16): samples one live node in N and checks every entry of its newest version is live AS OF that version — a point read at each retained version revision, then an MVCC walk of the entry's `(value, node)` group at or below the newest revision (`property_delta::entry_state_as_of`, review fix): an entry kept by skips lives at the revision of the version that first wrote it, which history GC may have deleted, and point reads then reported a false miss after every GC (`verify_after_history_gc_finds_no_hole`). A group too crowded to walk within `PROBE_MAX_KEYS` (4096) counts as `inconclusive`, never as missing. It writes no index data; a miss resets the branch's rebuild state to `stale` (writes there fall back to full puts at once) and `run_repair` queues the `property_index` rebuild on this node (`repair/property_state.rs`). Fanned out like every repair. The rebuild uses the same probe to skip entries already live as of the version (no branch-wide re-put of skip-kept entries); a tombstone at the exact version revision is overwritten (with one canonical derivation it is a hole, not a decision).
- **Canonical empty vector** (review fix): `hash_property_value` hashes an EMPTY `Vector` as `[]`, like an empty `Array`. `PropertyValue` is untagged, so a stored `[]` (every node's `$mixins`, any empty list) decodes as `Vector([])`; hashed `vector:0d`, the writer's in-memory form and the decoded form a baseline, rebuild or verify derives disagreed — every update tombstoned a key nothing wrote, and the verify reported that entry missing on every node written since the rebuild, flipping the gate back to `stale` and queueing a branch-wide rebuild on every run (`verify_after_update_with_stamped_mixins_finds_no_hole`). PROPERTY_INDEX is local and derived, so no peer depends on the old key; live `vector:0d` entries already on disk become unreferenced orphans (nothing queries them).
- **Invalidation is unconditional and cannot be lost** (review fix). `invalidate_property_index_rebuild` sets the record to `stale` with no cursor WHATEVER its status (a `running` or crashed-resumable rebuild used to make it a no-op, then resume past the changed nodes and commit `done`), and replaces the branch's invalidation epoch (`repair_epoch` record beside the state record). A rebuild stores the epoch it started under in its state (`RepairState::epoch`, kept across a resume) and commits `done` only if it is still current — checked and written under one gate lock (`property_state::commit_rebuild`), else `stale` (`invalidation_during_rebuild_is_not_lost`).
- **Checkpoint ingest** invalidates every branch's rebuild state of every repository on this node BEFORE the copy starts (local writes keep running during the many-batch copy) and again AFTER it (INDEX_STATUS came with the copy, possibly carrying this node's own old `done` back from a peer); both are propagated as ingest errors, not logged (`invalidate_rebuilds_for_ingest`). The repair re-enqueue after the copy stays. The rebuild is NOT queued automatically (D11, admin-triggered).
- **Merge** (review fix): `copy_branch_indexes` (merge replay and fork) invalidates the target's rebuild for every node id holding a record there unless the same node id also rebuilt the SOURCE (`invalidate_after_branch_copy`; `merge_from_unrebuilt_source_invalidates_target`) — the replayed entries of an unrebuilt source may carry the very holes the rebuild fills. While a merge into a branch runs (from its start until the replay is done), skip-unchanged is off on that branch (`nodes::merge_window`, process-local, keyed by DB instance), so a write in the window is a full put that the replayed tombstones below it cannot mask. Not covered: a write staged before the window opened and committed inside it (narrow; the commit-time re-check below catches it when the stored versions changed).
- **Branch create / delete** (review fix): deleting a branch (locally or by replication) drops every repair state and epoch record under its name, and creating a branch whose record does not exist does too (`forget_branch_rebuild_state`), so a branch re-created under a deleted one's name never inherits `done` (`recreated_branch_does_not_inherit_rebuild_state`).

**Rollout order (the flag is the "later step").** *Superseded by Phase 7b (owner decision, 2026-10-04): the flag is on by default and step (2) runs by itself; the text below is the Phase 7 record.* (1) Ship this binary with `index.skip_unchanged` off: the one writer, the full-put replica writer with membership, the new label readers, the rebuild and the verify job are live; behaviour is the full put it always was. (2) Run `property_index` on every node through the fan-out endpoint (and `property_index_verify` to check). (3) Set `index_skip_unchanged = true` on every node. Rolling the flag back off is safe: the full writer produces a superset, and the out-of-order protection does not depend on the flag (below). A fork starts with no rebuild record and does full puts until rebuilt; a checkpoint ingest puts the repository back to full puts. No step of the review fixes needs a release split: every index they touch is local derived state, and the commit-time re-check, the in-place group lookup and the merge window are all inert while the flag is off.

**Tests (all green).**
- raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/delta_writer/` (every test with the flag on and the branch rebuilt unless stated): `property_tests::{unchanged_update_writes_no_entries_for_unchanged_props, time_travel_between_two_writes_with_skip, unique_violation_still_detected_after_unchanged_update, reference_removed_is_tombstoned_unchanged_is_reput, write_below_a_stored_successor_keeps_the_successors_skipped_entries}`; review regressions `review_replica_tests::{local_skip_write_then_older_replicated_op_keeps_head_entries, unique_claim_reput_after_replicated_create}`, `review_origin_tests::{out_of_order_write_after_rollback_keeps_successor_entries, out_of_order_write_keeps_successor_reference_and_unique_claim, concurrent_updates_proving_the_same_predecessor_keep_unchanged_values, out_of_revision_order_commits_keep_head_entries}`, `review_state_tests::{verify_after_history_gc_finds_no_hole, verify_after_update_with_stamped_mixins_finds_no_hole, invalidation_during_rebuild_is_not_lost, merge_from_unrebuilt_source_invalidates_target, recreated_branch_does_not_inherit_rebuild_state}`, unit `indexing::property_delta::tests::in_place_group_walk_is_capped`; `replica_tests::{is_a_and_has_mixin_on_replica (flag off and on), replicated_op_older_than_head_does_not_leave_live_entries}`; `merge_tests::{merge_keep_ours_after_skip_unchanged_property_still_matches, merge_keep_ours_preserves_target_order_label, merge_keep_ours_source_only_value_not_matched, promote_removed_property_not_matched_on_publish_branch, versionable_false_update_with_skip, versionable_false_with_merge}`; `ordering_tests::{reorder_and_rename_still_rewrite_ordered_entry, last_order_label_ignores_deleted_child_and_returns_max_live}`; `repair_tests::{verify_finds_a_hole_and_the_rebuild_fills_it, skip_needs_this_nodes_rebuild, property_index_rebuild_resumes_after_crash}`. Unit: `indexing::property_delta::tests` (entries include membership, predecessor writes only what changed, in-place tombstone lands on the newest group entry above R), `mvcc_read::baseline::tests::predecessor_is_strictly_below`.
- raisin-sql-execution `tests/all/delta_restore_tests.rs`: `gc_then_restore_through_sql_restore`, `gc_then_restore_through_restore_version_write` — six revisions with skips, `run_history_gc` keep 3, restore to a retained revision, the index answers at HEAD equal those at the restored revision.
- MVCC oracle under `RAISIN_INDEX_SKIP_UNCHANGED=1` (`Env::unlock_skip_unchanged` rebuilds `main` at bootstrap and every fork, and asserts the gate opened; `ORACLE_VERBOSE` prints `skipped_unchanged_entries`, >0 with the flag, 0 without): all stages green at `PROPTEST_CASES=64` (histories, history GC, replay), stage 3 with every case permuted through the causal buffer (`ORACLE_DELIVERY=permuted`, 48 cases), the unbuffered out-of-order witnesses, and a 64-history replay sweep with no finding. Replicas keep full puts, as above.
- Existing guards, green (re-run after the review fixes: `--lib` 1287, `--test all` 530 with the flag off AND with `RAISIN_INDEX_SKIP_UNCHANGED=1`; raisin-sql-execution `--test all` only the 8 known failures both ways; oracle stages at `PROPTEST_CASES=48`, `ORACLE_DELIVERY=permuted`, flag on): raisin-rocksdb `--lib` (1286) and `--test all` (519, including `index_parity_test`, `merge_resolution_applies_test`, `reorder_persists_order_key_test`, `apply_revision*`, `replication_out_of_order_test`); raisin-sql-execution `--test all` with the flag off and on: only the 8 known failures.

**Measured** (debug build, in-memory temp DB; relative numbers only). Harness H (new `tests/all/index_read_bench_writes.rs`: one property of a node with P = 30 changed, 50 SQL updates):

| | entries one update writes | bytes | entries after 50 updates | median update |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `index.skip_unchanged` off | 42 | 2945 | 2139 | 792 µs |
| on (branch rebuilt) | 6 | 416 | 339 | 734 µs |

The six: `counter` and `__updated_at` (a tombstone and a put each), plus a `$mixins` tombstone/put pair on every write — the stored empty `$mixins` array decoded as an empty `Vector` (untagged `PropertyValue`) and hashed `vector:0d` instead of `[]`. That pair was NOT harmless (it made the verify report a miss on every written node) and is fixed by the canonical empty-vector hash above, so an update of harness H's shape now writes 4 PROPERTY_INDEX entries (not re-measured). REFERENCE / UNIQUE / ORDERED_CHILDREN are re-put on every write again (one ordered entry, two entries per reference, the node's unique claims). Harness A (lookup by an unchanged slug, NO `LIMIT`, 2000 distractors), median: edits 1 / 10 / 100 / 1000 → off 210 / 215 / 279 / 918 µs, on 219 / 214 / 209 / 211 µs — flat with skip, because the slug's value prefix holds one entry instead of one per edit. `LIMIT 1` (A2) and the early-revision lookup (B) were already flat (~210 µs) after Phase 6 and stay so.

**Review fixes: out-of-order and concurrent writes (2026-10-04).**
- **Successor detection is independent of the gate.** `resolve_baseline` reads the versions above R even when skipping is not permitted (one seek, normally empty) and returns `OutOfOrder` when there are any. Before, flag off (the documented rollback) or a reset gate returned `Full(fallback)` without looking, and an out-of-order write tombstoned at R the entries a skip-written successor kept below R (`out_of_order_write_after_rollback_keeps_successor_entries`).
- **The replica apply path uses `OutOfOrder` too.** A cluster node is both origin and replica: a successor of a replicated op may be a LOCAL skip-written version. `crdt_ops.rs` reads `node_versions_above(R)` and passes `OutOfOrder { prior: replaced, successors }` to `write_all_node_indexes` (which takes a `Baseline` now, never `Predecessor`); `tombstone_superseded_by_newer` no longer tombstones PROPERTY_INDEX (the writer ends R's values at the first successor and re-asserts every successor). REFERENCE and ORDERED_CHILDREN are safe because they are never skipped (above) (`local_skip_write_then_older_replicated_op_keeps_head_entries`).
- **Deletes re-assert successors.** The delete tombstoner (`tombstones::tombstone_property_indexes`, shared by local and replicated deletes) re-puts every stored successor's entries at its own revision after tombstoning at R (`property_delta::reassert_successors`, the same function `write_successors` uses) — a replicated delete older than a local skip-written update no longer masks it.
- **The baseline is re-checked at commit** (`property_delta/staged.rs::StagedDeltaCheck`). While `index.skip_unchanged` is on, each node's first write in a transaction (and every `update_impl`) records the node's newest stored revision BEFORE its baseline is read; the commit re-reads it under the branch record lock that serializes commits (`transaction/commit/staged_index.rs`, `write_batch_with_head_as`) and, when it changed, appends a FULL corrective write of the node's final version against what is stored now (`Full(true prior)`, or `OutOfOrder` with the successors re-asserted). This closes both the in-order race (two writers proving the same predecessor; `concurrent_updates_proving_the_same_predecessor_keep_unchanged_values`) and, for PROPERTY_INDEX, the out-of-order commit the earlier "known gap" described (`out_of_revision_order_commits_keep_head_entries`). Cost while the flag is on: one seek per written node at staging and one under the branch lock at commit; `corrected_staged_writes()` counts corrections. Cross-branch stage no longer skips (`full_baseline`): its batch commits later with no re-check, and promotion is not the hot path.

**Deviations.**
- `Baseline::OutOfOrder` is new. The plan's `Full` ("a newer version already exists … leaving the successor's entries alone") is not enough once successors may have skipped: their unchanged entries live below the write revision, and the full put's tombstones would mask them at HEAD (`write_below_a_stored_successor_keeps_the_successors_skipped_entries`, which fails without it). It also ends this version's values at the first successor's revision (no phantom at HEAD) — the replica's `tombstone_superseded_by_newer` idea on the origin.
- **Known gap (narrowed) — RESOLVED by Phase 7b:** every write funnel now re-validates at commit under a per-node commit lock, flag on or off, and the REFERENCE / SPATIAL / UNIQUE stale-entry tombstones are re-derived beside PROPERTY_INDEX and compound; see Phase 7b for what is still derived at staging only. *The Phase 7 record follows.* Transactions have no conflict enforcement (`check_conflicts` is a no-op), so two overlapping transactions writing the same node can STAGE before either commits and commit out of revision order. For PROPERTY_INDEX with the flag on this is now corrected at commit (`StagedDeltaCheck`, above). Still open: with the flag OFF a staged write is not re-checked (only writes staged before a rollback can race a skip-written successor), and the full writer's out-of-order phantom in REFERENCE / UNIQUE / ORDERED_CHILDREN (a value of the lower version left live at HEAD) is the pre-Phase-7 behaviour. Per-node write serialization remains the general fix.
- **Not fixed (LOW):** the `property_index` rebuild writes at an existing revision (a `versionable=false` node's reused one) without holding `backfill_write_guard`, so an in-place write landing between its read and its batch flush can be overwritten by a stale live entry. Fixing it needs the `node_path` backfill's staged re-check under the guard (`node_path_stage.rs`), not a one-line lock; deferred.
- The per-node "built by the pre-Phase-7 writer" rule is the `property_index` rebuild state record (`done` under this node's id), not a per-content-node record; the flag is a `RocksDBConfig` field plus env var plus runtime setter, plumbed to the server's `[storage]` TOML.
- The verify job checks presence (HEAD entries live as of the newest version), not absence: a stale entry above a version's revision (a pre-Phase-7 `rebuild_indexes` writes everything at the branch HEAD) is masked by the readers' residual checks, not repaired. The legacy in-memory `rebuild_property_indexes` (`async_indexing/rebuild.rs`, clears and re-puts at HEAD) is untouched; the streaming `property_index` repair is the rebuild this phase depends on.
- `unchanged_update_writes_no_entries_for_unchanged_props` counts the PROPERTY_INDEX keys stored at the update's revision (exact), not RocksDB perf counters (which count reads, not writes).
- `gc_then_restore` through `NodeService::restore_version` cannot be driven on RocksDB: it finds versions through a `TreeCommitMeta` nothing writes (Phase 10b, "manual-version snapshot decode"), so the test reproduces its write verbatim (historical content onto the current node, `put_node`).
- The ORDERED_CHILDREN and UNIQUE / REFERENCE skips follow the same gate as PROPERTY_INDEX (a proven baseline); the label-hint seek is always on.
- **Found, not fixed (oracle, pre-existing):** a transaction that moves a node away and creates another at its vacated path fails with `Conflict: Node with path … already exists` (the create's path check reads committed state, not the move) — found by a random history under the flag, reproduces identically with the flag off; same family as the deferred "transactional `move_node_tree` reads the moved subtree from COMMITTED state". One unreproduced stage-3 failure was seen once (flag off, full oracle run under load, output not captured); 64 + 192 + 64 further replay cases and a 64-history sweep found nothing.

**Prerequisites.**
- Phase 6 and Phase 0b (including the interleaved two-origin stage 3).
- **Phase 2.8** (bounded, decode-correct baseline reader) and **Phase 2.10** (merge through shared writers).
- **`get_last_order_label` rewrite** (item 6 below) before this phase touches ORDERED_CHILDREN.
- **A full PROPERTY_INDEX rebuild on every node** (via the fan-out endpoint) before `index.skip_unchanged` is enabled anywhere, because the replica writer historically omitted IS_A/HAS_MIXIN membership and skip-unchanged would never rewrite it. Alternative: the delta writer does a full put whenever the per-node state record says "built by pre-Phase-7 writer".

**Goal.** Replace the three mirrored property-index writers with one function, and stop re-putting unchanged entries in every index that shares the pattern.

**Changes.**
1. New `indexing/property_delta.rs::write_property_index_delta(batch, cfs, ctx, baseline: Baseline, new: &Node, rev)`.
   - It emits tombstones for removed or changed `(name, value, tag)` triples, and live puts **only** for added or changed ones, pseudo-properties and IS_A/HAS_MIXIN membership included.
   - Create and a tag flip behave as today.
   - It replaces `transaction/.../create/indexing.rs:54-216`, `crud/indexing/property_indexes.rs` and `replication/application/index_writers.rs:19-108`. This also fixes the replica writer that omits membership entries.
2. **The baseline is the newest version strictly below the write revision, read bounded by `rev`, never "latest".** `Baseline` is one of:
   - `Predecessor(node)` — from `load_node_before(id, rev)` (Phase 2.8). Skip is allowed **only** when `rev` is strictly greater than the baseline's revision *and* no version newer than `rev` exists.
   - `Full` — a newer version already exists (out-of-order or LWW-applied replication), or the per-node state says "pre-Phase-7 writer". Do a full put at `rev`, emitting tombstones relative to the predecessor and leaving the successor's entries alone. Otherwise a delta against the latest version writes live puts at `rev` that nothing later tombstones (phantom HEAD matches), or writes nothing at `rev` and time travel shows the old value.
   - `NoPrior` — legal **only** when the target id provably has no live version on the target branch.
   - The replication apply path **keeps full puts** (`Full` unconditionally) until oracle stage 3 with permuted two-origin oplogs proves the delta there.
3. **Callers and their baselines** (the write-funnel matrix):
   - `put_node.rs:423-444` — bounded predecessor. **put_node is also the real restore funnel**: single-node RESTORE goes through `NodeService::restore_version` → put_node (`node_service/versioning.rs:227-264`). `jobs/handlers/restore_tree.rs` only delegates to an executor callback that the server wires as `None` (`main.rs:954-958`, `init_system/mod.rs:58-60`), so RESTORE TREE currently fails; if it is wired later it must go through put_node or this writer, never a hand-rolled one.
   - `crud/update.rs:218-233` — bounded predecessor.
   - Replication applicator (`crdt_ops.rs:206-219`, `index_writers.rs`) — `Full` until proven, then bounded predecessor.
   - **Merge resolution (`merge/apply.rs:135,268`) never uses the delta or skip writer.** At M it does a full put of every index, ORDERED_CHILDREN included, and tombstones the union of base, target-head and source-head values (Phase 2.10). Merge replays the source's index entries at their ORIGINAL revisions after writing M (`resolution.rs:173-209`, then `copy_branch_indexes` at `:388-392`); a skipped KeepOurs leaves the target's live entry at its creation revision under the source's later tombstone, so the node vanishes from `slug = A` at HEAD, and the target loses its order label.
   - **Cross-branch stage passes `old_dst` as the baseline** (`stage.rs:114-145,163-185`). It is an upsert, not a create; the earlier "copy and stage go through create (old = None)" claim was wrong for stage. Copy (`copy/{tree,single}.rs`) is a create with `NoPrior`.
4. **UNIQUE:** tombstone and re-put only the properties whose value hash changed (`put_node.rs:474-489`).
5. **REFERENCE:** skip `(path, target)` pairs present in both old and new (`reference_indexes.rs:55-96`).
6. **`get_last_order_label` and the fallback label scan** are rewritten first: `(label, child)` newest-wins, tombstones honoured, returning the lexicographically max **live** label. *(Partly landed with 0b: the fallback now returns the greatest fractional label, counting stale labels — safe, it only over-shoots — and renames/merges no longer leave a stale-low LAST cache. The newest-wins live-only refinement landed with this phase: `ordering/label_lookup.rs::last_live_order_label`, shared with the replication applicator.)* Today the fallback picks the label with the highest revision seen and skips tombstones without recording the label, so an older live entry of a deleted child counts again; skip-unchanged and run-collapse both shift those maxima, and on a metadata-cache miss the next append can mint a label that sorts before or collides with a sibling.
7. **ORDERED_CHILDREN** (`transaction/.../create/ordering.rs:245-294`, the repository `batch/ordered_children.rs`):
   - take the label from `existing.order_key`, verified by a **`(parent, label)` prefix seek filtered by child id** (an exact-key point read is impossible: the key carries the entry's write revision too), and fall back to the sibling scan on a miss;
   - skip the re-put when parent, label and name are all unchanged (the value stores the name, so a rename still rewrites).
8. **`versionable=false`.** The earlier claim "the reused-revision tombstone overwrites the original key, which is fine" holds only when the superseded entry sits at exactly R. Any index fed by a second revision stream (Phase 12 slugs from a translation overlay at `r_t > R`, any repair or rebuild entry above R) is not masked by a tombstone at R. So every derived-index writer in Phases 7, 8 and 12 writes its tombstone at `max(R, newest existing entry revision for that group)`, or allocates a fresh revision when the group's newest entry is above R. Document that `versionable=false` nodes are excluded from merge conflict detection (no revision is minted, so `RevisionMeta.changed_nodes` never sees them; `conflict/detection.rs`, `resolution.rs:414-460`), and both branches' writes land on the same R0 keys.
9. **Verify job:** `management/async_indexing/verify_property_index.rs` samples nodes and checks that their HEAD entries exist, then enqueues the existing rebuild for any misses. Streaming and fanned out like every repair. It replaces the self-healing that every update provided by accident, but sampling alone does not close pre-existing holes, which is why the full rebuild is a prerequisite.

**Migration.** None on disk. Precondition: the per-node full rebuild above.

**Replication.** The apply path writes indexes through the same function, with `Full` baselines until proven. Derived state is still rebuilt locally. Vault-before-index ordering is unchanged, because the delta writer runs after vaulting.

**Branch / copy / move / restore.** Merge: full put at M (item 3). Stage: `old_dst` baseline. Copy: `NoPrior`. Restore: put_node with a bounded predecessor. On a move, the ordered entry for the new parent is a change and is written. A reorder changes the label and is written.

**Tests to add.**
- `unchanged_update_writes_no_entries_for_unchanged_props` (perf-counter count).
- `is_a_and_has_mixin_on_replica`.
- `time_travel_between_two_writes_with_skip`.
- `replicated_op_older_than_head_does_not_leave_live_entries`.
- Oracle stage 3 with shuffled, interleaved two-origin oplogs and permuted apply order.
- `merge_keep_ours_after_skip_unchanged_property_still_matches`.
- `merge_keep_ours_preserves_target_order_label`.
- `merge_keep_ours_source_only_value_not_matched`.
- `promote_removed_property_not_matched_on_publish_branch`.
- `unique_violation_still_detected_after_unchanged_update`.
- `reference_removed_is_tombstoned_unchanged_is_not_rewritten`.
- `reorder_and_rename_still_rewrite_ordered_entry`.
- `last_order_label_ignores_deleted_child_and_returns_max_live` (also a 0b template).
- `versionable_false_update_with_skip`, `versionable_false_with_merge`.
- `gc_then_restore` through both SQL RESTORE and `restore_version`, asserting that the index state after the restore equals the index state of the restored revision.
- Existing guards: `index_parity_test`, `merge_resolution_applies_test`, `reorder_persists_order_key_test`, `apply_revision*`.

**Rollback.** Config flag `index.skip_unchanged=false` re-enables full re-puts, which is always safe because old behaviour produced a superset.

**Expected gain** (harness H).
- PROPERTY_INDEX puts per typical update drop from about P + 7 + members to 2–4 (`__updated_at` / `__updated_by` plus whatever changed) on the origin. Replicas keep full puts until stage 3 proves the delta.
- Similar reductions for UNIQUE, REFERENCE and ORDERED_CHILDREN.
- Fork cost and GC work shrink accordingly. This directly serves the 5k writes/sec goal.

**Risk.** Medium-high. An incorrect baseline loses or invents entries (mitigated by the bounded baseline, `Full` on any doubt, and oracle stage 3). Self-healing is lost (mitigated by the prerequisite rebuild and the verify job).

## Phase 7b — Commit-order-independent index deltas; `index_skip_unchanged` ON by default — DONE (uncommitted)

**Owner decision (2026-10-04).** Semantics stay last-writer-wins: transactions are NOT isolated, nothing is rejected or retried, and `raisin_locks` stays optional (never a requirement). The problem fixed is Phase 7's known gap: two overlapping writers of one node can commit out of revision order, and the delta writer then skips an entry the winning version needs, so the node-local index disagrees with the node's records. Derived indexes are node-local (nothing indexed replicates), so in-process serialization is enough and is cluster-safe.

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-04).**
- **The per-node commit lock** (`crates/raisin-rocksdb/src/indexing/node_lock.rs`). The existing in-process `KeyedMutex` (`jobs/keyed_mutex.rs`: waiters queue, the entry is retired on last release), one process-wide registry, keyed by `(database identity, tenant, repo, branch, node_id)` — the database identity keeps a test origin and its replica in one process from contending. No config, no Redis, no `[locks]` section. `lock_nodes` takes a commit's nodes in sorted key order (duplicates collapse), always BEFORE the branch record lock (`repositories::lock_branch_record`), never while holding it, and never twice in one call chain (the mutex is not re-entrant; no funnel nests one commit inside another). Acquisition is an async tokio lock; the write it guards runs on the blocking pool, and the node guard (and the branch record guard) MOVE INTO that blocking task and drop only after `db.write` returns — held in the async frame, a commit future dropped mid-write (client disconnect, timeout wrapper) released the node with its batch still in flight, and another writer re-validated against stored state that lacked it (review fix, 2026-10-04: `NodeCommit::write`, `write_batch_with_head_as`, the transaction commit).
- **The commit step** (`indexing/node_commit.rs::NodeCommit`): lock the nodes → re-validate each staged index write against what is stored NOW → write the batch on the blocking pool → release. Repository funnels either call `NodeCommit::write` or hand the commit to `BranchRepositoryImpl::write_batch_with_head_as` / `write_nodes_with_head` (node locks, re-validation, then the branch record lock and the HEAD advance; that write moved to the blocking pool too). The transaction commit does the same three steps in `transaction/commit/{mod,staged_index}.rs`: `lock_written_nodes` (every node in the read cache's `nodes`, `moved_nodes` and `delta_checks`), `revalidate_staged_index_writes`, then the branch record lock, then the write; the re-validation moved OUT of the branch record lock (it is correct under the node locks alone, and the branch lock is held for less).
- **The one re-validation, generalized** (`indexing/property_delta/staged.rs`, `StagedDeltaCheck`). (a) What a write assumed is recorded BEFORE the version it derives from is read (`before_read` → `PendingDeltaCheck::at(revision)`): `put_node` and `update_impl` used to record it after reading `existing_node` / `old_node`, so a commit landing between the read and the record went unseen whenever the baseline was that read (always with the flag off). (b) The markers carry a hash of the stored bytes beside the revision, so an in-place (`versionable=false`) rewrite at the same revision counts as a change. (c) Bulk readers that list nodes before staging (moves, re-stamps, cascade deletes, merge resolutions, promotion) record `always`: the commit re-derives unconditionally. (d) `revalidate_final(final_version: Option<&Node>)` corrects a DELETE too: the true predecessor's entries tombstoned at R, successors re-asserted (`tombstone_all_entries` + `reassert_successors`), and the compound delete tombstoner (`tombstone_compound_for_delete`); the compound half lives in `staged_compound.rs`. The correction is the one writer (`write_property_index_delta` with `Full` / `OutOfOrder`, the Phase 7 `OutOfOrder` baseline and `reassert_successors`) — no second derivation. (e) REFERENCE, SPATIAL and UNIQUE never skip but derive their stale-entry tombstones from the version read at staging too, so a racing version left values nothing ends (`REFERENCES(x)` or `ST_DWITHIN` matching a node whose newest version has neither; a UNIQUE claim refusing a value the node no longer holds). They are corrected beside it (`staged_neighbours.rs`) through their own one helpers (`add_stale_reference_tombstones`, `tombstone_superseded_spatial_indexes`, `unique_delta::{write_unique_delta, end_claims_at}`): the predecessor's values the final state lacks end at R, the final state's values the first successor lacks end at that successor's revision. UNIQUE reads the types' `unique: true` names from the definitions cache (no NodeType read under the lock); a cold cache skips that half and logs (a stale claim refuses a value, it never admits a duplicate).
- **Funnel matrix** (every local writer of a node record or node index takes the lock):

| funnel | check recorded | final state |
|---|---|---|
| transaction `put_node` (SQL DML, `NodeService`, restore via SQL `RESTORE`) | before the existing-node read (first write in the tx) | the cached version |
| transaction `put_node` as a create, `add_node` | before anything is read (markers usually `(None, None)`): a version of the id committed in between is ended | the cached version |
| transaction `put_node` / repository `update_impl` IN PLACE (`versionable=false`) | before the read, `in_place_at(R, resolved_targets)`: the marker's byte hash sees a racing in-place rewrite; the correction diffs against the content stored AT R and places entries like the staged write (`InPlaceTargets`, in-place UNIQUE) | the written version |
| transaction copy | none (fresh ids) — locked | — |
| transaction `move_node_tree` | `always` for each re-stamped record; `rekey(listed)` for each descendant re-keyed without a record rewrite | the moved record |
| transaction `delete_node` | before the node read (first write in the tx) | deleted |
| repository `update_impl` (also `update_property`, publish / unpublish) | before the `old_node` read | the written version |
| repository `add_impl`, `copy_node` (via `add_impl`), deep create's leaf | before anything is read (creates) | the created version |
| repository tree copy, deep create's intermediate folders | none (fresh ids) — locked | — |
| repository `delete_impl` | before the node read | deleted |
| repository cascade delete | `always` per deleted node; now ONE batch with its unique claims and revision index (it was three writes) | deleted |
| repository tree move, reorder, rebalance | `always` per re-stamped record; `rekey(listed)` per descendant a move re-keys without rewriting; every moved node locked | the re-stamped record |
| cross-branch promotion (stage + prune + displaced) | `always` per written or deleted target node | written / deleted |
| merge resolutions (`write_resolved_node`, `write_resolved_deletion`, now async) | `always` | written / deleted |
| replication applicator (`apply_operation`) | none: the lock is held for the whole apply of the op, so its baseline read (`load_node_replaced_by`, `node_versions_above`) and its write see no local commit of those nodes in between | — |

- **`index.skip_unchanged` ON by default** (`RocksDBConfig` presets, `RocksDBConfig::default()`; server `[storage] index_skip_unchanged`, unset = on). `RAISIN_INDEX_SKIP_UNCHANGED=0|false|off|no` turns it off and `1|true|on|yes` on, overriding the config either way (it could only turn it on before). The per-branch gate is unchanged: a branch skips only once its `property_index` rebuild is `done` under this node's id; until then its writes are the full puts they always were.
- **The rebuild queues itself** (`management/async_indexing/repair/auto_property_index.rs`), through the `node_path` backfill's chain (`auto_node_path`, now generic over `RepairKind::PropertyIndex`): ordinary `IndexRepair` jobs on the unified queue, one branch at a time, streaming, resumable, disk-prechecked, rate-limited, never on the boot path — the chain starts 60 s after the job system (`schedule_property_index_rebuild` in `init_job_system`), each link queues the next pending branch, a failed link is skipped until the next start (`auto_targets`). Requests: a fork asks once its index copy is done (`copy_branch_indexes`, which also covers a merge target the copy invalidated); a branch created without a source asks at once; a checkpoint ingest starts the chain after its invalidation (`reenqueue_repairs_after_ingest`); a verify miss queues the rebuild as before. Pending = the flag is on and the branch is not rebuilt here; with the flag off nothing is pending. `RAISIN_PROPERTY_INDEX_AUTO_REBUILD=0` turns the automatic rebuild off (the admin endpoint still works). The storage registry that lets code without a storage handle queue a build (`repair/requests.rs`: `register_requester`, the debounce) was the localized name index's private copy; it is now shared by both chains (`localized_name::auto` uses it).
- **Translations (owner item 5): confirmed nothing to race.** No write-time node-delete translation derivation exists any more: `stage_node_deletion`, `stage_deletion_of_late_version` and `add_translation_tombstones_to_batch` are gone; the one reader applies `translation_read::ended_by_node_delete` (also used by `management/default_language.rs`, history-GC collapse and the cross-branch prune note).

**Read-then-write derivations audited (owner item 5).** Under the lock and re-validated at commit: PROPERTY_INDEX, COMPOUND, REFERENCE, SPATIAL, UNIQUE. COMPOUND fails the workspace's indexes closed ONLY for an index-only NODE_PATH write — an entry newer than the recorded marker with no NODES version at its own revision (`property_delta/staged_markers.rs::index_only_path_write`: an ancestor move re-pathing a descendant it does not rewrite). Every record write writes both NODES and NODE_PATH, so an ordinary write race is corrected from NODES and leaves the indexes `Ready`; treating every NODE_PATH change as index-only (the first pass) marked a hot node's whole workspace `NotBuilt` and queued a rebuild on every race (review fix). A move's re-key of a descendant it does not rewrite is checked too (`StagedDeltaCheck::rekey` → `staged_compound::correct_rekey`): when the version stored below the move's revision is not the listed one (an update committed between the listing and the move's commit), the re-key is re-derived from the stored version — the staged tuples it lacks ended, its old-path tuples ended — and a node deleted in between has the staged tuples ended. Still derived at staging only — serialized now (no write lands inside another's commit step) but NOT re-derived when a racing version committed between a write's read and its commit:
- PATH_INDEX old-path tombstone, ORDERED_CHILDREN old-entry tombstone and NODE_PATH: a move committing between another writer's read and commit can leave the node reachable at both paths and listed under both parents (the newest record names one). Pre-existing; correcting it needs the replica's ownership-aware logic (`path_owned_at`, `stored_order_label_at`) on the origin.
- The localized name index in the repository funnels (the transaction commit re-syncs it under the branch lock): lookups are verified with an always-correct row-level fallback, so a stale claim costs a fallback, not a wrong answer.
- The `property_index` rebuild itself (Phase 7's LOW item, in-place writes only) and the merge's verbatim index replay (`copy_branch_indexes`, inside the merge window); backup import (offline restore) is not locked.
- **Open (review LOW items, deferred):** (a) a fail-closed COMPOUND mark is written and its build requested BEFORE the commit's batch lands, so a small workspace's build can stamp `Ready` before the batch's stale tuples land; the fix is staging the `NotBuilt` mark in the commit's own batch under the transition lock and requesting the build after the write — it now fires only for a true index-only race (above), which narrows the window to rare moves. (b) Re-validation runs synchronously on the async worker with every node lock held; bulk funnels (`always` per node: promotion, cascade delete, tree move, merge) do one or two reads and a re-derivation per node there and roughly double the batch. The fix is one blocking task that owns the guard and does re-validation plus write (the guard half is done); not taken here because the transaction's re-validation borrows its read cache across the branch-lock step.

**Rollout.** One release, no split: nothing changes on disk or on the wire — the lock is process-local, the re-validation writes only what the one writer writes, the automatic rebuild uses the existing repair records. The flag being on is the "later step" of Phase 7; the owner made it the default in this release because the lock removes the race that kept it off, and the per-branch gate still keeps every un-rebuilt branch on full puts. Rollback: `index_skip_unchanged = false` (or `RAISIN_INDEX_SKIP_UNCHANGED=0`) — the full writer produces a superset; the lock and the re-validation stay (they are correct with the flag off too). `RAISIN_PROPERTY_INDEX_AUTO_REBUILD=0` stops the background rebuilds alone.

**Tests (all green).**
- raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/delta_writer/commit_lock_tests.rs`: `overlapping_local_transactions_out_of_revision_order_match_a_rebuild`, `local_commit_racing_an_out_of_order_replicated_write_matches_a_rebuild`, `multi_node_transactions_in_opposite_orders_do_not_deadlock`. The first committer pauses between its re-validation and its write (`node_lock::test_hooks::pause_commit_of`, scoped to one database), so the interleaving is forced. "Equals a full rebuild" = at every stored version's revision and at HEAD, for every `(property, value)` any version held, the raw PROPERTY_INDEX answer equals what the versions say. Each race test runs twice: with the lock it must match (and the contender must still be waiting when the pause ends); with the lock switched off for that database (`disable_node_locks`) the same interleaving must REPRODUCE the divergence — the in-CI proof that the test fails with the mutex removed.
- `tests/all/delta_writer/auto_rebuild_tests.rs`: `skip_unchanged_is_on_by_default`, `unrebuilt_branches_get_the_rebuild_queued_branch_by_branch` (flag off → nothing pending; one link at a time; a finished link queues the next; a rebuilt branch is no longer pending), `a_fork_requests_its_rebuild`.
- `tests/all/delta_writer/commit_lock_review_tests.rs` (review fixes, each fails with its fix removed): `racing_writers_of_one_node_keep_the_workspace_compound_index_ready`, `a_descendant_updated_during_a_move_is_rekeyed_from_its_stored_version`, `overlapping_in_place_rewrites_leave_no_phantom_value`, `racing_creates_of_one_id_leave_no_phantom_value`, `a_commit_dropped_mid_write_keeps_its_node_locked_until_the_write_lands` (`node_lock::test_hooks::pause_write_of` pauses INSIDE the blocking write task; the commit future is dropped there and the node must stay locked). `compound_review_tests::concurrent_ancestor_move_fails_the_compound_index_closed` now moves `p` without renaming it: a rename rewrites the child's record (it holds the parent's NAME), and that record write is corrected from NODES, not failed closed.
- Unit: `indexing::node_lock::tests::{opposite_orders_do_not_deadlock, same_node_on_another_database_does_not_contend}`.
- MVCC oracle with the flag ON (now the default; `ORACLE_VERBOSE` shows `skipped_unchanged_entries` > 0): all stages at `PROPTEST_CASES=48`, stage 3 with `ORACLE_DELIVERY=permuted` at 48, and all stages with `RAISIN_INDEX_SKIP_UNCHANGED=0` (the rollback) at the default 16 — green.
- Existing guards: raisin-rocksdb `--lib` (1326 passed) and `--test all` (638 passed, 13 ignored; 643 after the review fixes — every Phase 7/8 delta-writer, merge, replication, cluster and repair test); raisin-sql-execution `--test all`: only the 8 known failures; raisin-transport-http `http_index_repairs`.

**Measured** (harness H, debug build, relative only): one property of 30 changed, 50 SQL updates — flag off 41 entries / 2876 bytes per update, median 907 µs; on 4 entries / 280 bytes (`__updated_at` and `counter`, a tombstone and a put each — the Phase 7 canonical empty-vector fix confirmed), median 859 µs. The lock costs a few map operations per written node; the recorded markers cost two seeks per update at staging and two under the lock at commit, as Phase 7's check did. (Harness H's "off" leg now sets the flag off explicitly; with the new default it had silently measured "on" twice.)

**Deviations.**
- The deadlock test cannot "fail with the mutex removed" (without the mutex there is nothing to deadlock on); it forces overlap with a pause and bounds both commits with a timeout, and the unit test drives opposite orders through `lock_nodes` directly.
- Bulk readers record `always` (re-derive at commit) instead of a check before each read: a listing has no per-node "before the read". This doubles the PROPERTY_INDEX / compound work of moves, re-stamps, cascade deletes, merge resolutions and promotions, none of which is a hot path.
- The re-validation moved out of the branch record lock (it was under it in Phase 7); the node locks are what make it sound.
- The cascade delete became one atomic batch, and the dead `delete_descendants_with_revision` / `delete_node_with_revision` (never called; unlocked writers) were removed. The merge resolution writers became async.
- REFERENCE, SPATIAL and UNIQUE corrections were added beyond the owner's PROPERTY_INDEX + compound scope, through their existing helpers, because the audit of item 5 found them derived at staging too; PATH_INDEX / ORDERED_CHILDREN / NODE_PATH were not (above).
- Not locked: backup import, checkpoint ingest, the merge's index replay, and repair/rebuild jobs.

## Phase 8 — COMPOUND: reader first, then derived tombstones, then replica writes — DONE (uncommitted)

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-04).** `crates/raisin-rocksdb/src/indexing/compound/` (`entries.rs`, `writer.rs`, `group.rs`, `delete.rs`, `defs.rs`, `cache.rs`, `cold.rs`, `tests.rs`):
- **Step 1, the reader** (`repositories/compound_index.rs::newest_per_tuple`). Each `(tuple, node)` is decided by its NEWEST entry at or below the read revision; a tombstone ends only its own tuple (the old node-wide "tombstoned" set hid a node's live tuple sorting after an ended one, and the reader relied on the writer overwriting keys in place). `CompoundIndexRepository::scan_compound_index` gained `max_revision: Option<&HLC>`; the SQL executor passes the statement revision (`statement_snapshot`, so a HEAD read is bounded by the branch HEAD and a `__revision = N` read sees the index as of N). Keys are parsed from the RIGHT (`entries::parse_entry_key`: node id after the last `\0`, the 16-byte revision before it) — splitting on `\0` broke on Integer / Boolean / timestamp columns and on a revision whose counter is 0. One row per node; a node with two live tuples (an index left inconsistent by an older writer) is reported once, at its first position.
- **Step 2, the one writer** (`writer::write_compound_delta`). The old tuple is DERIVED from the baseline version with the one derivation (`entries::compound_entries` → `extract_compound_column_value`), the tombstone is a NEW key at the write revision, and the workspace-wide prefix scan with its in-place `T` overwrite (`tombstones/index_tombstones.rs`, `tombstone_compound_indexes_only`) is gone. The baseline is the PROPERTY_INDEX's (`indexing::Baseline`, decided once by `resolve_baseline`): unchanged tuples are skipped only under `Baseline::Predecessor` (flag on, branch rebuilt, proven predecessor); `Full` / `NoPrior` put every tuple; `OutOfOrder` ends this version's tuples at the first successor and re-asserts every successor at its own revision (as Phase 7 does). A tombstone or put with no stored successors lands at `max(R, newest entry of its (group, node) above R)` (`group.rs`, Phase 7 item 8; one seek in the normal case, capped at 256 keys, `compound_group_walks_capped()`). Every write funnel goes through it: transaction `put_node` / `add_node` / `move_node_tree`, repository `add` / `update_impl` / tree move / copy / deep create (new: it wrote none) / cross-branch stage, and the re-stamp writers (`rewrite_node_record_to_batch`, now async: reorder and rebalance re-stamp `updated_at` and used to leave compound entries over it stale). The repository move writes a rewritten node's entries once, from the rewritten record. The commit-time re-check (`StagedDeltaCheck::revalidate`) corrects the compound write with the property one (`correct_compound`). Deletes (`tombstones::add_node_tombstones*`, every delete funnel) derive the deleted version's tuples when its definitions are cached, else find the node's live tuples by the old scan — but tombstone at the delete revision, never in place (`delete.rs`).
- **Step 3, replica and merge** (`replication/.../applicator/compound_marker.rs::commit_with_schema_indexes`, `branches/merge/apply.rs`). The replicated upsert writes COMPOUND (through the one writer, `Full` / `OutOfOrder` like its property write) and UNIQUE claims (`write_unique_delta`, plus `end_claims_at` the first successor out of order) in the node's own batch, from the **definitions cache** (`defs.rs`: per `{database, tenant, repo, branch, node type}` the compound declarations merged along `extends` — the ONE inheritance walk, the repository's and the rebuild's copies are gone — and the `unique: true` names). The apply path only PEEKS; there is no NodeType read on it. The cache is refreshed — re-resolved and swapped in atomically, so there is no cold window — right after every NodeType write: the local repository (`node_types/crud.rs`), the applicator's NodeType arms (between ops, never inside a node batch), and `Event::Schema` (`jobs/event_handler/compound_defs.rs`); it is warmed by the boot sweep and by every compound build, dropped on branch create/delete, and registered with `derived_cache_registry` (checkpoint ingest) as a DATABASE-scoped invalidator: the ingest calls `invalidate_derived_caches_for_database` with its own path, so it drops only that database's definitions (a process-wide drop colded every other database in the process — found by the gate as a cross-test flake, `merge_refreshes_the_target_definitions_cache` racing `checkpoint_network_test`). Generations are per branch (`cache.rs`): a single process-wide counter let one branch's refresh discard another's entries — found by the oracle, a replica going cold mid-replay. **Cold rule:** a type not cached → the write marks the workspace's compound indexes `NotBuilt` in the same batch (the Phase 2.5 generation) and requests a local build (`cold::request_build`); the job event handler drains requests on the next node or batch-applied event: warm the branch, sweep the workspace's builds. Never a silent skip. Merge resolutions resolve definitions with their unique names (`merge/unique_props.rs`), tombstone the union of superseded tuples at M and put the resolved tuples at M; the merge no longer marks `NotBuilt`. **Phase 2 item 5's always-stale marker is retired** for cached definitions: replica compound queries are index-served again.
- **Builds re-derive as of a HEAD floor (review fix).** ONE build pass (`indexing/compound/build.rs`), shared by the REBUILD (`management/async_indexing/compound_rebuild.rs`) and the per-index build job (`jobs/handlers/compound_index.rs`; the job's own scan is gone). After the clear it reads the branch HEAD — the **history floor**, stamped as `built_through` — and streams NODES: each node's newest version at or below the floor is written at that version's own revision (path materialized at the floor), and every version ABOVE the floor (a write committed during the build, or a version stranded above HEAD) is replayed oldest first at its own revision with the tombstones that end the tuples it drops. History below the floor is NOT re-derived (an ancestor move re-keys `__parent_path` without a NODES version, so NODES alone cannot reproduce it); instead `CompoundAvailability::at_revision` makes the planner refuse the index for a `__revision = N` read below the floor (`PhysicalPlanner::set_read_revision`, set by both engine paths and EXPLAIN), so time travel before a rebuild scans. Definitions are read from STORAGE for every build (`defs::fresh_branch`, never cache-first), and the same read feeds the cache. Both builds are prechecked BEFORE anything is cleared: disk headroom (2x COMPOUND_INDEX) and a read-only pass that refuses when any node cannot be decoded or placed (no path); a writing pass that still meets one never stamps `Ready` (the job marks `NotBuilt`, the REBUILD leaves `Building`). The pass holds one node's versions in memory at a time and commits bounded batches (10k entries); it is still clear-then-rewrite with no resumable cursor or rate limit (deviation, see below).
- **One text encoding for String columns** (`CompoundColumnValue::text` / `date_text`, raisin-storage, used by the writer AND the SQL executor's equality prefix). A string `parse_from_rfc3339` accepts decodes from storage as `PropertyValue::Date` (untagged), so the in-memory version a writer indexes and the stored version a baseline or rebuild derives disagreed: an update could not tombstone what the insert wrote (`dml_index_and_predicate_maintenance` caught it), and a rebuild silently left such nodes out. Both spellings now encode as the date's canonical RFC3339 text. Because a row-level `->>` comparison sees the property's own spelling, the planner keeps an equality on a String column whose literal parses as RFC3339 as a RESIDUAL filter after the compound scan, so the index-served rows are exactly the scan's (review fix).
- **Compound state record v2** (`CompoundIndexState::VERSION`). Indexes built by an earlier writer hold entries the derivation cannot reproduce (raw date strings) and no history; a v1 record reads `Unusable` (a scan) until rebuilt. The sweeps (boot, schema events, cold drains) do NOT rebuild an index whose record is merely an older format (`is_format_upgrade`) unless `RAISIN_COMPOUND_FORMAT_REBUILD=1` is set (default OFF; `compound_state::format_rebuild_enabled`) — a format bump must not rebuild every index on every node at boot (repair discipline: admin-triggered, disk precheck).
- **Review fixes (2026-10-04).** Two reviewers' findings, each verified and fixed in the shared path with a regression test:
  - *Writer.* A PUT lands on a newer entry of its group only when the write is IN PLACE (a stored NODES version at its own revision, `writer::stored_at`); an ordinary write's put stays at R, so an ancestor move's newer tombstone keeps masking it (it used to be overwritten with LIVE). The commit-time re-check (`StagedDeltaCheck`) is captured on every update regardless of `index.skip_unchanged` (with skipping off, two writers that both read P each ended only P's tuple; the loser's stayed live), and it also records the node's newest NODE_PATH revision: a concurrent index-only write (ancestor move) cannot be corrected from NODES, so it fails the workspace's compound indexes closed (`NotBuilt` + build request).
  - *Delete.* With successors stored, the tombstoner ends the version in force at the delete revision (newest at or below it), not the caller's newest version (a late replicated delete), matching the cold scan.
  - *Merge.* `compound_after_merge_copy` (both merges, after `copy_branch_indexes`): re-reads the target's definitions from storage (the copy brought the source's NodeType versions); and marks each `Ready` target index `NotBuilt` (+ build request) unless the source's same index is `Ready`, same declaration, with a floor BELOW the earliest source revision that changed nodes since the common ancestor — a source rebuilt after its changes no longer holds the tombstones that end the target's tuples. A fork now inherits the source's `Ready` records with the copied entries (`compound_state::fork`), so a fork that is merged back without a rebuild keeps the target `Ready`.
  - *Definitions cache.* `refresh.rs` holds the one refresh (local NodeType write, applicator schema arm, `Event::Schema`, merge, builds, boot warm, cold drain). A refresh that finds a CACHED type's compound declarations changed marks those index names `NotBuilt` on every workspace of the branch and requests a build (`defs::changed_index_names`, shared with `invalidate_changed_compound_state`; `CompoundStateStore::mark_names_on_branch`) — on replicas and after merges this is the only place that notices. A cold build request carries the cold node types (`cold::request_build_for`), and the drain resolves them, so a type with no NodeType record is cached as "no definitions" after ONE cold write instead of rebuilding the workspace on every write.

**Rollout.** No release split: every index this phase touches is local derived state. Order: (1) ship — every pre-existing compound index reads `Unusable` (v1 format) and those queries scan, correct and slower; nothing rebuilds at boot. (2) Per node, at a quiet time with disk headroom, an admin runs `REBUILD … compound` per workspace (prechecked: 2x COMPOUND_INDEX free, no unplaceable node), or sets `RAISIN_COMPOUND_FORMAT_REBUILD=1` on ONE node at a time and restarts it so its boot sweep queues the builds, then unsets it. Never all nodes at once. Indexes created or redeclared after the upgrade build as before (v2 records). Skipping unchanged tuples follows `index.skip_unchanged` (Phase 7's flag, default OFF, gated per branch on this node's `property_index` rebuild) through the shared baseline; nothing extra to enable. Downgrade: an older binary reads tuples ended by a non-overwriting tombstone with the old node-wide reader and can hide rows — downgrading below this release is unsupported (already the case below Phase 10b).

**Tests (all green).**
- Review regressions: raisin-rocksdb unit `indexing::compound::review_tests::{ordinary_put_stays_below_a_newer_move_tombstone, in_place_put_still_lands_on_a_newer_entry, late_delete_ends_the_version_in_force_at_its_revision, build_indexes_the_floor_version_and_replays_versions_above_it, build_counts_a_node_it_cannot_place}`, `jobs::handlers::compound_index::tests::compound_build_refuses_before_clearing_an_unplaceable_node`; `tests/all/delta_writer/compound_review_tests::{time_travel_below_a_rebuild_is_not_index_served, merge_after_a_source_rebuild_does_not_leave_the_target_tuple_live, merge_from_an_unrebuilt_fork_keeps_the_target_ready, concurrent_updates_with_skip_off_end_the_losing_tuple, concurrent_ancestor_move_fails_the_compound_index_closed, merge_refreshes_the_target_definitions_cache, replica_type_without_nodetype_turns_warm_after_one_cold_write, rebuild_refuses_before_clearing_when_a_node_cannot_be_placed, format_upgrade_is_left_to_an_admin_rebuild}`; raisin-storage `compound::tests::{a_read_below_the_build_floor_is_unusable, an_older_record_version_is_a_format_upgrade}`; raisin-sql-execution `planner::tests_compound_review::{time_travel_below_the_build_floor_is_not_index_served, date_literal_equality_stays_a_residual_filter}`. After the fixes: raisin-rocksdb `--lib` 1301 and `--test all` 549 green; raisin-sql-execution `--lib` 937 green and `--test all` only the 8 known failures, flag off and on.
- raisin-rocksdb unit (`indexing::compound::tests`): `entry_key_round_trips_through_null_bytes`, `changed_tuple_is_tombstoned_at_the_revision_and_history_reads_it`, `unchanged_tuple_is_skipped_only_under_a_predecessor`, `tombstone_lands_on_an_entry_above_the_write_revision`, `out_of_order_write_reasserts_a_skip_written_successor`, `a_tombstone_hides_only_its_own_tuple`, `delete_derives_with_warm_definitions_and_scans_when_cold`, `date_like_string_and_its_stored_date_derive_one_entry`.
- raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/delta_writer/`: `compound_tests::{compound_time_travel_after_update (flag off and on), repository_update_and_delete_keep_compound_history, cold_delete_scans_and_keeps_history, compound_update_cost_independent_of_workspace_size, concurrent_updates_keep_the_compound_tuple (in and out of revision order), merge_resolution_writes_compound_entries}`; `compound_replica_tests::{replica_compound_query_correct_without_rebuild (with the replica's UNIQUE claim), replica_out_of_order_compound_keeps_head, replica_cold_definition_cache_marks_not_built}`. Existing guards green: `move_tree_compound_reindex_test`, `branch_fork_index_copies_test`, `replica_compound_state_test` (now the cold case), the compound build lease tests; raisin-rocksdb `--lib` 1295 and `--test all` 540, flag off and on.
- `compound_update_cost_independent_of_workspace_size` measures three ways at 20 and 400 indexed nodes: the whole SQL-path update, the writer alone (equal reads), and — proof the perf counters see a scan — the cold delete fallback (grows by ≥ 300 steps).
- MVCC oracle: the compound template is asserted exactly at every revision (expected failure pruned); stage 3's replica warms its definitions at boot and the replay asserts its compound index is still `Ready` at the end, so the replica's compound templates are INDEX-served under random, permuted and unbuffered-witness delivery. Green at `PROPTEST_CASES=48`, flag off and on, `ORACLE_DELIVERY=permuted`. raisin-sql-execution `--test all`: only the 8 known failures, flag off and on.

**Deviations.**
- The skip uses the property index's baseline rather than a compound-specific one, so one decision governs both writers, and the commit-time correction covers both.
- Beyond the plan text: the String-column date canonicalization, the compound state v2 bump, the build's inheritance and clear, deep create's compound entries, the re-stamp writers' compound re-key, and the UNIQUE claims on replicas (`end_claims_at`).
- "Fall back to the scan when a NodeType's compound definition changed between old and new" is not needed: a declaration change marks the index `NotBuilt` (`invalidate_changed_compound_state` locally; the definition-hash check in availability on replicas), and the build clears and re-derives.
- The definitions cache reads each NodeType at its NEWEST stored version, not HEAD: on a replica a NodeType op lands before the op that advances HEAD over it.
- **Known gaps.** A cold replicated write holds no UNIQUE claim for that version (UNIQUE has no availability gate; the pre-Phase-8 replica behaviour, counted in `cold_definition_writes`; the refresh-after-schema-op and boot warm make it rare). A delete with COLD definitions landing below stored successors cannot re-assert them (only matters with skip on). The legacy apply arms (`MoveNode`, `SetProperty`, legacy create) still write no compound entries and keep the mark (+ build request). *(Those arms are gone since Phase 11d.)* ~~A node whose type has no NodeType record is never cached~~ — fixed by the review: the cold request names its types and the drain caches them. The commit-time re-check cannot CORRECT a concurrent index-only write (ancestor move); it fails the index closed instead (a rebuild). A long transaction that staged its compound write under definitions a NodeType write has since replaced is not re-checked at commit (the declaration change marked the index, so it is not served, but the stray entries survive until the next build). The build has no resumable cursor or rate limit (clear-then-rewrite under `Building`); its memory is one node's versions.
- The unbuffered random replay (`ORACLE_DELIVERY=unbuffered`) still fails on the documented ORDERED_CHILDREN old-parent gap; no compound mismatch was seen in a 32-case probe, but it stops at the first failing case, so the "~1 in 16 compound past-revision on a replica" note is not claimed fixed.

1. **Reader:** newest-per-`(tuple, node)` shadowing. Today the reader relies on the writer overwriting keys in place.
2. **Writer:** on UPDATE, derive the old tuple from the bounded baseline (Phase 2.8's reader) with the shared `extract_compound_column_value`, write `T` at `max(new revision, newest existing entry revision for that group)` (Phase 7 item 8), and skip unchanged tuples. This removes the workspace-wide prefix scan and the in-place overwrite in `tombstones/index_tombstones.rs:417-456`. If a NodeType's compound definition changed between old and new, fall back to the scan for that update only.
3. **Replica and merge:** write compound and unique entries on replicated upserts and on merge apply. Definitions are resolved **off** the apply hot path and cached; the cache is invalidated on `Event::Schema` and registered with `derived_cache_registry`, so there is no NodeType read inside the batch (the deadlock rule). **Cold-cache rule:** when a definition is unresolved at apply time, mark `NotBuilt` (with the Phase 2.5 generation) and enqueue a local build; never skip silently. Then remove Phase 2's `NotBuilt`-on-every-upsert marker. **Prefer pulling this step forward into Phase 2** over shipping a long-lived replica scan fallback.

**Migration.** History destroyed by the old in-place overwrite cannot be recovered, and HEAD stays correct. A compound rebuild re-derives HEAD (per-node lease, Phase 2.11). Do not run Phase 9's run-collapse on COMPOUND before step 2.

**Tests to add.** `compound_time_travel_after_update`, `compound_update_cost_independent_of_workspace_size` (perf counter), `replica_compound_query_correct_without_rebuild`, `replica_cold_definition_cache_marks_not_built`, `move_tree_compound_reindex_test` (existing), `branch_fork_index_copies_test` (existing).

**Rollback.** Per step. Step 1 is safe on its own.

**Expected gain.** Removes an O(workspace) read from every update of a node type that has compound indexes. Replicas serve compound queries from the index.

**Risk.** Medium. The order must be reader, then writer.

## Phase 9 — Run-collapse GC (the migration for existing bloat) — DONE (uncommitted)

**Goal.** Shrink existing redundant index history without changing any answer at any revision.

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-04).** `crates/raisin-rocksdb/src/management/history_gc/collapse/` (`mod.rs`, `detector.rs`, `pass.rs`, `prereq.rs`, `watermark.rs`, `tests.rs`), `history_gc/pins.rs`, `management/cf_exclusion/`, `transaction/inflight.rs`:
- **The mode** is the `collapse_runs` index repair (`RepairKind::CollapseRuns`), so it reuses the repairs' one bounded writer (`repair::cursor::BoundedWriter`, which gained `delete`), state record (`{t}\0{r}\0{b}\0repair_state\0collapse_runs\0{node}`), throttle, crash hook and the admin fan-out endpoint (`POST /api/management/{repo}/repairs/collapse_runs`, per-node status). Never at boot, never on the maintenance schedule.
