Gated by `RocksDBConfig::history_gc_collapse_runs` / server `[storage] history_gc_collapse_runs` / `RAISIN_HISTORY_GC_COLLAPSE_RUNS=1`, **default OFF** — `run_repair` refuses the kind while it is off (the blocking body `run_repair_blocking` is ungated; tests and the oracle use it).
- **Groups** are retention GC's (`history_gc/layout.rs`, the one table): PROPERTY_INDEX, REFERENCE_INDEX, ORDERED_CHILDREN, UNIQUE_INDEX and COMPOUND_INDEX (Phase 8 step 2 has landed). Walking each CF of a branch in key order sees a group's versions newest first; a version whose next-older version has the same state (live with byte-identical value, or `T` after `T`; `T` and the legacy `\0` compare alike) is deleted; the oldest of each run stays (`collapse/detector.rs`). Memory is one remembered version per group of the open chunks, capped at 100k groups per chunk with least-recently-seen eviction (`forgotten_groups`; forgetting only keeps) — no chunk buffering, so the 2M-entry skip of retention GC does not apply, and a hot value does not grow the heap without bound.
- **Editorial order** (review fix): ORDERED_CHILDREN readers order children that share a label (verbatim merge copies) by their visible entry's key position `(~rev, child)` (`ordering/paged.rs`, `paged_desc.rs`), so deleting a child's newer twin can move it past another child. There a newer twin is deleted only when the older twin is its IMMEDIATE successor in the chunk (`kept_interleaved` otherwise): then no other child's entry lies between them in key order, so no relative order changes at any read revision, whatever else is deleted.
- **Watermark** (`watermark.rs`): only versions strictly below it are deleted. Single node: `min(HEAD, now − collapse_min_age, collapse_watermark_cap, oldest in-flight transaction revision)`, `collapse_min_age` default 10 min. The in-flight clamp is a review fix: a transaction allocates its revision at its first write and may commit at any later time (`psql` `BEGIN`, no timeout, `check_conflicts` a no-op), landing tombstones between twins collapse already merged — and the commit-time `StagedDeltaCheck::revalidate` only re-asserts PROPERTY and COMPOUND successors, not REFERENCE / UNIQUE / ORDERED_CHILDREN. `transaction/inflight.rs` registers every allocated, uncommitted transaction revision per database (released on commit, rollback or drop) and `oldest_inflight_revision` clamps the watermark. **Cluster: REFUSED.** No trustworthy cluster-wide causal-stability watermark exists — a push `Ack` is logged and dropped (`raisin-replication/src/coordinator/push.rs`), `raisin_replication::PeerWatermarks` is an in-memory op-sequence map nothing on the server feeds — so `run_repair` sets `cluster_mode` from `RocksDBConfig::replicates()` and the run errors with the reason. `replicates()` is `replication_enabled || replication_peers || replication_configured`; the last (review fix) is set by the server whenever a cluster node id, a replication port, peers or `replication.enabled` is present, because the coordinator starts from a node id and port alone even with capture off (`scripts/start-cluster.sh` does exactly that) and applies peers' ops at their original revisions. Collapse on a replicating node waits for a persisted per-peer applied-HLC record (not built; it would need an ack that carries the applied HLC and a store for it).
- **Prerequisites** (`prereq.rs`), per `(branch, CF)`, from this node's repair state records (`status == "done"`): ORDERED_CHILDREN needs `ordered_children` (2.1) and `node_path` (Phase 10). PROPERTY / REFERENCE / UNIQUE / COMPOUND have no Phase 2 repair; their rebuilds only put states true at their version's revision or clear-and-re-derive, under the exclusion. A refused CF is reported (`refused: ["ordered_children: repair pending on this node: …"]`), the others still run, the branch is not `done`. **The checkpoint-ingest hook now resets those records to `queued`** (`repair::mark_repairs_pending`, called by `reenqueue_repairs_after_ingest`), so data imported from an unrepaired peer re-arms the refusal until the repairs ran again. **A branch copy does the same on its target** (review fix; `collapse::rearm_after_branch_copy`, called by `copy_branch_indexes_blocking` beside `invalidate_after_branch_copy`): a MERGE replays the source's ORDERED_CHILDREN history verbatim into a target whose records say `done`, so each required repair whose target record is `done` while the source's record for the same node id is not is reset to `queued` on the target.
- **Exclusion** (`cf_exclusion/`, process-local, keyed `(db path, tenant, repo, branch, CF)`): inserters hold it SHARED for their whole run — every inserting repair (`repair_branch`), the branch copy used by fork AND merge (`copy_branch_indexes_blocking`, per target CF), `rebuild_indexes` (every CF its index type clears), the compound build job (COMPOUND, across clear + re-derive). A collapse SLICE takes it EXCLUSIVELY with `try` (2 s patience), reads/decides/deletes one bounded batch and commits it with its cursor, and releases it before the throttle sleeps (`commit_prepared`). A busy CF stops the run resumable (`busy: [cf]`). A waiting inserter is preferred: no new slice starts while one waits. Every inserter acquisition bumps an epoch; a slice that sees a new epoch re-walks the cursor's chunk from its start instead of trusting its memory.
- **Retention GC vs collapse** (review fix): retention keeps the NEWEST version at or below its cutoff and deletes the older ones; collapse keeps the OLDEST of a run and deletes the newer twin. Interleaved, both commit and the group is empty. `run_history_gc_on_db` (maintenance loop, `POST …/history`, `compact_repository`) takes a per-DATABASE **pruner** hold (`cf_exclusion::enter_pruner`) for the whole run: it waits out a slice in progress, and no slice starts while it runs or waits (collapse reports `busy`). A pruner bumps the epoch too.
- **In-place writers** (review fix): a slice holds the WRITE side of the branch's `in_place_guard` stripe (`backfill_write_guard`) from its first read through its commit; `versionable=false` writers and the replicated in-place upsert take the read side around their batch write, so a key a slice doomed cannot be rewritten (to `T`, or to another value) before the slice deletes it. What a slice remembers from an EARLIER slice is re-read with a point get before it is deleted, and kept if its state changed (`skipped_changed`).
- **Streaming / resume:** a slice ends at 8 MB of deletes, `collapse_slice_keys` (100k) keys scanned or `collapse_slice_time` (250 ms), whichever comes first, and commits its cursor even with nothing deleted (review fix: before, a slice over redundancy-free data walked the whole CF holding the exclusive slot and a snapshot, with no cursor persisted). The throttle (32 MB/s) charges bytes READ as well as written (`BoundedWriter::note_read`). The cursor commits with the deletes. A resumed (or post-inserter) slice restarts at the start of the cursor's CHUNK and re-learns it from the survivors — two adjacent survivors of a group always differ, so re-walking deletes nothing new and a resumed run ends exactly where an uninterrupted one does (`collapse_resumes_after_crash` compares the two).
- **Disk:** before anything is deleted, each selected CF must have 2× its size free (`check_headroom_assuming`; `RepairOptions::free_bytes_override` is the test hook). The dry run deletes nothing, takes no exclusion, and reports per CF `scanned`, `deleted`, `bytes_reclaimable` (key + value) and `kept_above_watermark`; a real run reports the same numbers. Compaction is not triggered by collapse (see deviations).
- **Merge-base pin** (`pins.rs`, retention GC): every merge commit (`RevisionMeta.merge_parent` set) and its second parent are pinned on every branch of the repository, beside tags and fork points.

**Rollout.** (1) Ship with the flag off — nothing changes. (2) On one node at a time, at a quiet time with headroom: run the `ordered_children` and `node_path` repairs on every branch (fan-out endpoint), take the prod-snapshot dry run (`{"dry_run": true}`) and record it, then set the flag on that node and `POST …/repairs/collapse_runs`. (3) Compact the touched CFs outside the backup window (`compact_column_family`, admin); space returns after backup retention rotates. Clustered deployments: refused until the watermark exists. Rollback: unset the flag; what ran deleted only redundant versions.

**Tests (all green).**
- Review fixes (2026-10-04), each with a regression test, each verified failing with its fix disabled: `race_tests::{retention_gc_racing_a_collapse_slice_keeps_the_group, in_place_rewrite_of_a_doomed_key_is_not_deleted, collapse_slice_over_clean_data_releases_the_exclusion, collapse_reports_busy_while_retention_gc_runs}`, `watermark_tests::{late_commit_below_watermark_keeps_the_masking_entry, cluster_settings_without_replication_enabled_refuse_collapse}`, `prereq_tests::merge_from_unrepaired_branch_rearms_the_ordered_children_prerequisite`; unit `collapse::tests::{collapse_keeps_editorial_order_of_children_sharing_a_label, hot_chunk_memory_is_capped_and_forgetting_only_keeps, a_version_remembered_from_an_earlier_slice_is_re_read}`, `cf_exclusion::tests::{retention_gc_and_collapse_exclude_each_other, a_waiting_inserter_is_not_starved_by_the_next_slice}`, `transaction::inflight::tests::*`. Deferred: the merge-base pin still pins every merge commit forever and skips an undecodable `RevisionMeta` silently (`pins.rs`); narrowing it to the newest merge per live branch pair needs its own design and tests.
- raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/history_gc_collapse/` (a module directory next to `history_gc_test`, which is already 436 lines): `collapse_tests::{collapse_preserves_every_index_read_at_every_revision (raw newest-per-group decision of all five CFs at every recorded revision), collapse_idempotent, collapse_ignores_entries_above_watermark, collapse_dry_run_reports_bytes_and_deletes_nothing, collapse_resumes_after_crash, collapse_disk_precheck_refuses_without_headroom, collapse_refuses_in_cluster_mode_and_when_disabled, collapse_waits_out_an_inserter_then_reports_busy}`; `prereq_tests::{collapse_refuses_while_repair_pending (incl. re-armed by mark_repairs_pending), repair_then_collapse_equals_collapse_then_repair}`; `gc_tests::{gc_keeps_reordered_child_hidden_in_old_group, gc_keeps_merge_base_after_second_merge}`; `gc_copy_tests::{gc_then_cross_branch_copy_at_pinned_revision, gc_then_restore_to_retained_revision, gc_on_fork_independent}`. Each GC test runs retention GC and collapse. Verified not vacuous: with the merge pins removed `gc_keeps_merge_base_after_second_merge` fails (the base reads `v0`), and with the prerequisite gate removed `repair_then_collapse_equals_collapse_then_repair` fails exactly on the plan's hazard (the stale re-created entry vanishes at its own revision).
- raisin-rocksdb unit: `history_gc::collapse::tests::*` (run detection: oldest kept, state change ends a run, `T` after `T`, byte-different values, interleaved nodes, watermark strictly-below, ORDERED_CHILDREN label groups), `collapse::watermark::tests::*`, `cf_exclusion::tests::*`.
- MVCC oracle **stage 2b** `mvcc_index_oracle::stages::oracle_collapse_runs` (`collapse.rs`): drive, optionally retention GC with a tag pin, the prerequisites, collapse on every branch at HEAD, then every template at every retained revision. Green at `PROPTEST_CASES=48` with `index.skip_unchanged` off and on (60–700 versions deleted per history).
- Suites: raisin-rocksdb `--lib` 1318 and `--test all` 571 green (after the review fixes); oracle `oracle_collapse_runs` green; raisin-sql-execution `--test all` only the 8 known failures (last full run before the review fixes).

**Deviations.**
- Implemented as an index-repair kind rather than an option of `run_history_gc`, so it gets the streaming writer, resumable cursor, per-node state and the fan-out endpoint the plan requires without a second copy of them; the GC groups are still `history_gc/layout.rs`'s.
- Cluster mode refuses (above); nothing is collapsed on a replicating node.
- Collapse does not compact. `compact_column_family` compacts a whole CF in one call, which is not the "bounded slices with a rate limiter" the discipline asks for; a key-range sliced compaction is not built. Space returns through RocksDB's own compactions or an admin compaction outside the backup window.
- PROPERTY / REFERENCE / UNIQUE / COMPOUND have no repair prerequisite (reasoned in `prereq.rs`); the Phase 2 REFERENCE REBUILD has no per-node state record to gate on, and it clears and re-derives anyway.
- `versionable=false` in-place writes overwrite exactly the keys collapse reads (`T@R` over a remembered `v@R`), so "absolute-state writes collapse never contradicts" was false under concurrency: a slice deleting a key rewritten after its read restored the older twin at HEAD. Closed by the in-place guard and the cross-slice re-read above (review fix); it holds for the `max(R, newest)` landing with `index.skip_unchanged` on too, since those writers take the same read side.
- The exclusion is process-local (one process owns a database).
- The prod-snapshot dry run (Operational test gates) is still owed before enabling it in production.

**Prerequisites (hard).**
- **Every Phase 2 repair for that CF and branch has completed**, recorded in the per-node repair state record; collapse refuses to run on a branch where a repair is pending. The Phase 10 backfill likewise, for NODE_PATH-dependent groups.
- **A cluster-wide causal-stability watermark** exists: the minimum HLC every peer has applied and acknowledged.
- Phase 7 item 6 (`get_last_order_label` rewrite) before ORDERED_CHILDREN is collapsed.
- Phase 8 step 2 before COMPOUND is collapsed.
- Prod-snapshot dry run (Operational test gates).

**Why the earlier "lossless; pins irrelevant" claim was wrong.** Collapse assumes nothing will ever be inserted below existing entries. Out-of-order replication, the Phase 2 repairs (tombstones at historical delete revisions) and the Phase 12 rebuild all do exactly that. Concrete case: a child is deleted at `rdel` with a mis-keyed (missing) tombstone, then re-created at r3 with the same label and name. Collapse sees live(r1), live(r3) as identical and deletes r3; the repair then inserts T(rdel), and the re-created child disappears at HEAD. A collapse decision is also invalidated by any concurrent historical-revision insert between its read and its delete.

**Changes.**
- New history_gc mode `collapse_runs` (`management/history_gc/{mod,layout}.rs`). Within each existing GC group (PROPERTY_INDEX `(tag, name, value, node)`, and the analogous groups for REFERENCE, ORDERED_CHILDREN and UNIQUE), walk from newest to oldest. Delete a version whose next-older version has an identical state (live with the same bytes, or `T` after `T`), keeping the **oldest** entry of each run.
- **Only entries strictly below the causal-stability watermark** are eligible, never "per node at will".
- Takes a **per-(branch, CF) exclusion** against repair, rebuild and merge copy for the duration of each slice.
- Streams per value prefix, keeping a per-node "last state" map (capped per chunk, least recently seen forgotten), so hot values over 2M entries are no longer skipped. Bounded batches, resumable cursor, rate limiter, per the repair discipline.
- **Disk:** RocksDB deletes add markers, so disk grows until compaction. Free-space precheck of at least 2× the CF size; compaction by key range in bounded slices with a rate limiter, outside the backup window. Reclaimed space only appears after backup retention rotates (checkpoints hard-link SSTs). The dry run reports the bytes it expects to reclaim.
- **Merge-base pin:** pin merge revisions in `history_gc/mod.rs:368-376` (the divergence base can be a merge revision; see invariants dossier). This applies to retention GC.

**Migration.** Admin job, fanned out per node, run per branch. Excludes COMPOUND until Phase 8 step 2.

**Replication.** Local only; derived data. The watermark is the replication-facing part.

**Checkpoint.** A checkpoint from an uncollapsed peer re-imports redundant entries; harmless (a superset), collapsed again on the next run.

**Fork.** Shrinks the cost of future forks. Forks copied earlier hold their own copies.

**Tests to add** (in `history_gc_test`):
- `collapse_preserves_every_index_read_at_every_revision` (oracle stage 2);
- `repair_then_collapse_equals_collapse_then_repair`;
- `collapse_refuses_while_repair_pending`;
- `collapse_ignores_entries_above_watermark`;
- `gc_keeps_reordered_child_hidden_in_old_group`;
- `gc_keeps_merge_base_after_second_merge`;
- `gc_then_cross_branch_copy_at_pinned_revision`;
- `gc_then_restore_to_retained_revision`;
- `gc_on_fork_independent`;
- `collapse_idempotent`;
- `collapse_disk_precheck_refuses_without_headroom`.

**Rollback.** Disable the mode in config. Entries deleted below the watermark after all repairs were redundant.

**Expected gain.** Proportional to past edit counts. Expect PROPERTY_INDEX size to drop by roughly the average number of edits per node, for unchanged properties. Measured on a production snapshot copy with the dry run; the space shows up only after backup retention rotates.

**Risk.** Medium-high. It deletes data. Mitigated by the watermark, the repair dependency, the exclusion, the dry run and oracle stage 2.

## Phase 10 — Transaction write path writes NODE_PATH + StorageNode through one writer — DONE (uncommitted)

**Prerequisites.** Phase 2.8 (decode-correct baseline reader). **Lands before or together with Phase 4.** The read-path half (item 1) was to ship **one release before** the write-path half — superseded by Phase 10b (owner decision: no release split, no gate).

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-03).** Both halves are in the tree. *Phase 10b then removed the gate described below: the one format is now the only one written, by every writer.*
- **Read rule (release N, always on).** `mvcc_read/node_path.rs`: `materialize_path(…, read_at, embedded: Option<EmbeddedPath>)` takes the newer, by revision, of NODE_PATH newest ≤ `read_at` and the blob's embedded path at its blob revision (`embedded_path_wins`, the rule in one function; `current_path` is its one blob-less form, behind `ordering::node_path_at` and the PATH_INDEX repair). **A disagreeing tie is settled by PATH_INDEX**, not by either source: the embedded path wins exactly when PATH_INDEX maps it to the node AT that revision. (Review fix — the first cut gave every tie to the blob, claiming every writer of both at one revision writes one path. Two cases break that both ways: `put_node(c)` then an ancestor `move_node_tree` in ONE transaction leaves a legacy blob at R with the pre-move path beside the move's NODE_PATH at R — any older database already holds this shape, and "blob wins" read it stale where the old "NODE_PATH wins" read it right; and a legacy in-place rename of a `versionable=false` node over a repository NODE_PATH at the reused revision needs the blob. In both the last writer at R wrote PATH_INDEX for its path at R and tombstoned the other, so PATH_INDEX is the arbiter. Only a disagreeing tie pays the extra point read.) `StorageNode` gained `embedded_path` (`#[serde(rename = "path", default, skip_serializing)]`, last field): a legacy full-`Node` blob is map-encoded and already decoded as a `StorageNode`, which is exactly how its path used to be dropped — now it survives the decode and nothing new is ever written. `mvcc_read/node_decode.rs` is the ONE decoder (`deserialize_node_with_path[_as]`, now taking `read_at` AND `blob_revision`); the repository's `path_materialization.rs` was a mirrored second body of it and is now a delegate. The positional legacy-`Node` fallback answers to the rule too. Every caller passes the blob's revision (repository get/get-at-revision/list/scans/bulk descendants/path prefix/history/time travel, transaction `get_node`, replication baseline + newer-version, merge `superseded`, conflict listing, ORDERED_CHILDREN repair).
- **Readers that bypassed the decoder, now routed through it** (each previously returned `""` for a repository-written node and, once the writer is on, would have for transaction-written ones too): the index-rebuild scan (`async_indexing/helpers.rs`), the compound build job's scan (`jobs/handlers/compound_index.rs`, which feeds `__parent_path`), branch diff (`branches/diff.rs`), revision changed-nodes (`revisions/trait_impl.rs`), the light path reader `ordering::node_path_at` (was "NODE_PATH first", now the rule, decoding the blob only when it is newer than the entry), and replication's legacy `SetProperty`/`MoveNode` handlers (current-node read via `mvcc_read::decode_entry_with_path`; parent path via `node_path_at`). Not converted, because they never read `path`: backup export, `storage/deltas.rs`, graph projection, the spatial job, lazy property indexing.
- **Writer (release N+1, gated — gate REMOVED in Phase 10b; the format argument and `LegacyFullNode` are gone).** `crud/indexing/node_record.rs::write_node_record(db, batch, …, node, parent_id, revision, format)` is the ONE node-record writer (NODES blob, plus NODE_PATH in the `Storage` format). Callers: the repository (`add_node_indexes_to_batch_with_parent_id`), the batch path behind copy/promote/deep-create (`add_node_to_batch_with_parent_id`), and the transaction path — `create/storage.rs::write_node_to_batch` (used by `put_node` and `add_node`, which now pass the looked-up parent id; `"/"` is stored as `None`) and `move_tree.rs`'s rewritten records. The transaction path's format comes from `NodeRepositoryImpl::tx_node_record_format()`: `NodeRecordFormat::Storage` when the gate is on, `NodeRecordFormat::LegacyFullNode` (byte-for-byte the pre-Phase-10 `put_node` blob, no NODE_PATH) when off.
- **The gate, and the rollout order (SUPERSEDED by Phase 10b: the env var, the setter and the release split no longer exist).** `RAISIN_TX_NODE_RECORD_WRITER` (`1`/`true`/`on`/`yes`; default OFF), read when the node repository is built; `NodeRepositoryImpl::set_tx_node_record_writer(bool)` (reachable as `storage.nodes_impl()`) flips it per storage at runtime (tests use it to write "old binary" bytes). Rollout: **release N** ships with the gate off — the read rule, every converted reader and the backfill are live; run the backfill (admin, fanned out). **Release N+1** sets the env var on every node (a later release may make it the default). Rolling back N+1 → N is safe (N's rule prefers the newer embedded path the old writer writes); **downgrading below N after any node ran with the gate on is unsupported** (stale NODE_PATH would win again in a pre-rule binary).
- **One transaction, write and move (review fix).** `put_node` and `move_node_tree` share revision R, and the move lists the subtree from COMMITTED state. Write-then-move: the move re-stages, through `write_node_record` and from the STAGED node, every node this transaction already wrote or moved (`transaction/context/nodes/move_view.rs::transaction_view`), so a blob and a NODE_PATH entry at R never name different paths and the write's own changes survive; a node the transaction deleted is not moved (it would write a live path over its tombstone). Move-then-write: `record_moves` leaves every moved node, with its new path, where the transaction's `get_node` finds it — staged nodes in `ReadCache.nodes`, committed ones in the new `ReadCache.moved_nodes`, which (unlike `nodes`) still passes RLS — so a later `put_node` carries the post-move path instead of storing the old one back at R. Rewritten records now carry their real `parent_id` (target parent for the root, the pre-order listing's parent below it).
- **Backfill** `management/async_indexing/repair/node_path_backfill.rs`, `RepairKind::NodePath` (slug `node_path`): streams the branch's NODES, groups each node's versions, and — oldest first, against committed NODE_PATH plus the entries it has queued for that node — writes NODE_PATH at every legacy full-blob revision whose embedded path NODE_PATH ≤ that revision does not already answer. Same discipline as the Phase 2 repairs: `BoundedWriter` batches with the per-node state record and resumable cursor (checkpointed per node), rate limit, disk precheck on NODE_PATH, dry run, admin endpoint `POST /api/management/{repo}/repairs/node_path` with peer fan-out (the existing handler accepts the new slug), and re-enqueued by the checkpoint-ingest hook (`reenqueue_repairs_after_ingest`). `RepairReport.node_path` reports versions / legacy versions / written / conflicts / changed_during_scan. **It never overwrites an entry at the blob's own revision** (review fix): an entry at exactly R naming another path (or a tombstone) is a second writer at R, which the read rule's tie-break settles; overwriting it with the blob's path destroyed the only record of a moved path, automatically, from the ingest hook. Such a version is counted in `conflicts` and logged. And since the scan reads blobs through a long-lived iterator (an implicit snapshot), each blob is re-read by point lookup right before its entry is staged; one rewritten in place since (`versionable=false`) is counted in `changed_during_scan` and skipped. `parse_node_key` moved to `async_indexing/node_key_parse.rs`, shared with the PATH_INDEX repair.
- **PATH_INDEX repair converted (review fix).** `async_indexing/path_repair.rs::repair_path_index` took the newest NODE_PATH entry per node as the path, so while legacy blobs exist it restored a renamed node at its OLD path (a phantom), skipped transaction-created nodes (no entry) and treated legacy recreate-after-delete as deleted. It now walks NODES (every node with a record), skips a tombstoned newest record, decides the path with `mvcc_read::current_path` and writes PATH_INDEX at the winning record's own revision. Residue: a disagreeing same-revision tie is arbitrated by PATH_INDEX, which the clear destroyed, so such a node is restored at its NODE_PATH path. `repair/branches.rs` took `list_branches`/`branch_head` out of `repair/mod.rs` (line budget).

**Tests (all green).** raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/node_path_writer_test.rs`: `repo_created_then_put_node_rename_reads_new_path` (HEAD, r2, r1, `get_by_path`, with the old writer and again with the new), `put_node_writes_node_path` (and that the old writer writes none), `downgrade_rename_through_old_put_node_reads_new_path` (new writer creates and renames, the old writer renames again, read back at every revision, then the new writer once more), `node_path_backfill_writes_at_each_divergent_revision` (dry run counts, writes at r2 and r3 but not r4 whose path r3's entry already answers, NODE_PATH alone then answers every revision, second run writes 0, state `done`), `node_path_backfill_resumes_after_crash` (crash hook after 4 batches, resume from the cursor without rescanning, totals equal an uninterrupted run, re-run writes 0). Unit: `mvcc_read::path_rule_tests` (the rule both ways, a StorageNode blob, the legacy full-`Node` blob round trip through every decoder, and the three disagreeing ties: `put_then_ancestor_move_in_one_tx_tie_reads_the_moved_path`, `versionable_false_in_place_rename_tie_reads_the_blob_path`, `recreate_after_delete_in_one_tx_tie_reads_the_blob_path`), `async_indexing::node_key_parse::tests::parses_node_keys_and_nothing_else`. Review regressions, raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/node_path_tx_move_test.rs`: `put_then_ancestor_move_in_one_tx_reads_moved_path` and `ancestor_move_then_put_in_one_tx_keeps_moved_path` (each with the gate off and on: by id at HEAD and at R — what the replication capture reads — by path, in `get_descendants_bulk`, the write's property survives, blob and NODE_PATH at R agree, backfill reports no conflict), `node_path_backfill_never_overwrites_a_same_revision_entry` (the pre-fix shape written raw: read rule answers the moved path, backfill counts one conflict, writes nothing), `repair_path_index_restores_paths_by_the_read_rule` (renamed legacy node restored at its new path with no phantom, transaction-created node restored). Regression runs: raisin-rocksdb lib + `tests/all`, and raisin-sql-execution `tests/all` including the MVCC oracle — each with the gate OFF and again with `RAISIN_TX_NODE_RECORD_WRITER=1`; sql-execution shows only the 8 known failures in both modes.

**Deviations.**
- The gate is an env var plus a runtime setter, not a `RocksDBConfig` field (no config-file plumbing through `raisin-server`).
- Phase 4's `batch_get` did not exist yet; when it lands it must decode through `mvcc_read::deserialize_node_with_path_as` (the item-1 rule comes with it for free). (Resolved in Phase 4: it decodes through `deserialize_node_with_path_in`, the same body over a snapshot-pinned read source.)
- `write_node_record` owns NODES + NODE_PATH only. PATH_INDEX stays with each caller (what a write tombstones differs: a move vacates its old path), and so does the virtual-mount registry call (the batch/copy path never recorded mounts; changing that is out of scope).
- The "downgrade" test reproduces the old binary with the gate OFF, which writes byte-identical legacy blobs; the release-level `downgrade_open_previous_release` gate still stands.
- `StorageNode.parent_id` from the transaction `move_node_tree` is now the real parent id (no reader depends on it yet; the oracle reads it).
- **Known exceptions to the one record writer (RESOLVED in Phase 10b — all of them now write through `write_node_record`; the `node_operations/` copies were dead code and are deleted).** Branch merge apply (`branches/merge/apply.rs`) and every replication applicator (`applicator/{crdt_ops, move_node_ops, legacy_node_ops}.rs`, `node_operations/{create_node, move_rename, set_property}.rs`) still hand-roll the NODES blob as a full `Node` (and, where they write it, NODE_PATH). The gate and `NodeRecordFormat` do not reach them, so merged and replicated nodes stay legacy-format and the backfill keeps classifying them as legacy. Routing them through `write_node_record` is owed: `LegacyFullNode` on replicas until every peer runs release N, then the gated format. Any record-format change (a new `StorageNode` field, Phase 12 record hooks) must touch these sites until then.
- **Owed (LOW): legacy `SetProperty` / `MoveNode` apply (FIXED in Phase 10b: `write_node_record_keeping_path`).** `node_operations/set_property.rs` and `applicator/legacy_node_ops.rs` write a full `Node` at the op's NEW revision embedding the LOCAL current path, with no NODE_PATH. Under the read rule a newer blob beats an older entry, so an ancestor move at a LOWER revision that arrives after such an op (out of order, from an old-binary peer) loses to the stale embedded path. Not fixed here: writing a path-less `StorageNode` instead would make a node with no NODE_PATH entry (one only the legacy writer ever wrote) unreadable, so the fix needs a "no path asserted" record variant or a NODE_PATH existence check; a property-only op should never assert a path.
- Seen once each, in 2 of 5 full gate-off runs of raisin-rocksdb `tests/all`: `integration_tests::delete_operations::test_delete_node_with_incoming_relations_fails` and `integration_tests::tag_repository::test_tag_creation_copies_indexes` (both repository-only paths, untouched beyond the byte-identical record-writer refactor); both pass in isolation (15/15) and in the gate-on full run. Treated as a load flake, not investigated.
- Still owed: the prod-snapshot dry run of the backfill (operational gate).

**Goal.** The SQL/WS write path (`put_node`/`add_node`) stores a full Node blob with its path (`create/storage.rs:54`) and never writes NODE_PATH, so every read of such a node decodes twice. A node created via the repository (NODE_PATH p1 at r1) and then renamed via put_node (blob path p2 at r2) reads back p1, because `deserialize_node_with_path` parses the full Node as StorageNode and NODE_PATH wins (`path_materialization.rs:138-150`).

**Changes.**
1. **Read rule first (release N).** `materialize_path` and Phase 4's `batch_get` take the newer of (NODE_PATH newest ≤ snapshot) and (the embedded blob path at the blob's revision), by revision. This fixes the stale path immediately and makes a later downgrade safe: a downgraded binary's put_node writes full-Node blobs and no NODE_PATH, and this rule still reads the blob's path.
2. Write the test first: `repo_created_then_put_node_rename_reads_new_path` (at HEAD and at r2).
3. **Writer (release N+1).** Route both layers through one `write_node_record(batch, …)` (the existing `crud/indexing/mod.rs:80-98`), placed next to `write_path_index` at `put_node.rs:393`.
4. **Backfill** (streaming, admin-triggered, fanned out): for every full-blob revision whose embedded path differs from NODE_PATH newest ≤ that revision, write NODE_PATH **at that revision**. The earlier "nodes with no NODE_PATH entry, at their latest revision, no history rewritten" scope missed exactly the affected population and left time travel to r2 wrong forever. Idempotent and data-detected.

**Files.** `transaction/context/nodes/create/{storage.rs, core/put_node.rs, core/add_node.rs}`, `crud/indexing/mod.rs`, `path_materialization.rs`, `crud/read/batch_get.rs`, new `management/async_indexing/repair/node_path_backfill.rs`.

**Migration.** Backfill as above. The reader keeps its legacy full-Node fallback forever.

**Replication.** Origin now matches replicas, which already write NODE_PATH. Checkpoint: NODE_PATH arrives from the peer; the read rule tolerates a missing or older entry, and the backfill is data-detected and re-run from the ingest hook.

**Tests to add.** The stale-path test (HEAD and r2), `put_node_writes_node_path`, a round-trip of a legacy full-Node blob, `node_path_backfill_writes_at_each_divergent_revision`, and **`downgrade_rename_through_old_put_node_reads_new_path`** (open with the old writer after the new one has run, rename through the old put_node, read back).

**Rollback.** (As planned:) the writer (release N+1) can be reverted to release N safely because release N's read rule prefers the newer embedded path. Downgrading below release N after the writer has run is unsupported (stale NODE_PATH would win again). **Phase 10b:** with no release split, downgrading below the release that ships 10b is unsupported.

**Expected gain.** One fewer full decode per read of every SQL/WS-written node.

**Risk.** Medium (write path).

## Phase 10b — One node record format everywhere: gate removed, merge + replication through `write_node_record` — DONE (uncommitted)

**Owner decision (2026-10-03).** No production deployment needs a two-release rolling upgrade for the node record format, so the release N / N+1 split of Phase 10 is dropped: the new format is written everywhere at once. This overrides the "gate the later step behind a default-OFF flag" rule **for the node record format only**; other phases keep their flags. **Downgrading below the release that ships 10b is unsupported** (a binary older than the Phase 10 read rule would let a stale NODE_PATH win over the paths this one no longer embeds).

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-03).**
- **Gate removed.** `RAISIN_TX_NODE_RECORD_WRITER`, `tx_writer_enabled_from_env`, `NodeRepositoryImpl::{set_tx_node_record_writer, tx_node_record_format}`, `NodeRecordFormat` and the `LegacyFullNode` WRITE format are gone. `put_node`/`add_node` (`create/storage.rs`) and the transactional `move_node_tree` always store a `StorageNode` + NODE_PATH through `write_node_record`. The read rule (`mvcc_read::materialize_path`), the legacy decode and the backfill stay forever for legacy data already on disk.
- **One writer, every funnel** (`crud/indexing/node_record.rs`, re-exported as `repositories::nodes::{write_node_record, write_node_record_keeping_path, parent_id_of}`): branch merge apply (`branches/merge/apply.rs`, parent id from the placement), the replicated upsert (`applicator/crdt_ops.rs`), the legacy `CreateNode` (`applicator/legacy_node_ops.rs`, which used to write NO NODE_PATH at all), the legacy `SetProperty` and `MoveNode` (below), and backup import (`management/backup/import.rs`, which stored full `Node` blobs with no NODE_PATH). `node_operations/{create_node, delete_node, move_rename, set_property, snapshot_ops}.rs` were a second, never-dispatched copy of the applicator handlers (the dispatcher calls the `OperationApplicator` methods; the module was `#![allow(dead_code)]`) that had already drifted — deleted rather than routed, so there is nothing left to drift. `node_operations` keeps only the shared event helpers.
- **Audit: no production code outside `write_node_record` writes a live NODES blob.** Checked every file that names `cf::NODES` or serializes a node (`to_vec_named`/`StorageNode::from_node`): the writer itself; tombstones only — `tombstones/core_tombstones.rs`, `branches/merge/deletion.rs`, `queries/copy/cross_branch/prune.rs`; verbatim copies of EXISTING records (no new record is formed) — `branches/copy.rs` + `copy_existing.rs` (fork/merge copy), `tags.rs` (tag snapshot), `replication/integration/checkpoint_ingestor.rs` (peer checkpoint), and `format_migration/json_to_msgpack.rs` (the one-time, marker-gated re-encoding of JSON-era blobs); deletes only — `history_gc`, `storage/tenant_wipe.rs`, `storage/repo_purge.rs`; reads only — the scans, repairs, rebuild jobs, `lazy_indexing.rs`, `relation_consistency.rs`, `jobs/handlers/{snapshot, spatial_index, compound_index}.rs` (snapshot writes REVISIONS, not NODES), `revisions/trait_impl.rs` (REVISIONS); test-only — `config/tests.rs`, `mvcc_read/path_rule_tests.rs`. The oracle now asserts it on every funnel it drives (new template `one_record_format`: no stored blob embeds a path).
- **Legacy `SetProperty` / `MoveNode` apply (the Phase 10 owed item).** *(Phase 11d removed both ops and `write_node_record_keeping_path`; records it stored stay readable by the read rule.)* A property-only op never asserts a path: `write_node_record_keeping_path` stores the path-less blob and **anchors** the node's current path (by the read rule, `mvcc_read::current_path` at the op's revision) in NODE_PATH at the revision of the record that DECIDED it, when the entry there does not already name it — exactly the entry the backfill would write, never one at the op's revision. So a node only legacy writers ever wrote (full blob, no NODE_PATH entry) stays readable, and an out-of-order ancestor move at a LOWER revision wins on its own (its NODE_PATH entry is newer than the anchored one). With nothing live at or below the revision (unknown or deleted node) it asserts the node's own path as `write_node_record` would. Legacy `MoveNode` asserts the path when it changes and is content-only when it only reorders; it is now ONE batch (it was five separate writes), tombstones with `T` (it wrote empty values PATH_INDEX/ORDERED_CHILDREN readers do not recognise as tombstones), and keys the old ORDERED_CHILDREN tombstone by the parent's ID and stored label (it used `node.parent`, the parent's NAME). Legacy `SetProperty` now marks the workspace's compound indexes stale like every other replica write that writes no compound entries.
- **Out-of-order apply: the newer version's path includes the staged entry** (`applicator/newer_version.rs::load_node_after`). When an older upsert arrives beneath a path-less newer record, the newer version's path is NODE_PATH newest ≤ its revision — and when that entry is not above the upsert's revision, it is the entry the upsert is staging in the same batch, invisible to a committed read. Read from committed state alone it was the pre-move path, so the out-of-order ancestor move tombstoned its own new PATH_INDEX entry at the newer revision (the node vanished from `get_by_path`).
- **Automatic backfill** (`management/async_indexing/repair/auto_node_path.rs`). After the job system starts (`init_job_system`, spawned, `AUTO_BACKFILL_DELAY` = 30 s, never on the boot path), the branches whose `node_path` state record on this node is not `done` are backfilled by ordinary `IndexRepair { repair: "node_path" }` jobs on the unified queue — the same streaming, bounded-batch, resumable, disk-prechecked, rate-limited run as the admin endpoint (`run_repair` with default `RepairOptions`) — **one branch at a time, as a chain** (review fix below): the start queues ONE job, marked `AUTO_CHAIN_META` in its job context and registered with no retries; when it finishes, succeeded or not, the maintenance handler calls `continue_node_path_backfill_chain`, which queues the next pending branch strictly after it in `(tenant, repo, branch)` order. A start while a link is live queues nothing; a branch with its own live (admin) `node_path` job is skipped; a branch that fails is retried at the next start, not in a loop. `RAISIN_NODE_PATH_AUTO_BACKFILL=0|false|off|no` disables the chain; the admin endpoint and the checkpoint-ingest hook are unchanged. `repair_node_id` (the state-record node id) is now one function shared with `run_repair`.


**Review fixes (2026-10-03, two independent reviewers).**
- **Backfill vs in-place writers** (MEDIUM). The backfill decided `NODE_PATH(R) := /a` for a legacy blob at R at staging time but wrote it only when its batch filled, many nodes later; a `versionable=false` rename (or a replicated in-place upsert) landing in that window stored a path-less blob and `NODE_PATH(R) = /b` at the SAME R, which the backfill then overwrote with the stale `/a` — permanently, as the blob was no longer legacy. Now entries are held in `repair/node_path_stage.rs` and re-checked at commit (blob at R still the legacy blob embedding that path, `NODE_PATH` at R still empty — one multi-get per CF) under the WRITE side of a new process-wide, branch-striped guard (`repositories/nodes/crud/indexing/in_place_guard.rs`). Every in-place writer holds the READ side around its batch write: the transaction commit when a `put_node` reused its node's revision (`TransactionMetadata::in_place_record_write`), the repository `update_impl` (`write_batch_with_head_as(.., in_place)`), and the replicated upsert when a version is already stored at its revision (`crdt_ops.rs`). Ordinary writes, at fresh revisions, never take it. `BoundedWriter` gained `commit_prepared` (the guard is held through the write, released before the throttle sleeps) and a test-only `RepairOptions::before_commit` hook. Test: `node_path_backfill_race_test::in_place_rename_between_backfill_stage_and_commit_keeps_new_path`.
- **Backup export lost every one-format path** (MEDIUM, both reviewers). `export_all_repository_nodes` decoded each `NODES` blob raw as a `Node`; `Node.path` is `#[serde(default)]`, so a path-less `StorageNode` exported `"path": ""`, and the restore wrote `""` into `NODE_PATH` and no PATH_INDEX entry. The export now takes, per branch, the version of each node visible at the branch HEAD (deleted nodes skipped; it used to export every version, and the import kept the OLDEST) and decodes it through `mvcc_read::deserialize_node_with_path`, stamping the workspace from the key. The import refuses the whole file, before writing, when any node has an empty path, and writes through the batch funnel (`add_node_to_batch_with_parent_id`: record + PATH_INDEX + property/reference/relation/spatial entries) into the node's own workspace. Still pre-existing and unchanged: the backup records no branch, so every branch's nodes restore into `main` at revision 0, with no ORDERED_CHILDREN and no parent id in the blob. Tests: `backup_round_trip_test::{backup_round_trip_of_one_format_records_keeps_paths, restore_refuses_nodes_without_a_path}`.
- **Automatic backfill was unbounded** (MEDIUM). It queued one job per pending branch at once — every branch on a first boot — filling the shared background pool with full-branch NODES scans (the write throttle is per run; the reads are not throttled), so live writes' fulltext/embedding jobs waited behind them. Now the one-at-a-time chain described above. Test: `node_path_auto_backfill_test::first_boot_queues_one_backfill_and_chains_branch_by_branch`. Not done (optional in the finding): a fork inheriting `done` from its source.
- **Legacy `MoveNode` out of order** (LOW) — *moot: the op is removed by Phase 11d.* Deferred, not trivial: resolving the old path/parent/label as of the op's revision is easy, but the matching `tombstone_superseded_by_newer` step for the new PATH_INDEX / ORDERED_CHILDREN entries is a real change to a handler only pre-replication-v2 peers reach.
- **Manual-version snapshot decode** (LOW) — not reachable: `NodeService::list_versions` filters on a `TreeCommitMeta` read from `keys::k_commit_meta`, which nothing in the codebase writes, so on RocksDB it returns no versions at all and the raw `rmp_serde::from_slice::<Node>` of the snapshot never runs. Manual versions on RocksDB are a pre-existing, separate gap.

**Tests.**
- raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/node_path_writer_test.rs` and `node_path_tx_move_test.rs`: legacy bytes are now produced by a TEST-ONLY raw rewrite (`legacy_record` / `legacy_tx_put`: the committed record rewritten as the full `Node` blob, its NODE_PATH entry at that revision deleted — byte-for-byte what a pre-Phase-10 `put_node` left), never by a production switch. `put_node_writes_node_path` also asserts that a legacy node's next write goes through the one writer; the two in-transaction move tests run once (there is no gate to loop over) and assert a path-less blob at R; `downgrade_rename_through_old_put_node_reads_new_path` now models legacy bytes landing above one-format records (a checkpoint from an older peer).
- New `tests/all/node_record_replication_test.rs`: `legacy_set_property_then_out_of_order_ancestor_move_reads_moved_path` and `legacy_reorder_then_out_of_order_ancestor_move_reads_moved_path` (each in order AND out of order: path at HEAD, at the op's revision, at the move's, at the create's; `get_by_path` of the new and old paths; the op's record is path-less with no NODE_PATH entry at its revision), `legacy_set_property_on_a_legacy_only_node_stays_readable` (a raw legacy blob with no NODE_PATH entry; after `SetProperty` it reads at HEAD and before, and its path is anchored at the legacy revision), `replicated_records_are_the_one_format` (replicated upsert and legacy `CreateNode`).
- New `tests/all/node_path_auto_backfill_test.rs`: `pending_branches_get_the_node_path_backfill_queued_once` (pending → one job → no second job while it is live → after the run the branch is `done` and no longer pending) and `first_boot_queues_one_backfill_and_chains_branch_by_branch`.
- MVCC oracle: new template `one_record_format` in `checks/nodes_cf.rs`.

**Measured: the mixed format is NOT what makes path reads slow** (`raisin-sql-execution` `tests/all/index_read_bench_paths.rs`, scenario E; debug build — relative numbers only; 311 nodes — `/root`, 10 sections, 30 pages each — written through SQL, i.e. the transaction path; median of 50 runs; ops = RocksDB `PerfContext` counts of ONE run on the calling thread). `legacy` = every record rewritten raw into the pre-Phase-10 transaction format (full `Node`, no NODE_PATH); `legacy_backfilled` = that, after the `node_path` backfill.

| read | one format | legacy | legacy + backfill | ops (one format) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `get_by_path('/root/s5/p17')` | 29 µs | 31 µs | 31 µs | 1 get, 4 seeks, 1 next |
| `get` by id (control) | 25 µs | 27 µs | 27 µs | 1 get, 3 seeks |
| `SELECT id … WHERE path = …` (PathIndexScan) | 200 µs | 203 µs | 201 µs | 1 get, 4 seeks |
| `CHILD_OF('/root/s5')`, 30 rows | 839 µs | 877 µs | 880 µs | 68 seeks, 39 nexts |
| `DESCENDANT_OF('/root')`, 310 rows | 8.0 ms | 8.3 ms | 8.3 ms | 950 seeks, 720 nexts |

The legacy format costs 2–5 %, not a multiple: a legacy blob is map-encoded and decodes as a `StorageNode` in ONE pass (its `path` key lands in `embedded_path`), so there is no double decode in practice, and the read rule's extra PATH_INDEX lookup is paid only on a disagreeing same-revision tie (none here). The legacy rows' extra `nexts`/`keys_skipped` are partly an artifact of the bench's raw rewrite (it deletes NODE_PATH entries, leaving delete markers a real legacy database never had). No extra point reads, PATH_INDEX misses or whole-prefix scans were found: `WHERE path =` is a PathIndexScan; `CHILD_OF` takes the direct-children path (`list_by_parent_page`, ~2.3 seeks per child, despite EXPLAIN printing `PrefixScan: prefix=/root/s5/`); `DESCENDANT_OF` is the ordered ORDERED_CHILDREN traversal at ~3 seeks per row (child entry, newest blob, NODE_PATH). What the numbers DO show: a SQL point lookup by path costs ~7× the storage call it wraps (200 µs vs 29 µs) — the parse/plan/execute overhead Phase 14 (harness J) exists to measure — and tree listings are per-row point reads (Phase 4's batched snapshot reads). If the owner's database is slow on path queries, the suspects are those two and history depth (superseded revisions show up as `keys_skipped`), not the record format. Still owed: the same bench on a copy of the real database (the operational dry run), in a release build.

**Deviations.**
- `node_operations/{create_node, delete_node, move_rename, set_property, snapshot_ops}.rs` were deleted, not routed through the writer: they were never dispatched, and routing a dead copy would keep a mirror alive.
- The automatic backfill is an exception to D11 (admin-triggered only), by owner decision; it is per node, one branch at a time (a job chain), delayed after boot, and switchable off (`RAISIN_NODE_PATH_AUTO_BACKFILL`).
- The legacy `MoveNode` handler also had its tombstones fixed (empty values → `T`; old ORDERED_CHILDREN key by parent id and stored label, not by `node.parent`) and was made one batch — in scope because the record write moved into that batch.
- `rocksdb` became a dev-dependency of `raisin-sql-execution` (same version and features as `raisin-rocksdb`'s, so the C++ library is shared) for the bench's `PerfContext` counts.
- No expected-failure entry of the oracle changed: all six still produce their listed wrong results (`expected_failures_still_fail` passes).
- Not changed (pre-existing, noted): the legacy `SetProperty` handler hard-codes workspace `default` and writes no PROPERTY_INDEX entry for the property it sets; on a node whose newest version is a tombstone it decodes the next-older version (resurrecting it). Legacy ops come only from pre-replication-v2 peers.

**Test runs (green).** raisin-rocksdb `--lib` (1283 passed) and `--test all` (489 passed, 13 ignored) — including `node_path_writer_test`, `node_path_tx_move_test`, `node_record_replication_test`, `node_path_auto_backfill_test`, every replication (`apply_revision*`, `cluster_*` non-ignored, `replica_*`) and merge test; raisin-sql-execution `--test all`: 233 passed, only the 8 known failures (6 `hash_join_integration_tests`, `compound_index_hierarchy::bare_child_of_order_by_uses_the_compound_index_and_elides_the_sort`, `profiling_test::profile_complex_json_projection`), the MVCC oracle green in every stage (histories, history GC, replication replay, witnesses in and out of order, expected failures); raisin-transport-http `http_index_repairs` and `http_revision_queries` (6 passed).

---

## Phase 11 — Translation substrate (prerequisite for Phase 12) — DONE (uncommitted)

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-04).**
- **One reader, revision-correct (item 1).** `translation_read.rs` (+ `translation_read/blocks.rs`) is the one reader of `TRANSLATION_DATA` *and* `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS`: newest version at or before the bound decides, `T` there means absent. The repository's `get_translation`, `list_translations_for_node`, `get_block_translation` and `list_nodes_with_translation` now honour the revision they are given (they read HEAD whatever they were asked); the batch reader goes through the same body (its `T` check, `queries.rs`); the transaction's block reader no longer skips a tombstone to fall through to an older version; merge conflict detection (`conflict/helpers.rs::get_translation_at_revision`) delegates instead of hand-decoding. `TRANSLATION_INDEX` is revision-correct too: per node, the newest entry at or before the bound decides and `T` means deleted (node deletes now write index tombstones).
- **One writer, atomic (item 2).** `translation_write.rs`: `stage_version` (the version + its index entry, or the block version), `stage_history` (translation meta + snapshot), `materialize_node_deletion` (history GC only — see Phase 11b). Every writer stages into its caller's `WriteBatch` through it: the repository (`store_translation` / `store_block_translation` are now ONE batch — version, index, meta, snapshot, revision meta — where they were five separate puts), the transaction (node and block; since Phase 11b its commit also stages the meta + snapshot), tree copy and cross-branch copy, merge resolutions, the replication apply arm and history GC. Node deletes write NO translation versions any more (Phase 11b: a read rule; the applicator's and `crud/helpers.rs`' own locale scanners were dead mirrors and are deleted). The `TRANSLATION_INDEX` value is unified: empty for a live entry (the transaction wrote the node id), `T` for a deleted one.
- **One key builder (item 3).** `repositories/translations/keys.rs` is `pub(crate)` and the only builder (node/locale prefixes, block keys, the block orphan marker, index, meta, hash); `key_parse.rs` parses a version key back (fixed 16-byte revision tail). The hand-rolled `format!` copies in the transaction write and block paths, `nodes/queries/translations.rs`, both tombstoners, the snapshot job, the applicator, conflict detection and the orphan marker are gone. Fixed on the way: the snapshot job read translations from the NODES CF, so no translation snapshot was ever created.
- **Replication (item 4).** `raisin-replication`: new `OpType::UpsertTranslationOverlay { workspace, node_id, locale, block_uuid?, overlay: ReplicatedOverlay::{Properties, Hidden, Deleted}, revision, history_complete_from? }` — the ONLY translation op; the pre-Phase-11 `SetTranslation` / `DeleteTranslation` variants are deleted (no binary ever applied them; one in a saved oplog decodes as `Unknown` and is skipped) — and `OpType::Unknown { tag, payload }` — `OpType`'s serde is derived with `remote = "Self"` and wrapped (`operation/op_type_serde.rs`): the variant tag is read first and replayed into the derived visitor with the untouched content (no buffering, every format keeps its own `is_human_readable`), and only an unknown tag is kept as an opaque `rmpv::Value`, re-serialized as the same `{tag: payload}` map so persisting or forwarding it loses nothing. Its target is `OperationTarget::Unknown` (never LWW-merged); the applicator skips it with a warning. `repositories/translations/replication.rs` is the ONE op builder (`translation_op`, `received_version`, `capture_version`), used by the repository, the transaction commit (`transaction/replication/translations.rs` — transaction translation writes were never captured at all), tree and cross-branch copy (also never captured), merge resolutions and the resync. The applicator arm (`replication/application/translation_operations.rs`) applies it through the one writer at the origin's revision (version, index, meta, snapshot; idempotent, out-of-order safe), so Hidden arrives as Hidden and a deletion as `T`. An `Unknown` op is marked applied and is NOT re-dispatched after an upgrade (documented on `OpType::Unknown`): a release adding an op whose loss matters must not let any node emit it before every node decodes it.
- **Resync (item 5).** `RepairKind::ResyncTranslations` (slug `resync_translations`) through the existing admin fan-out endpoint and job: streams every `TRANSLATION_DATA` and `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS` version of the branch (tombstones included) and captures one op per version at its ORIGINAL revision; per-node state record with a resumable cursor (committed per chunk), dry run, disk precheck, rate limit (`repair/translation_resync{,_scan}.rs`).
- **History floor.** `translation_history.rs`: per BRANCH, `{tenant}\0{repo}\0translation_history\0{branch}\0complete_from` (this node's read floor) and `…\0gc_cutoff` in `INDEX_STATUS` (tenant-first: tenant wipe and repo purge cover them). History GC raises a branch's `gc_cutoff` only where it actually deleted a `TRANSLATION_DATA` / `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS` version, to `min(cutoff, branch HEAD at GC time)` (`history_gc/translation_floor.rs`) — retention keeps the newest version at or below the cutoff, so every read at or above that is exact, HEAD reads of an idle branch included. The resync sends the branch's own `gc_cutoff` (never a floor it received: the resync is broadcast, so every peer gets a GC'ing node's floor from that node directly) on that branch's ops; a replica raises its `complete_from` for that branch, unless the floor is at or below its own `gc_cutoff` there (its own floor coming back). Every bounded repository read of the branch (`get_translation`, batch, listings, block read) fails with `InvalidState` ("complete only from revision …") below it. Cached per `(database, tenant, repo, branch)` (insert-if-absent, so a racing raise is never overwritten by a stale miss), dropped by the checkpoint-ingest hook (`derived_cache_registry`).
- **Merge apply writes translations (the Phase 2 item 10 gap).** A TRANSLATION conflict (`translation_locale` set, `{locale}::{block}` for a block) writes exactly the chosen overlay — target's, source's, the manual payload (a stored overlay or a pointer → value map), or a tombstone for `null` — at M (`merge/translations.rs`), and no longer rewrites the node. A NODE resolution carries the kept side's overlays to M and tombstones at M every overlay live on either side but not on the kept one; a deletion tombstones the union (`merge/node_translations.rs`). A locale with its own translation resolution in the same merge is left to it. Both capture their versions for replication.

**Rollout.** By owner decision there is no two-release split (no cluster is in use, so no old-op compatibility is carried): every node emits and applies only `UpsertTranslationOverlay`; there is no `RAISIN_REPLICATION_EMIT_TRANSLATION_V2` flag. Downgrading below this release is unsupported. The flag was removed by owner decision; Phase 11b confirmed this and added the saved-oplog test. Convergence of an existing replica, should one ever exist: `POST /api/management/{repo}/repairs/resync_translations` (fans out; each node re-emits what it holds).

**Review fixes (2026-10-04, uncommitted).** Two reviews of the Phase 11 work found these; each has a regression test named after the failure.
1. *The floor was one value per repository* and equal to the time-based retention cutoff, so after a resync every replica refused the HEAD reads of every branch idle since before the cutoff, and the resync fan-out carried the floor back to the origin. Now per branch, capped at the branch HEAD, recorded only where GC deleted a translation version, sent as the sender's own GC floor only, and ignored by the node that produced it. Tests: `translation_history_floor_test::{idle_branch_head_reads_on_a_replica_after_gc_and_resync, a_floor_returning_to_its_origin_is_ignored}`.
2. *`TRANSLATION_INDEX` is repo-wide*, and the listing let its newest entry decide, so a node delete (or merge tombstone) on one branch hid the node from every branch's "translated into L" listing, and a translation written only on a branch listed it on `main`. The index now yields candidates only; the one reader decides each on the caller's branch and workspace (`list_nodes_with_translation` / `TranslationService::list_translated_nodes` take both). Test: lib `repositories::translations::list_tests::a_delete_on_another_branch_does_not_hide_the_node`.
3. *A node delete's translation tombstones depended on apply order.* They were derived on every node from what was live at HEAD then, so an origin that deleted the node before a peer's translation arrived kept it live forever. The first fix (bounded derivation plus a late-version `T` in the apply arm) still raced — see Phase 11b; the rule is now a READ rule in `translation_read`. Test: `translation_delete_convergence_test::a_translation_racing_a_delete_converges_in_both_orders`.
4. *Cross-branch copy (publish) never tombstoned* an overlay live on the target but deleted on, or absent from, the source; the deleted translation stayed published and replicated as authoritative. It now tombstones those at the copy revision, replicates the deletion, and reports the node as changed. Test: `translation_copy_test::publish_removes_a_translation_deleted_on_the_source`.
5. *The copy collectors were a second reader*: no revision bound, and the block scan decoded the orphan marker as an overlay, failing every later publish or tree copy of a node with one. Both are rewritten over `translation_read::{live_locales, read_version, live_block_overlays, read_block_version}`, bounded at the source pin/HEAD (tree copy: the branch HEAD; the target side of a cross-branch copy: the copy revision). Test: `translation_copy_test::copy_of_a_node_with_an_orphan_marker_succeeds`.
6. LOW: a transaction that translated a node and then deleted it replicated the live overlay from its read cache (and, for a first translation, committed it live). The transaction delete now tombstones the overlays it wrote itself (`transaction/context/translations/write.rs::tombstone_own_writes`), so the commit and the captured op are both `T`. Test: `translation_delete_convergence_test::translate_then_delete_in_one_transaction_replicates_the_deletion`.
7. LOW: `OpType::Unknown` is marked applied and never re-dispatched after an upgrade — documented on `OpType::Unknown` and above, not changed.

**Tests (all green).** raisin-rocksdb `tests/all`: `translation_substrate_test::{overlay_read_at_revision, batch_overlay_read_with_tombstone, translation_write_is_atomic (reads the WAL: one batch of 5 records, 3 for a block), locale_time_travel_below_history_floor_fails_loudly}`; `translation_replication_test::{translation_replicates_to_peer (repository + transaction node + block writes, through the production receive path; plus a peer's op), hidden_replicates_as_hidden (Hidden vs Deleted)}`; `translation_resync_test::{resync_translations_preserves_overlay_history (every read at r0..r5 equal on origin and replica; crash after two chunks then resume; a re-run is idempotent), resync_carries_the_history_floor, unknown_optype_is_skipped_not_stalled (an unknown op in a TCP-codec batch decodes, persists verbatim, is skipped, the ops around it apply, the vector clock moves past it)}`; `merge_translation_resolution_test::{keep_ours_keeps_the_target_overlay_over_a_later_source_edit, keep_theirs_writes_the_source_overlay, manual_resolution_writes_the_given_overlay_or_deletes_it}`; existing `default_language_change_test`, `branch_fork_publish_test::cross_branch_copy_carries_translation_overlay`, `translation_tombstone_tx_test`, `two_node_replication_test::test_translation_sync` green. raisin-replication lib `operation::unknown_tests` (msgpack and JSON decode + round trip, a batch with an unknown op, malformed known content still an error, every translation op round-trips, a removed legacy op decodes as `Unknown`). raisin-rocksdb lib `key_parse` and `translation_read` tests. MVCC oracle: three expected failures pruned (`locale_read_at_past_revision`, `locale_read_after_resolution_over_delete`, `locale_read_on_replica`); their witnesses now run as regressions. Runs: raisin-rocksdb `--lib` (1316 passed) and `--test all` (583 passed, 13 ignored); raisin-replication `--lib` (101) and `--test all` (33); raisin-sql-execution `--test all` (240 passed, only the 8 known failures); raisin-transport-http `http_default_language`.

**Deviations.**
- The legacy `SetTranslation` / `DeleteTranslation` ops are deleted rather than extended (owner decision, no cluster in use): their shape (no workspace, no revision, Hidden folded into delete) cannot be applied correctly, and no binary ever applied them.
- A UNIT variant unknown to an older peer still fails to decode (serde cannot skip content it never sees); every new op must carry data. Documented on `OpType`.
- Bounded reads now hide a translation written ABOVE the branch HEAD until HEAD reaches it — the repository-level write paths that do not move HEAD (direct `TranslationRepository::store_translation` calls; `TranslationService` moves it) are therefore invisible to HEAD-bounded reads. One test helper (`raisin-sql-execution` `resolve_rls_tests::locale::hide`) wrote that way and now moves HEAD like the service.
- `list_block_translations_for_node` stays a HEAD read (the trait passes no revision); the resolver reads `get_block_translations_for_node` at its revision instead (Phase 11c review). ~~Block overlays of a deleted node are still not tombstoned (pre-existing, unchanged).~~ *Done by Phase 11c: a node delete ends its block overlays (read rule) and stores their `T`; the `block_overlay_tombstones` repair cleans up existing data.*
- The floor is checked on the repository's bounded reads only — not on transaction reads (HEAD) or merge conflict detection (which reads at pinned merge bases) — and the origin does not fail its own reads below its GC cutoff (history GC's documented contract: reads below the cutoff see the cutoff state).
- The resync is a `RepairKind` over the existing repair endpoint/job rather than a separate job type; its crash-resume is asserted inside `resync_translations_preserves_overlay_history`, not in a separately named `repair_resumes_after_crash`.
- `management/default_language.rs::count_live_in_cf` still parses keys itself (a repo-wide count, it builds none).

**Legacy replication code candidates for removal** (listed by Phase 11b; **REMOVED by Phase 11d** except the rows marked keep — owner decision 2026-10-04). Everything below exists only for mixed-version clusters, pre-replication-v2 op shapes, or databases older than a format change. Production node writes replicate as `ApplyRevision`, decomposed into `UpsertNodeSnapshot` / `DeleteNodeSnapshot` (`raisin-replication/src/operation_decomposer.rs`, called from `storage/replication.rs::push_operations_to_peers`). `OpType` is serialized by variant NAME (`#[serde(remote = "Self", rename_all = "snake_case")]` + `op_type_serde.rs`), so removing any variant below needs no placeholder: a saved entry of it decodes as `OpType::Unknown` and is skipped (proved for the translation ops by `legacy_translation_op_test`).
| Code | What it is | What depends on it |
|---|---|---|
| `OpType::{CreateNode, SetProperty, RenameNode, SetArchetype}` + `replication/application/applicator/legacy_node_ops.rs` (`apply_create_node`, `apply_set_property`, `apply_rename_node`, `apply_set_archetype`; 515 lines) | Pre-v2 granular node ops. `SetProperty` hard-codes workspace `default`, writes no PROPERTY_INDEX entry and can resurrect a tombstoned node (Phase 10b notes). | No production emitter. `capture_create_node` / `capture_set_archetype` (`replication/operation_capture/node_ops.rs`) are called only by `tests/all/cluster_move_node_test.rs` and `multi_node_crdt_integration.rs`. Referenced by `raisin-replication` `priority.rs`, `operation/op_impl.rs` (`target()`), `crdt.rs` (`merge_property_operations`), `replay/engine.rs::conflict_group_key`, `compaction/compactor.rs`, the `replication_performance` bench, and test modules of `jobs/handlers/{oplog_compaction,replication_gc,vector_clock_verification}.rs`. **Removed by Phase 11d.** |
| `OpType::MoveNode` + `applicator/move_node_ops.rs::apply_move_node` | Pre-v2 move op; out-of-order apply is a known deferred gap (Phase 10b review, "Legacy `MoveNode` out of order"). | No production emitter; `capture_move_node` is called only by `cluster_move_node_test.rs`. `crdt.rs::merge_move_operations`, `replay/engine.rs`, `priority.rs`, `op_impl.rs`. **Removed by Phase 11d.** |
| `OpType::DeleteNode` + `legacy_node_ops.rs::apply_delete_node` | Pre-v2 delete op. | **Still emitted** by cross-branch copy (`repositories/nodes/queries/copy/cross_branch/capture.rs:61`) for nodes deleted on the target. It must move to a `Delete` change inside that copy's `ApplyRevision` before the variant can go. Also `capture_delete_node` (unused), `crdt.rs::merge_delete_operations`, `replay/engine.rs`. **Removed by Phase 11d** (the prune is now a `Delete` change in the promotion's `ApplyRevision`). |
| `OpType::{DeleteProperty, SetOrderKey, SetOwner, PublishNode, UnpublishNode, ListInsertAfter, ListDelete}` | Ops with NO applicator arm (they fall to the `_ => debug!` arm in `applicator/mod.rs`); applying one does nothing. | Builders `capture_set_order_key` / `capture_set_owner` / `capture_publish_node` / `capture_unpublish_node` exist but are called only by `multi_node_crdt_integration.rs` (or nowhere). `crdt.rs` list/property merges, `priority.rs`, `op_impl.rs`. **Removed by Phase 11d.** |
| `raisin-replication/src/compaction/` + `jobs/handlers/oplog_compaction.rs` | Oplog compactor; its only compaction key is `SetProperty`, so it compacts nothing a current node emits. | `OpLogCompactionHandler` is registered in `jobs/handlers/mod.rs`. Dead in effect, not in wiring. **Removed by Phase 11d.** |
| `applicator/node_baseline.rs` `scoped_miss_fallbacks` branch scan, and the `workspace.unwrap_or("default")` defaults (`applicator/mod.rs:55,354`, `crdt_ops.rs:37,618,672`, `move_node_ops.rs:218`, `legacy_node_ops.rs:463`) | "An op's node carries its workspace — except from older peers": a defaulted-workspace miss falls back to a whole-branch scan. | The module doc already says it goes after a release with the counter at zero; `replication_baseline_test.rs` asserts the counter. **Removed by Phase 11d** (the scan fallback and counter; a workspace-less node is applied in `"default"` explicitly, no scan). |
| `crdt_ops.rs` (~line 310) empty `cf_order_key` handling | Ops captured before `order_key` was stamped carry `""`; the apply path refuses to relabel from it. | Defensive against pre-stamp captures and legacy copies; keep unless every oplog predates nothing. |
| `raisin-replication/src/coordinator/sync.rs::sync_with_peer` | "Backward compatibility" wrapper that syncs a hard-coded `tenant1/repo1`. | No production caller. **Removed by Phase 11d.** |
| `replication/operation_capture/core.rs` (~line 409) empty vector-clock-snapshot rebuild | Startup fallback for a DB that predates snapshot maintenance. | A storage-format compat path, not a mixed-version one; cheap, keep. |


**Changes.**
1. **Revision-correct overlay reads.** `translations/{nodes,blocks}.rs` share one reader for "newest ≤ rev, `T` means absent", and the batch reader gains the `T` check (`queries.rs:115`). This is a correctness fix for time-travel reads with a locale, and the prerequisite for Phase 3's historical-translation behaviour.
2. **Atomic writes.** The repository `store_translation` writes **one WriteBatch** (`nodes.rs:102-130`). The TRANSLATION_INDEX value is unified with the transaction writer's.
3. **One key builder.** Every translation key comes from `repositories/translations/keys.rs` (`pub(crate)`), replacing about 15 `format!` copies.
4. **Overlay replication.**
   - New op `UpsertTranslationOverlay { workspace, node_id, locale, overlay | Hidden | Deleted, revision }` in `raisin-replication/src/operation/op_type.rs`, plus an applicator arm in `applicator/mod.rs`.
   - **Release 1 also adds an `Unknown` catch-all OpType variant** so that future ops degrade to skip-and-warn (or are buffered) instead of stalling the applier. The OpType enum has no catch-all today, so an older binary cannot decode a new op.
   - ~~Two-release rollout: release 1 *applies* both the new and the legacy op, and still *emits* the legacy op. Release 2 emits the new op, gated by `RAISIN_REPLICATION_EMIT_TRANSLATION_V2`.~~ *Superseded by owner decision (no cluster in use): one op, emitted and applied from this release, no flag.*
   - Hidden is captured as Hidden, not as Delete.
5. **Convergence of existing replicas.** An admin "resync translations" job, fanned out per the discipline above.
   - It **re-emits the full overlay history**: every TRANSLATION_DATA version and its tombstones at their original revisions. Re-emitting only the current overlay falsifies replica history (today's translation stamped at an old revision, or a change that never happened at a new revision).
   - Where full history is unavailable, record a per-repo `translation_history_complete_from` revision on the replica. Below it, a locale-scoped time-travel read **fails loudly** (or falls back to the default language with an explicit marker), never serves a fabricated version.

**Tests to add.**
- `overlay_read_at_revision`;
- `batch_overlay_read_with_tombstone`;
- `translation_write_is_atomic` (fault injection between puts is not possible, so assert a single batch);
- `translation_replicates_to_peer`, `hidden_replicates_as_hidden`;
- `resync_translations_preserves_overlay_history`;
- `locale_time_travel_below_history_floor_fails_loudly`;
- `unknown_optype_is_skipped_not_stalled` (mixed-version, see Operational test gates);
- `a_removed_legacy_translation_op_in_a_saved_oplog_is_skipped` (Phase 11b: a `set_translation` / `delete_translation` entry in a saved oplog decodes as `Unknown`, is skipped, and stalls nothing);
- `default_language_change_test` (existing);
- `cross_branch_copy_carries_translation_overlay` (existing).

**Rollback.** Downgrading below this release is unsupported: its oplog holds `UpsertTranslationOverlay` ops an older binary cannot decode.

**Expected gain.** Correctness: replicas serve translated content, and time travel shows historical translations.

**Risk.** Medium. It changes the replication protocol. It was planned behind a flag; by owner decision (Phase 11b) it is not gated, because no cluster is in use.

## Phase 11b — Translation replication V2 on by default (no rollout flag) — DONE (uncommitted)

**Owner decision (2026-10-04).** No cluster is in use, so translation replication carries no code for mixed-version clusters or old oplogs. This overrides the "gate the later step behind a default-OFF flag" rule for Phase 11's V2 emission.

**Done (2026-10-04, uncommitted).**
1. **Flag and legacy ops removed.** `RAISIN_REPLICATION_EMIT_TRANSLATION_V2` does not exist anywhere in the tree. `OpType::SetTranslation` / `DeleteTranslation` are gone, along with their emission, apply arms, builders and tests. Every writer emits `UpsertTranslationOverlay` through the one builder, `repositories/translations/replication.rs`, which `resync_translations` uses too. `OpType` is serialized by variant NAME (externally tagged, `snake_case`, through `op_type_serde.rs`), so the variants were deleted outright and no positional placeholder is needed. `OpType::Unknown` stays. Downgrading below this release is unsupported.
2. **A saved legacy op is skipped, never a stall.** New test `tests/all/legacy_translation_op_test.rs::a_removed_legacy_translation_op_in_a_saved_oplog_is_skipped` covers it end to end. The test writes `set_translation` / `delete_translation` entries into `cf::OPERATION_LOG` as raw bytes, byte for byte as a pre-Phase-11 binary encoded them. Both oplog readers (`get_operations_from_node`, catch-up `get_missing_operations`) decode them as `Unknown`, and re-persisting them loses nothing. The applicator skips them and writes no translation. Replaying them through the production receive path ahead of a current op still applies that op, and the vector clock moves past them. The decode half was already covered by `raisin-replication` `operation::unknown_tests::a_removed_legacy_translation_op_decodes_as_unknown`.
3. **The Phase 11 review findings were all already fixed by the fix step.** This step checked each one in the code and re-ran its regression test (green):
   - HIGH, per-repo `translation_history_complete_from` floor above branch HEAD: now per branch and `min(cutoff, HEAD)` (`history_gc/translation_floor.rs`), recorded only where GC deleted a translation version. Tests: `translation_history_floor_test::{idle_branch_head_reads_on_a_replica_after_gc_and_resync, a_floor_returning_to_its_origin_is_ignored}`.
   - MEDIUM, `TRANSLATION_INDEX` has no branch: the index only yields candidates, and the one reader decides each one on the caller's branch and workspace (`translations/queries.rs::list_nodes_with_translation`). Test: lib `repositories::translations::list_tests::a_delete_on_another_branch_does_not_hide_the_node`.
   - MEDIUM, node-delete translation tombstones not replicated / order-dependent: they were derived on every node, bounded at the delete's revision, with a late version tombstoned by `stage_deletion_of_late_version` — superseded by the read rule (Review fixes below). Tests: `translation_delete_convergence_test::{a_translation_racing_a_delete_converges_in_both_orders, translate_then_delete_in_one_transaction_replicates_the_deletion}`.
   - MEDIUM, cross-branch copy never tombstones target overlays deleted on the source: it now does, at the copy revision, and replicates the change. Test: `translation_copy_test::publish_removes_a_translation_deleted_on_the_source`.
   - MEDIUM, copy paths use two hand-rolled collectors: both now go through `translation_read::{live_locales, read_version, live_block_overlays, read_block_version}`. Test: `translation_copy_test::copy_of_a_node_with_an_orphan_marker_succeeds`.
4. **Legacy replication code is LISTED, not removed.** See "Legacy replication code candidates for removal" in Phase 11. *(Removed since, by Phase 11d.)*

**Test runs (green).** raisin-rocksdb `--test all translation` (25 passed, including the new module) and `default_language` (5); raisin-rocksdb `--lib translation` (26); raisin-replication `--lib` (101).

**Deviations.** The only code change is the new test. The Phase 11 fix step had already removed the flag, the legacy ops and their tests, and fixed every review finding, so nothing else was left to change.

**Review fixes (2026-10-04, uncommitted).** Two reviews of Phase 11b found that review fix 3 (node-delete translation tombstones) converged only when the delete and the translation were written one after the other. Each finding has a regression test named after the failure (`tests/all/translation_delete_read_rule_test.rs` unless stated).
1. MEDIUM ×3 (one root cause): *deriving a node delete's translation tombstones was a read followed by a separate write, in two places, unserialized, and the late-version half ran only in the replication apply arm.* A translation committed BELOW a delete that had already staged its tombstones (`TranslationService` allocates its revision, then reads the node; transaction conflict checks are a no-op) stayed live on the origin while every replica tombstoned it, and `resync_translations` could not repair it; two peers' batches applied concurrently on one replica (one task per TCP connection, no per-node lock) each missed the other's derivation; checkpoint ingest ran no derivation at all. **Fix: the rule is a READ rule, in the one reader.** A live node-overlay version at `R` is absent at bound `B` when its node has a delete tombstone at some `Rd` with `R <= Rd <= B` (`translation_read::ended_by_node_delete` → `mvcc_read::delete_in_range`: one `NODES` seek at `B`, walked down to `R`, nothing decoded). `read_version` / `read_overlay` / `live_locales` apply it, so every caller does — repository reads and listings (`list_nodes_with_translation` decides each index candidate through it), the transaction reader, the batch reader, merge conflict detection and resolutions, both copy collectors — and so does the default-language overlay count (`management/default_language.rs`, which reads the CF itself). Block overlays are untouched (a node delete never ended them) — *superseded by Phase 11c: the same read rule now ends block overlays too*.
