The write-time derivation is gone: `stage_node_deletion`, `stage_deletion_of_late_version`, `first_delete_at_or_above`, step 10 of `tombstones::add_node_tombstones`, the repository/cross-branch-prune `add_translation_tombstones_to_batch` and the apply arm's late-version call. Nothing races because nothing is derived; a checkpoint needs no derivation pass. Tests: `a_translation_committed_below_a_delete_that_staged_first_is_absent_everywhere` (origin, in-order replica, reversed replica; listings and the default-language count), `a_delete_and_a_peers_translation_applied_concurrently_converge` (two `put_operations_batch` calls joined on one replica), `a_checkpoint_carrying_a_translation_below_a_stored_delete_does_not_revive_it`; lib `translation_read::tests::a_node_delete_ends_the_versions_below_it_from_its_revision_on`.
   - **History GC owes the rule its evidence.** Retention deletes `NODES` tombstones (one below the cutoff that is not the newest there, and orphans), and a dropped delete would revive the overlays it ended. Before deleting a node tombstone at `Rd`, the GC pass stages `T` at `Rd` for every locale whose newest STORED version at or before `Rd` is live (`translation_write::materialize_node_deletion`, `history_gc/node_delete_translations.rs`), in the same batch as the tombstone's delete; retention then keeps that `T` as the newest version at or below its cutoff. Test: `history_gc_dropping_a_node_delete_keeps_its_translation_absent` (verified to fail with the hook disabled). Known edge, documented: a translation version BELOW the cutoff that arrives by replication after GC dropped the delete it precedes is live — the same "reads below the cutoff see the cutoff state" contract history GC already has.
   - Cost: a live node-overlay read now also walks the node's `NODES` versions in `[R, B]` (keys only; a HEAD read walks every version newer than the translation). Typical nodes have few; a node rewritten thousands of times since its translation pays that many key steps per locale read, bounded by retention GC.
2. LOW: *a transaction translation write stored no `TranslationMeta`* while the replica's apply arm stored one from the op, so `get_translation_meta` differed. The commit now stages meta + snapshot for every translation version it wrote (`transaction/replication/translations.rs::stage_translation_history`, through `translation_write::stage_history`), with the same revision, actor (`attributed_actor`, the resolution the captured op uses) and message (now carried on the op) as the replica stores. Timestamps can differ by the capture delay, as for repository writes. Test: `a_transaction_translation_has_the_same_meta_on_origin_and_replica`. (The derived node-delete `T`s that also lacked meta no longer exist.)
3. LOW: *`legacy_translation_op_test`'s last part could not fail* (the receive path only logs apply errors). The batch now also goes through the TCP message codec and `ReplayEngine::replay` (asserting the removed ops stay `Unknown` and the current op survives), and the test says that the direct `apply_operation(..).unwrap()` loop is the check that the `Unknown` arm returns `Ok`.
4. LOW: *legacy `SetProperty` resurrected deleted nodes*: its apply arm patched the first version that DECODED, skipping a newest tombstone. It now reads through the one baseline reader (`mvcc_read::node_version_at_or_before`) and refuses on a tombstone. Test: `legacy_set_property_delete_test::a_legacy_set_property_on_a_deleted_node_does_not_resurrect_it`. Removing the pre-v2 node ops outright (`CreateNode`, `SetProperty`, `RenameNode`, `SetArchetype`, `MoveNode`, the arm-less ones, and `DeleteNode` once cross-branch copy stops emitting it) is still the owner's call in the table above; it touches 38 files, most of them tests that use `SetProperty` as a generic op. *Done by Phase 11d: the ops, their arms and this test are gone.*

**Test runs (green).** raisin-rocksdb `--lib` (1319 passed) and `--test all` (596 passed, 13 ignored); raisin-sql-execution `--test all` (240 passed, only the 8 known failures); raisin-transport-http `http_default_language` (6).

## Phase 11c — Node delete tombstones block translations — DONE (uncommitted)

**Owner request (2026-10-04).** A node delete ended its node-level translation overlays but NEVER its block overlays in `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS`. They stayed live forever: history GC could not reclaim them (the newest version was not a tombstone), direct scans (translation resync, default-language management, copy collectors) saw them as live, and a restore/recreate under the same id resurrected them.

**Done (2026-10-04, uncommitted).** The request was written against the pre-Phase-11b code (`stage_node_deletion`, `stage_deletion_of_late_version`); Phase 11b's review had already replaced that write-time derivation for node overlays with a READ rule, because each half was a read followed by a separate write and raced (a translation committed below a delete that had staged first; two peers' batches applied concurrently; checkpoint ingest). So block overlays get the same two layers node overlays have, plus the `T` the owner asked for as storage hygiene:
1. **The read rule ends block overlays (the guarantee).** `translation_read/blocks.rs`: `read_block_version` treats a live version at `R` as absent at bound `B` when the node has a delete tombstone in `[R, B]` (`ended_by_node_delete`, as for node overlays); `live_block_overlays` asks the node's deletes ONCE per listing (`mvcc_read::newest_delete_in_range` from the oldest candidate's revision — a version is ended exactly when the newest delete in range is `>= R`). Every block reader goes through these two — the repository (`get_block_translation`, `list_block_translations_for_node`), the transaction (`get_block_translation`, `list_block_translations_for_node`), both copy collectors, merge conflict detection and node resolutions — and the default-language count (`management/default_language.rs`, which reads the CF itself) now applies the rule to `block_trans` keys too. This is what makes the answer the same in any arrival order, after a checkpoint ingest, and after a recreate under the same id.
2. **The delete stores `T` for its block overlays.** One body, `translation_write::materialize_block_deletion` (`translation_write/block_deletion.rs`): `T` at the delete's revision for every block overlay whose newest STORED version at or before it is live (`translation_read::stored_live_block_overlays`, bounded at the delete). Called by the delete funnel (`tombstones::add_node_tombstones_with_parent`, new step 13 — transaction, repository, cascade, merge, cross-branch-copy occupant and the replication apply path all go through it), by the two delete paths that do NOT go through the funnel (cross-branch `delete_missing` prune, `copy/cross_branch/prune.rs`; merge deletion with no live version on either side, `merge/deletion.rs`), and by history GC before it drops a node tombstone (`materialize_node_deletion` now covers blocks — otherwise dropping the tombstone would revive what the read rule ended). Derived and local: never captured for replication; every node derives the same `T` from the same versions. The transaction delete also tombstones the block overlays THIS transaction wrote (`tombstone_own_writes`, which only covered node overlays), so a translate-then-delete transaction commits and replicates `T`.
3. **A late block version gets the delete's `T` too.** `translation_write::stage_version` — the one writer — on staging a LIVE block version at `R` stores `T` at the OLDEST stored delete at or above `R` (`block_deletion::stage_late_version`, `mvcc_read::deletes_in_range`): a replicated version arriving after the delete it precedes, or a local write racing one, ends up stored exactly like the in-order case. A `T` at a delete's revision can never disagree with the read rule (every version at or below it is ended for reads at or after it; nothing above it is touched), so a materialization that loses a race changes no answer; the repair below catches the storage up.
4. **Cleanup for existing data: the `block_overlay_tombstones` repair** (`RepairKind::BlockOverlayTombstones`, `repair/block_overlay_tombstones.rs`). Streams the branch's `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS` a node at a time, walks each node's `NODES` deletes up to the branch HEAD, and writes, for EVERY delete (oldest first, not only the newest — a deleted-and-recreated node still owes its first delete), exactly what the delete funnel now writes, through the bounded writer (cursor = the last finished node's key prefix, committed with each batch; dry run; disk precheck on `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS`; rate limit). Data-detected and idempotent: a stored `T` is the newest version at its delete, so a re-run over clean data writes nothing. **Queued by itself** like the `node_path` backfill (`repair/auto_block_overlays.rs`): after the job system starts (90 s delay, after the other three chains' first links), one branch at a time, on every branch whose state record on this node is not `done`; `RAISIN_BLOCK_OVERLAY_TOMBSTONES_AUTO=0` turns it off (the admin fan-out endpoint still runs it, `POST /api/management/{repo}/repairs/block_overlay_tombstones`). **Checkpoint transfer:** an ingest put-merges the peer's `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS`, possibly with versions of deleted nodes and no `T`; readers are unaffected (the read rule), and the ingest hook (`reenqueue_repairs_after_ingest`) marks the cleanup pending on every branch and restarts its chain.
5. **History GC** now reclaims what a delete ended: retention keeps the newest version at or below its cutoff, which is the `T` instead of the live version (index-family `T`s themselves are kept, as for every translation CF).

**Rollout.** No new CF, no new op, no key-format change; the `T`s are ordinary `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS` versions every release already reads (an older binary reads a `T` as "deleted"), so no release split or gate is needed. The only switch is the automatic cleanup's (default ON).

**Tests (green).** raisin-rocksdb `tests/all`: `block_overlay_delete_test::{a_node_delete_tombstones_its_block_overlays (gone at HEAD, present at a revision before the delete, `T` stored), block_overlays_converge_whichever_of_the_delete_and_the_version_arrives_first (both peers plus two replicas taking the two batches in opposite orders: delete before the block version, and after), translate_block_then_delete_in_one_transaction_replicates_the_deletion, a_recreated_node_does_not_get_its_block_overlays_back (no `T` stored, as in a pre-11c database; repository and transaction reads; written again on purpose it is back), history_gc_dropping_a_node_delete_keeps_its_block_overlays_absent (also the default-language count)}`; `block_overlay_cleanup_repair_test::{the_cleanup_stores_every_missing_block_tombstone_once (dry run, every delete of a recreated node, live node untouched, state `done` leaves the automatic chain's pending list, idempotent re-run writes nothing), the_cleanup_resumes_after_a_crash, after_the_cleanup_history_gc_reclaims_the_ended_versions, after_a_concurrent_apply_the_cleanup_stores_the_tombstone}`. Lib: `translation_read::tests::a_node_delete_ends_its_block_overlays_too`; `tombstones::tests::test_deletion_column_families_count` (12). Verified to fail with the late-version path and the GC block materialization disabled (the convergence and GC tests), and with the block read rule disabled (recreate, GC and cleanup tests). Runs: raisin-rocksdb `--lib` (1327 passed, 3 ignored) and `--test all` (652 passed, 13 ignored). The MVCC oracle (`raisin-sql-execution --test all mvcc_index_oracle`) was started but had not finished when this step ended; it does not model block overlays, so it serves only as a regression guard for the delete-funnel change. Re-run it before committing.

**Deviations.**
- The two functions the request names no longer existed (Phase 11b review removed them); the request's three write-side items are implemented as materializations of a read rule instead of as the rule itself, for the race reasons above. The read rule for block overlays is the addition that actually guarantees items 1–2's "same result in any arrival order".
- The delete funnel materializes BLOCK overlays only; node overlays stay read-rule-only plus the GC materialization (Phase 11b's decision — deriving their `T` at delete time would also re-derive `TRANSLATION_INDEX` and localized-name entries the rule already decides). The asymmetry is deliberate: block overlays have no index side effects.
- The repair tombstones at EVERY delete of a node with block overlays, not only "nodes whose newest version is a delete": a recreated node's earlier delete owes its `T` too, and that is what retention GC needs to reclaim the version below it.
- The repair holds no node lock: a `T` at a delete's revision is correct whatever lands concurrently.

**Review fixes (2026-10-04, uncommitted).** Two reviewers' findings, each verified against the code first:
1. **MEDIUM ×2 (one root cause): a version written ABOVE a delete into the dead generation it opened stayed live for good.** The rule only ended a version at `R` with a delete in `[R, bound]`, and every materialization was bounded the same way, so a peer that had not seen the delete, a merge replaying a branch's translation onto a node the target deleted (conflict detection keys by `(node, locale)` and does not see it), or a write racing the delete left an overlay live at HEAD, counted, never reclaimed, and back after a recreate. **Fix: one more clause in the one rule** — a version is also ended when the node's newest `NODES` record at or before `R` is a delete tombstone (`mvcc_read::NodeLifeline`, the same walk one record further; node AND block overlays, so the two do not drift; every reader and the default-language count inherit it). A version with no node record below it (its create not yet arrived) is not ended. Not materialized at write time: a live record landing later between the delete and the version (out-of-order replication) makes it live again, and a stored `T` cannot be taken back. History GC, which relies on its cutoff for exactly that, materializes it when it drops the delete (`translation_write::materialize_dead_generation`: `T` over every live node/block version in `(D, next record)`, at its own revision — never visible at any bound, so nothing changes), and keeps the live record directly above every kept tombstone (`keep_generation_starts`), or dropping a recreate under a pin would have turned the tombstone below it into the "newest record" of a live generation. Merge conflict detection is unchanged: the merged state is consistent by the rule. Tests: `block_overlay_dead_generation_test::{a_translation_written_above_a_delete_by_a_peer_that_had_not_seen_it_stays_ended, history_gc_dropping_a_delete_keeps_a_dead_generation_version_ended}`; lib `translation_read::tests::a_version_written_into_a_dead_generation_stays_ended_after_a_recreate`, `history_gc::node_delete_translations::tests::a_dropped_recreate_above_a_kept_delete_is_kept`. Two earlier lib tests modelled "a recreated node" with no recreate record; they now store one.
2. **MEDIUM, not a defect (guarded): a transaction deleting and recreating a node at one revision.** Not reachable: `validate_create` checks the id against COMMITTED state, so `put_node`/`add_node` of an id the same transaction deleted fails with `Conflict`, and the replication tracker coalesces one op per node per transaction. Step 13 stays in the delete funnel; the test `a_transaction_cannot_recreate_a_node_it_deleted` pins the assumption (and the funnel comment says what must move to the commit if it ever changes).
3. **MEDIUM: a time-travel read lost the blocks a LATER delete ended.** The resolver chose blocks from the HEAD listing. **Fix:** `TranslationRepository::get_block_translations_for_node(.., locales, revision)` — every live block overlay of the node in the fallback chain AS OF the read revision, in one call; `resolve_node` and `resolve_nodes_batch` apply from it. Test: `a_time_travel_read_applies_the_block_translations_a_later_delete_ended` (resolver and batch).
4. **MEDIUM: per-block `NODES` walks on the hot localized render path.** **Fix:** `translation_read::live_block_versions` — one `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS` prefix scan and ONE lifeline walk per node, decoding only the wanted locales — behind the new repository method, both copy collectors and merge node resolutions; `live_locales` also walks once per node instead of once per locale. Test: lib `reading_every_block_of_a_node_walks_its_history_once` (40 blocks × 2 locales, 300 node versions, one walk).
5. **LOW (fixed): the cleanup charged only key-prefix bytes and committed its cursor only when the write batch filled.** It now charges the node's whole `NODES` walk (keys and values, `mvcc_read::deletes_in_range_counted`) and commits (cursor included) once the bytes read since the last commit reach the batch size (`BoundedWriter::checkpoint_scan`). Charging the `BLOCK_TRANSLATIONS` scan itself is still approximate (prefix only).
6. **LOW (docs corrected, not fixed): a lost materialization race on a branch already `done` is not caught up automatically.** `block_deletion.rs` and `auto_block_overlays.rs` no longer promise it: the read rule serves the right answer; storage catches up when the admin endpoint runs the cleanup or history GC drops the delete.
7. **LOW (deferred): the ingest re-mark can be overwritten by a running link** (needs an invalidation epoch like `property_index`'s `commit_rebuild`; storage hygiene only, reads unaffected).

Runs: raisin-rocksdb `--lib` (1330 passed, 3 ignored), `--test all` (656 passed, 13 ignored); raisin-core `--lib translation` (22); raisin-sql-execution `--test all` translation/locale filters (14). The new rule, the GC materialization and the revision-bounded resolver read were each disabled once to confirm their tests fail.

## Phase 11d — Remove legacy replication node ops — DONE (uncommitted)

**Owner decision (2026-10-04).** Nobody runs a raisindb cluster yet, so no code is carried that exists only for mixed-version clusters or for old persisted oplogs/checkpoints. This phase works through the "Legacy replication code candidates for removal" table in Phase 11. Like Phase 11b, it overrides the "gate the later step behind a default-OFF flag" rule: nothing new is emitted, so there is no later step to gate.

**Done (2026-10-04, uncommitted).**
1. **Nothing current emits a pre-v2 node op any more.** A grep of every capture and emit site (transaction capture, repository writes, copy and promotion, merge, functions, transports, the CLI, tests) found one production emitter. Cross-branch promotion's `delete_missing` prune captured one `DeleteNode { node_id }` per pruned node beside the copy's `ApplyRevision`. Each prune is now a `Delete` change inside that same `ApplyRevision`, built where the other node changes are built (`copy/cross_branch/capture.rs`). The change carries the pre-delete node, stamped with its workspace, plus its ORDERED_CHILDREN placement. That placement (parent id and label) is resolved by the prune itself (`prune.rs` now returns `PrunedNode { node, placement }`). The push path decomposes the change into a `DeleteNodeSnapshot`, like every other delete. Every other emitter of a granular node op was a test.
2. **The ops are gone.** These `OpType` variants are deleted: `CreateNode`, `DeleteNode`, `SetProperty`, `DeleteProperty`, `RenameNode`, `SetArchetype`, `SetOrderKey`, `SetOwner`, `PublishNode`, `UnpublishNode`, `MoveNode`, `ListInsertAfter` and `ListDelete`. Their uses are removed from `target()`, `is_delete()` (which now counts `DeleteNodeSnapshot`), `priority.rs` and `replay/engine.rs::conflict_group_key`. The legacy property, move, list and delete-wins merges in `crdt.rs` are deleted too, and `ConflictType` keeps only `ConcurrentSchemaUpdate`: a node write is one snapshot register merged LWW, and delete-wins is decided by the applicator's tombstones. Also deleted:
   - the apply arms `applicator/legacy_node_ops.rs` and `applicator/move_node_ops.rs`;
   - every legacy capture builder in `operation_capture/node_ops.rs` (`capture_create_node`, `capture_delete_node`, `capture_rename_node`, `capture_move_node`, `capture_set_archetype`, `capture_set_order_key`, `capture_set_owner`, `capture_publish_node`, `capture_unpublish_node`).

   `OpType` is serialized by variant NAME, so no placeholder is needed. A saved entry of any removed op decodes as `OpType::Unknown` and is skipped. The `OpType` doc lists the retired tags, which must never be reused for a differently shaped op.
3. **Dead helpers removed.** Each of these only served the removed ops:
   - `write_node_record_keeping_path` and its `anchor_node_path` (the path-less record write; only the legacy `SetProperty` and `MoveNode` used it).
   - The oplog compactor (`raisin-replication/src/compaction/`, `COMPACTION.md`, `OpLogRepository::compact_oplog`, `jobs/handlers/oplog_compaction.rs`). Its only compaction key was `SetProperty`.
   - `JobType::OpLogCompaction`. Nothing ever enqueued it, so no persisted job carries it; job types are stored as their display string.
   - The `oplog_compaction_*` and `oplog_merge_property_updates` config fields.
   - The defaulted-workspace branch-scan fallback in the replication baseline reader: `WorkspaceHint::Defaulted` and the `scoped_miss_fallbacks` counter. A replicated node's workspace is now always `Explicit`. Every emitter stamps it, so a miss is a plain miss. Only an id-only `DeleteNodeSnapshot` still scans, counted by `unknown_workspace_scans`.
   - `ReplicationCoordinator::sync_with_peer`, the hard-coded `tenant1/repo1` wrapper. Its test callers use `sync_with_peer_for_tenants`.
4. **Kept, as the table said:** the read rule for legacy node RECORDS on disk (Phase 10). That is data, not old code paths: a full-`Node` blob with no `NODE_PATH` entry stays readable, and a path-less record a removed op stored before this release is still read through `newer_version.rs`. Also kept: `crdt_ops.rs`'s empty-`cf_order_key` handling and the startup vector-clock-snapshot rebuild (storage-format compat, not mixed-version).

**Tests (green).**
- New `tests/all/legacy_node_op_test.rs::every_removed_legacy_node_op_in_a_saved_oplog_is_skipped`, the sibling of `legacy_translation_op_test`:
  - Every removed node op is written into `cf::OPERATION_LOG` byte for byte as the last binary that had them serialized it. Each op is aimed at a live node and would change it if applied (delete, retitle, move, rename, publish, set the archetype, run a list op, create a second node).
  - Both oplog readers decode the ops as `Unknown`, and re-persisting them loses nothing.
  - The direct `apply_operation(..).unwrap()` loop proves the `Unknown` arm returns `Ok`.
  - The ops then go through the TCP codec, the replay engine and the production receive path, followed by a current op from the same peer.
  - The no-stall assertion reads the APPLIED STATE directly: the target node is untouched (path, name, title, archetype, published, no list items, no created node), the op after the removed ones is applied (its node exists with its title), and the vector clock moves past them. `put_operations_batch` only logs apply errors, so a returned `Ok` proves nothing.
- New `tests/all/cross_branch_prune_replication_test.rs::a_promotion_prune_replicates_as_a_delete_change`:
  - The prune is a `Delete` change in the promotion's `ApplyRevision` on `publish`, carrying the pre-delete node, its workspace, its parent id and its label.
  - Two replicas receive the origin's oplog, one as stored and one decomposed as the push path sends it. Both keep the promoted section and prune the page.
  - Verified to fail with the delete changes left out of the capture.
- `raisin-replication` lib `operation::unknown_tests::every_removed_legacy_node_op_decodes_as_unknown` covers all thirteen tags, from JSON and from the named msgpack the oplog and the wire use.
- `legacy_translation_op_test` is unchanged in substance. Its placeholder op and its helpers are shared with the new sibling.
- Rewritten:
  - `node_record_replication_test` drops the legacy `SetProperty`/`MoveNode` scenarios. It gains `a_legacy_only_node_stays_readable_and_a_snapshot_stores_the_one_format`, which reads a raw legacy record through the read rule and then applies a replicated snapshot on top, and `replicated_records_are_the_one_format` now covers snapshots only.
  - `replication_baseline_test::an_explicit_workspace_miss_does_not_scan` replaces the defaulted-fallback test.
  - `nested_reference_paths_test::replicated_create_indexes_nested_references` now uses a snapshot.
  - `apply_revision_test`, `audit_attribution_test`, `replication_out_of_order_test` and `translation_resync_test` use `DeleteNodeSnapshot` at the op's revision.
- Deleted: `legacy_set_property_delete_test` (its subject is gone) and the two legacy `MoveNode` tests in `cluster_move_node_test` (`test_move_tree_replication`, the `ApplyRevision` one, stays).
- Tests that used a legacy op only as "some op" now use one of three stand-ins. Storage, oplog, GC, queue and priority unit tests use `DeleteNodeSnapshot` or `CreateTag`. The proptest model in `raisin-replication` `tests/all/property_tests.rs` is rebuilt on node snapshots, node deletes and relations. The cluster tests capture an `UpsertNodeSnapshot` through the new test helper `tests/all/replicated_node_ops.rs` (forwarded as is, so op ids match across peers).
- `multi_node_crdt_integration::test_out_of_order_delivery_with_causal_buffer` now chains a parent and three children. Successive snapshots of ONE node would be LWW-merged to one by the replay engine.

**Test runs.** `raisin-replication` `--lib` (94 passed) and `--test all` (33 passed). `raisin-rocksdb` `--lib` (1328 passed, 3 ignored; 2 fewer because the compactor's tests are gone) and `--test all` (653 passed, 13 ignored). `raisin-storage` `--lib jobs` (34 passed). `cargo check --tests` is clean for `raisin-sql-execution`, `raisin-transport-http`, `raisin-storage` and `raisin-server`. `raisin-sql-execution` `--test all`: 244 passed, only the 8 known failures (6 `hash_join_integration_tests`, `compound_index_hierarchy::bare_child_of_order_by_uses_the_compound_index_and_elides_the_sort`, `profiling_test::profile_complex_json_projection`); the MVCC oracle green.

**Rollout.** Nothing new is emitted. The promotion's deletes now travel inside the `ApplyRevision` every release already applies, so an older binary applies them too, and downgrading is unaffected. A pre-v2 peer's granular node ops would be skipped by this binary. By owner decision no such peer exists.

**Deviations.**
- A replicated node with no `workspace` is applied in `"default"` (`applicator::node_workspace`, one place, no scan). It is not rejected: rejecting would need every hand-built test fixture audited, and no current emitter can produce such a node. Only the scan fallback was the mixed-version code.
- `ChangeTracker` (`replication/change_tracker.rs`) still records per-property changes, moves and name changes on the transaction write path, but nothing reads them since node writes became snapshots. That is dead work on a hot path, but it predates this phase, is not legacy-op code, and lives in `put_node` / `tracking.rs`. Left as a follow-up.
- The unused relation builders `capture_add_relation` / `capture_remove_relation` are kept: relations are current ops.
- `ConflictType` lost three variants (`ConcurrentPropertyUpdate`, `ConcurrentMove`, `DeleteWinsOverUpdate`). Only `ReplayResult` / `ConflictInfo` carry the type, and nothing persists it.

**Review fixes (2026-10-04).** Two reviewers checked the promotion's deletes against the code. Every HIGH and MEDIUM finding was real, and each is fixed in the shared path:
1. *The prune erased its replacement's path.* `delete_missing` tombstoned a node that the same promotion had already displaced, and wrote that tombstone AFTER the replacement's put to the same PATH_INDEX key (and to the same UNIQUE key, which carries no node id). The path then resolved to nothing. Now the prune runs BEFORE any node is staged, so a delete always precedes a put on a shared key. Staging skips an occupant the prune already retired (`CopyAccumulators::retired`).
2. *A displaced occupant was never replicated.* Displacement deleted the old generation on the origin but left it out of the `ApplyRevision`, so it stayed live on every replica. Each displaced node is now a `PrunedNode` (`CopyAccumulators::displaced`) and travels as a `Delete` change. `capture.rs` emits all deletes BEFORE the upserts, mirroring the origin batch. With `delete_missing`, a ROOT re-created under a fresh id now has its previous generation and that generation's subtree pruned. Before, the prune looked the root up by id only and orphaned that subtree.
3. *One retire body.* The prune's hand-mirrored tombstoner missed NODE_PATH, COMPOUND, SPATIAL, SECRETS and the vmount registry, which replicas DID tombstone. It is replaced by `prune::retire_target_node`, which runs the shared `tombstones::add_node_tombstones_with_parent` plus UNIQUE. The prune and displacement both use it, and `helpers::displace_path_occupant` is gone.
4. *The shared delete tombstoner never ends another node's entry.* `tombstone_path_index` skips the tombstone when the newest committed entry at or before the delete names a DIFFERENT live node, and `tombstone_unique_entries` does the same for UNIQUE claims. A replica applying an upserts-first `ApplyRevision` (one captured by an earlier binary) therefore cannot erase the replacement's mapping either. An older upsert of the deleted node that lands later is still superseded by `tombstone_superseded_by_newer`.
5. *Decomposition dropped the delete's context.* `OpType::DeleteNodeSnapshot` gains two `#[serde(default)]` fields, `node` (the pre-delete node, carrying its workspace) and `parent_id`. The decomposer fills both, and the applicator (`applicator/delete_ops.rs`, split out of `crdt_ops.rs`) applies the op through the same body as the `ApplyRevision` change. Before, it scanned the branch for the node and resolved the parent by path at HEAD, which a pruned or renamed parent had already tombstoned, so the child stayed listed on decomposed replicas only. An id-only op from an older binary still works: it is found by scan, as the version in force at the delete, and its parent is resolved as of that version.
6. *Replicated deletes now end UNIQUE claims.* `apply_replicated_delete` ends the deleted node's UNIQUE claims from the definitions cache (`compound_marker`'s source; cold leaves them, as on the upsert side). Before, every replicated delete left the node's claims live on replicas. *(Superseded by Phase 13a: the claims are now found from the index, warm or cold.)*
Correction to the reviewers: decomposed upserts and deletes share `Low` priority, so `sort_operations_by_priority` never moved deletes after upserts. The reversed order comes from oplog entries captured before (2). Tests: `cross_branch_displacement_test` (five cases), each checked on the origin and on three replicas: the oplog as stored, decomposed, and decomposed with deletes moved last. Two mutation checks confirmed the guards matter: disabling the PATH_INDEX ownership check fails three of the cases, and dropping the carried parent fails the ORDER case. Runs: `raisin-rocksdb` `--test all` 658 passed and `--lib` 1328 passed; `raisin-replication` `--lib` 94 passed and `--test all` 33 passed.

## Phase 12 — Core LOCALIZED_NAME_INDEX (built like the internal derived indexes) — DONE (uncommitted)

**Owner decision (2026-10-04).** The index is ON BY DEFAULT for every repository: a first implementation, no rolling-upgrade constraint. This overrides the "gate the later step behind a default-OFF flag" rule for this phase, and it is why 12.0 (registration) and 12 (writers) ship in ONE release: downgrading below this release is unsupported. `RAISIN_LOCALIZED_NAME_INDEX=0` (`false`/`off`/`no`) switches it off: writers stop, builds stop, every lookup takes the fallback, and at start every state record is reset to `NotBuilt` so switching it back on rebuilds. Each branch's initial build is queued automatically in the background (unified job queue, one branch at a time, never on the boot path).

**Owner decision (2026-10-04, naming and source).** A node's localized URL segment is its TRANSLATED NODE NAME, not a "slug": the overlay pointer is `/__node_name`, the SQL column (and `UPDATE … FOR LOCALE … SET` target) is `__node_name`, and the code says node name (`indexing/localized_node_names.rs`, `NameIn`, `node_name_in`, `localized_node_names`, `NameConfig`; `raisin-context` `LocalizedNameConfig` as `RepositoryConfig.localized_names`, which keeps only `enforce_unique`; the reverse row's JSON is `{parent_id, name}`; `LocalizedNameSource::node_name`). The `url_{locale}` base-property pattern source is REMOVED with its config field, fingerprint input, config-change trigger, tests and docs. The selector is: `Hidden` overlay -> invisible; overlay `/__node_name` -> that name; else the canonical name; the default language never has localized names. Nothing under the old slug names was ever committed, so there is no compatibility alias. Text below that still describes the pattern source or the old names is the superseded design, kept as the record.

**Implemented (uncommitted, 2026-10-04).** Code: `crates/raisin-rocksdb/src/localized_name/` (`keys`, `config`, `config_change`, `rows`, `plan`, `reads`, `sync`, `catch_up`, `state`, `lookup` + `lookup/{view,paths}`, `rebuild` + `rebuild/collisions`, `auto`, `unique`, `source`), `availability`, `rebuild/node_rows`, the selector `indexing/localized_node_names.rs`, `transaction/commit/localized_names.rs`, `repositories/branches/merge/localized_after_copy.rs`, `management/async_indexing/repair/auto_targets.rs`; `raisin-context` `LocalizedNameConfig` (`RepositoryConfig.localized_names`); `raisin-storage` `localized::LocalizedNameSource` (`Storage::localized_names`); `raisin-core` `NodeService::resolve_localized_path` (`node_service/localized.rs`); SQL, HTTP, WS and JS SDK surfaces; docs `docs/website/docs/access/sql/localized-paths.md`.
- **12.0 registration.** `cf::LOCALIZED_NAME_INDEX` in `mod cf` and `all_column_families()`; `BRANCH_CF_REGISTRY` (`Copied`, `RevisionLocator::Tail` — both key shapes end with the revision); `TENANT_PREFIXED_CFS` (`storage/tenant_wipe.rs`, guarded by the integration test `tenant_wipe_lists_cf`); `storage/repo_purge.rs`; `history_gc/layout.rs` (`lname`, `lname_of`, both `Tail`); `tombstones` (step 12, `DELETION_COLUMN_FAMILIES`). The checkpoint ingestor skips and warns on a CF this binary does not know, and `open_db_with_config` opens any unknown on-disk CF generically (default options, never dropped), so a DB a newer release touched still opens here.
- **Shape.** forward `{t}\0{r}\0{b}\0{ws}\0lname\0{locale}\0{parent_id}\0{name}\0{node_id}\0{~rev}` -> empty | `T`; reverse `…\0lname_of\0{node_id}\0{locale}\0{~rev}` -> JSON `{parent_id, name}` | `T`. Fixed-tail parsers (`keys.rs`), never split on `\0`.
- **Selector** (`indexing/localized_node_names.rs`, `node_name_in` / `localized_node_names`): per locale, `Hidden` overlay -> no segment and invisible; the overlay pointer `/__node_name`; else the canonical name. It reads the node's overlays only, never the base node. The default language never has localized names (its paths are canonical). A value is trimmed of `/`; a value with inner `/` contributes its last segment. Used by every writer, the rebuild, the fallback, candidate verification, uniqueness and the SQL columns.
- **Writers** (one `sync`): `write_node_record{,_keeping_path}` (every create/update/move/reorder/copy/deep-create/transaction/merge/replication/backup-import record write; the keeping-path variant syncs the path it keeps); `translation_write::stage_version` (every overlay write: repository, transaction, copies, merge, replication apply arm, history GC — a node not yet committed is left to its own writer); `tombstones::add_node_tombstones_with_parent` (repository, cascade, transaction, merge deletion, replicated `DeleteNodeSnapshot`) and cross-branch prune (`stage_node_deleted`). Where a node and its overlays share one unreadable batch, the funnel syncs once more with the staged overlays, as a FULL put of the final view (`sync_node_final`): transaction commit (`stage_localized_names`, under the branch lock, for every node written, against the transaction's final view: written node, `moved_nodes`, cached overlays, cached paths for a parent created in the same transaction), tree copy and cross-branch copy. Merge apply does a FULL put at M (`sync_node_full`) — the copy that follows replays the source's rows below M (a delete's tombstones among them), and only a row AT M outranks them. Restore is `put_node` (covered).
- **`sync_node` under out-of-order apply.** Reverse rows are read bounded by the incoming revision; a changed locale gets a claim + reverse row at the revision and this node's previous claim a `T`; when a newer reverse row of that locale exists, the new claim is tombstoned at the next-newer row's revision (no phantom outlives the write that superseded it). Added: a write below newer state of the same node (out-of-order apply; `versionable=false` rewriting in place under a newer overlay) also re-derives the node's state as of its newest record and stages it at that revision (`catch_up.rs`), so HEAD lookups are complete whatever the arrival order.
- **A claim is a hint, the selector decides.** The lookup re-derives every candidate through the selector at the read revision — node live there, parent path, name, visibility — so a stale claim (out-of-order, a delete applied without tombstones, a checkpoint from an older peer, a collision) is never served; the index only has to be complete.
- **Lookup** (`lookup.rs`): one seek per segment per chain locale; per segment the claims of each `get_fallback_chain(L)` locale (bar the default), newest first, verified; else the canonical name via `PATH_INDEX`; a node hidden anywhere in the chain is not there. Answers `canonical_path`, `canonical_localized_path` and `redirect` (the request is not the canonical localized path: a canonical name where the node has a translated name, or a fallback locale's name). Not `Ready` (or a read below `built_from_rev`): the row-level fallback walks the parent's children (`ORDERED_CHILDREN` at the bound) through the same selector — always correct, O(children).
- **State** (`state.rs`): `{tenant}\0{repo}\0{branch}\0{ws}\0lname_state` in `INDEX_STATUS`: status, fingerprint of `(default_language, supported locales, fallback chains)`, `built_from_rev`, a generation and the collision count. Lookups use the index only while `Ready`, fingerprint-equal and at/above `built_from_rev`. A localized-name configuration change flips every record of the repository `NotBuilt` (generation + 1) in the SAME batch as the repository record, on the origin (`update_repository_config`) and in the `UpdateRepository` apply arm (both `config_change::write_repository_record`), and requests builds. A build stamps `Ready` only if the record is still `Building` under its fingerprint, pin and generation (A -> B -> A safe). Checkpoint ingest marks every record `NotBuilt` (the peer's records describe the peer) and queues builds.
- **Rebuild** = `RepairKind::LocalizedNames` (slug `localized_names`) on the repair machinery: streaming NODES of a branch, bounded batches with the resumable cursor, disk precheck, rate limit, per-node state, the admin fan-out (`POST /api/management/{repo}/repairs/localized_names`). Pinned at the branch HEAD (kept in the repair state across a resume; a resume under another fingerprint starts over); each node read as of the pin, its segments put at the revision of the newest record they derive from (node version or overlay version, at or below the pin); data-detected (a maintained index writes nothing). A collision pass counts groups with two or more VERIFIED claims (samples in the report).
- **Automatic builds** (`auto.rs`): the `node_path` auto-chain (`repair/auto_node_path.rs`) is now generic over the repair kind (`start_chain`, `continue_chain`, `schedule_chain`); `localized_names` uses it after the job system starts (45 s delay), on lookups that find a branch not built (forks, publish targets, config changes, ingests; debounced 5 s), after configuration changes and after checkpoint ingest. A branch is pending when any workspace of the repository is not `Ready` under the current fingerprint.
- **Uniqueness** (`unique.rs`): per `(branch, ws, parent, locale)` over EFFECTIVE names (a translated name, else the canonical name), enforced only when `localized_names.enforce_unique` is set AND the workspace's build is `Ready` with zero collisions; checked by a forward-claim probe (verified) plus a canonical-name probe through `PATH_INDEX` on the local write paths — transaction commit (under the branch lock), repository create/update, repository translation store, repository move and same-branch tree copy (the moved or copied root under its destination parent) — never on replication apply, merge or cross-branch promotion. Before that, the newest claim wins on every node (fallback orders the same way). Phase 13c adds the transaction overlay write (at staging) and makes every in-transaction check see the transaction's own writes by their final view.
- **Surfaces** (all through `LocalizedNameSource`, i.e. the one lookup; `NodeService::resolve_localized_path(locale, path)` adds RLS, translation and alternates):
  - SQL: `RESOLVE_PATH(workspace, locale, path) -> id` (NULL for missing, hidden or unreadable); opt-in columns `__node_name` and `__localized_path` (populated only when named, withheld from `SELECT *`); the `LocalizedPathLookup` operator for `WHERE locale = … AND __localized_path = …` (emits only when the node's canonical localized path IS the requested one — exactly the predicate). Bound parameters are substituted before analysis (`raisin_sql::substitute_params`), so `locale = $1` already reached `extract_locale_predicate` as a literal; it now also unwraps a cast (`'fr'::TEXT`).
  - HTTP: `GET /api/repository/{repo}/{branch}/head/{ws}/by-localized-path/{locale}/{*path}` -> `{node, canonical_path, canonical_localized_path, redirect, alternates, served_by}`; alternates per supported language, omitted where the node is hidden or the caller cannot read the translated node; missing/forbidden/hidden are the same 404. `PUT /api/repositories/{repo}` accepts `localized_names` (kept when omitted; the WS repository update keeps it too unless its config names it).
  - WS: `node_get_by_localized_path { locale, path }`.
  - JS SDK: `ws.nodes().getByLocalizedPath(locale, path)` (`LocalizedNode`).
- **MVCC oracle** (Phase 0b): `main` is built at setup; the French overlay of the op log also sets `/__node_name`; new template `localized_path_lookup` (`checks/localized.rs`) asks `RESOLVE_PATH` at every recorded revision (and in HEAD form) against the model's localized path — through creates, moves, renames, copies, transactions, merges with resolutions, history GC, run-collapse and replication replay (stage 3 answers through the fallback). It found the merge full-put bug and a path-at-bound bug (a node's path must be materialized as of the read, not as of its blob: `reads::node_at`) before they shipped. Chains through a node rewritten in place after the snapshot (`versionable=false`, `Snapshot.tainted`) are skipped, as the other content templates do.

**Tests (all green).** raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/localized_name/`: `lookup_tests::{lookup_at_every_revision, ancestor_move_and_rename_needs_no_rewrite, hidden_locale_hides, localized_alternates_hide_hidden_locales, rls_404_parity, fallback_to_row_scan_until_ready, sibling_uniqueness_when_enforced}`, `maintenance_tests::{carried_by_copy_cross_branch_copy_and_fork, pruned_by_cross_branch_prune, removed_on_delete, restore_version_keeps_the_translated_name, versionable_false_with_translated_names}`, `state_tests::{default_language_change_falls_back_until_rebuilt, default_language_change_rekeys, fork_is_not_ready_until_rebuilt}`, `replication_tests::{rebuilt_on_replica, removed_on_replicated_delete, default_language_change_replicated_from_peer_falls_back, out_of_order_name_apply_leaves_no_phantom, out_of_order_aba_apply_keeps_the_current_name}` (production receive path), the review regressions (below), `registration_tests::{tenant_wipe_lists_cf, downgrade_to_registration_release_opens_db, downgrade_open_previous_release}`; lib `localized_name::{keys, config}` (incl. `a_record_from_before_the_field_decodes`: every existing repository record decodes with the default) and `indexing::localized_node_names`. raisin-sql-execution `tests/all/localized_paths::{resolve_path_returns_the_id, node_name_and_localized_path_columns, localized_path_lookup_operator_and_bound_locale, a_node_name_set_through_update_for_locale_resolves}` and the oracle (`mvcc_index_oracle`, also `PROPTEST_CASES=64 ORACLE_OPS=20..60`). raisin-transport-http `http_localized_path::{a_node_by_its_localized_path, localized_names_config_is_set_and_kept_by_repository_updates}`; raisin-transport-ws lib `handlers::nodes::localized::tests`. Runs: raisin-rocksdb `--lib` (1324 passed) and `--test all` (618 passed, 13 ignored); raisin-sql-execution `--test all` (244 passed, only the 8 known failures); raisin-transport-http `--test all` (116 passed); the JS SDK type-checks (`tsc --noEmit`).

**Deviations.**
- 12.0 and 12 are one release (owner decision above): downgrade below this release is unsupported; `downgrade_open_previous_release` asserts that boundary (the previous release's CF list refuses the DB) and the forward tolerance added here (an unknown CF opens and is kept); `downgrade_to_registration_release_opens_db` opens with the registration CF list after the writers ran.
- The forward key carries the node id before the revision (the plan had it as the value): each node's claims are their own version group, so one node's `T` never masks another node's claim and history GC keeps every node's state.
- The rebuild is the `localized_names` repair kind (per branch, all workspaces), not a separate `RebuildLocalizedNameIndex` job type; it pins the branch HEAD and writes each node at the revision of its newest contributing record at or below the pin (the plan said "current revision").
- Collisions resolve newest-claim-wins (the plan's rule for concurrent replicated claims) rather than lowest `created_at`; the fallback orders candidates the same way.
- The fallback is the same selector over the parent's children, not a `url_{locale}` property-index lookup.
- Checkpoint ingest marks every state `NotBuilt` (and queues builds) instead of revalidating it.
- Dropped with the pattern source (owner decision): the optional guarded rewrite of `properties->>'url_fr'::String = $1` onto the index. Not done: `raisin.nodes.getByLocalizedPath` for functions (needs the bindings generator round trip, `make gen-bindings`); the SDK's `localizedPaths(id)` (alternates come with `getByLocalizedPath`).
- `restore_version_keeps_the_translated_name` performs the restore funnel's write (a transaction `put_node` of the version's content read by time travel) rather than `NodeService::restore_version`, whose manual-version listing needs the async snapshot job.
- `removed_on_delete_and_replicated_delete` is two tests: `maintenance_tests::removed_on_delete`, `replication_tests::removed_on_replicated_delete`.
- Owed operational gate: the prod-snapshot dry run of the rebuild (`POST /api/management/{repo}/repairs/localized_names` with `dry_run`).
- Noted, pre-existing, not touched: raisin-sql `--lib` has 130 failing tests (`FROM nodes` is `TableNotFound` once the default catalog registers the `default` workspace, `analyzer/semantic/from_clause.rs`).
- `RepositoryConfig` is msgpack-POSITIONAL: never put `skip_serializing_if` on its fields (a skipped `Option` shifted a later field into the wrong slot during this work). `localized_names` is its LAST field.

**Review fixes (2026-10-04).** Two reviewers' findings, each verified against the code, fixed in the shared path and pinned by a test named after the failure (`tests/all/localized_name/{regression,merge_chain,unique}_tests.rs`, `replication_tests`):
1. *Final-view re-syncs were deltas against the stored rows*, blind to what the record and overlay writers had staged at the same revision in the same batch. They are FULL puts now (`plan::Prior { full }`: every locale an earlier partial-view sync can have touched is re-staged, a reverse `T` included). The failure shown (an `url_fr` -> overlay migration) needed the removed pattern source; the overlay-only shapes are covered by `one_transaction_move_and_rename_serves_the_final_name` and the concurrency test below.
2. *Catch-up diffed against the stored rows only* — in an A-B-A out-of-order apply the late write's rows (same batch) were invisible and it stayed HEAD. Catch-up now overlays the main plan's staged rows (`Prior { staged }`). `out_of_order_aba_apply_keeps_the_current_name`.
3. *The rebuild wrote at the newest LIVE input* and diffed below newer rows (a deleted overlay's, a catch-up's, a merge's, a peer's): it skipped the node or tombstoned its own claim, then stamped `Ready`. It now writes at `max(node version, any TRANSLATION_DATA version incl. deletes, newest reverse row)` at or below the pin. `rebuild_over_a_newer_stored_row_keeps_the_rebuilt_claim`.
4. + 10. *Merge copied the source's rows and left the target's state alone.* After the copy, every node the source changed since the merge base is re-synced at M from the merged view (a full put; a deleted one gets its tombstones), and a `Ready` target workspace whose source is not `Ready` is invalidated and rebuilt (`merge/localized_after_copy.rs`). `merge_restores_names_the_source_rows_lack`, `merge_from_unbuilt_source_flips_target`.
5. *Commit staging ran outside the branch lock.* `stage_localized_names` now runs under it (after `update_branch_head`, beside `revalidate_staged_index_writes`) for every node written, as a full put plus catch-up against every earlier commit; uniqueness is probed there too. `concurrent_commits_keep_the_head_name`. Repository-layer writes (`add_impl`/`update_impl`) still do not take the branch lock — their diff is still against the rows they read.
6. *No generation guard.* `begin_build` gives every run a fresh generation above any record of the branch, kept in the resumable epoch (`{pin}|{fingerprint}|{generation}`); `finish_build` stamps only records still carrying it. `stale_build_cannot_stamp_ready_after_a_newer_begin`.
7. The owner decision above (naming, pattern removal).
8. *Only `node_path` links continued their chain.* `repair::after_link` continues every automatic chain (`node_path` gated on its flag, `localized_names` on the index flag) and records a failed link, which `enqueue_next` skips until the next start; `request_build` queues the REQUESTED branch directly (a standalone job, never a chain link) before starting the chain, debounced per branch. `localized_names_chain_continues_to_every_pending_branch`.
9. *An unresolvable parent was warn-and-skip.* Deep create, backup import and the repository move's descendant rewrites pass the parent id they know; a sync that still cannot resolve the parent of a node with a name to claim invalidates the workspace's build and requests one. With the pattern source gone a fresh node has no name, so the hole needs a node with overlays: `unresolvable_parent_invalidates_the_build`.
11. *A re-created branch inherited `Ready`.* `forget_branch_rebuild_state` (branch delete, create under a deleted name, and their replicated arms) also deletes the branch's `lname_state` records. `deleted_branch_recreated_is_not_ready`.
12. *Builds ran with the index switched off.* `rebuild_branch` refuses while `RAISIN_LOCALIZED_NAME_INDEX` is off (not testable in-process: the flag is read once per process).
13. *Uniqueness holes* (canonical names, move, same-branch copy): see Uniqueness above. `translated_name_equal_to_a_sibling_canonical_name_is_refused`, `canonical_name_equal_to_a_sibling_translated_name_is_refused`, `move_and_copy_into_a_colliding_parent_are_refused`. A collision brought in by replication or merge still does not revoke enforcement until the next build counts it.
14. *Every record write paid for the index.* `sync` first probes two key prefixes (the node's `TRANSLATION_DATA` and reverse rows); a node with neither returns before the config read, and the catch-up loads the node body only when its newest record revision (keys only) is above the write. `untranslated_writes_touch_no_index_rows`.
- LOW: checkpoint ingest marks the state `NotBuilt` BEFORE the copy too, and propagates a failure of the post-copy mark (the peer's `Ready` would otherwise stay in force).


No functions, triggers or workflow engine are involved. It is maintained inline in the write batch at every funnel and rebuilt by a native job.

**Prerequisites.**
- Phase 11, Phase 2.8 (baseline reader for `sync_node`), Phase 7 item 8 (tombstone at `max(R, …)`).
- **Phase 12.0 — CF registration release.** One release **before** any writer: declare `localized_name_index` as an empty CF in `all_column_families`, and make the checkpoint ingestor skip and warn on unknown CFs. Alternatively that release opens every CF from `DB::list_cf` generically. An older binary cannot open a DB containing an unknown CF and cannot ingest a checkpoint carrying one. **Downgrading below the registration release is unsupported**; downgrading to it from the writer release is supported.
- Prod-snapshot dry run of the rebuild.

### Shape

New CF `localized_name_index`, branch-scoped, with two key families:
- forward `{t}\0{r}\0{b}\0{ws}\0lname\0{locale}\0{parent_id}\0{name}\0{~rev}` → `node_id | T` (parent_id is `/` at the root, the ORDERED_CHILDREN convention);
- reverse `{t}\0{r}\0{b}\0{ws}\0lname_of\0{node_id}\0{locale}\0{~rev}` → `(parent_id, name) | T`.

The reverse key lets any writer tombstone the previous forward key without reading the old overlay. Keys have fixed-tail parsers and are never split on `\0`.

### Name source

One selector, `localized_node_names(overlays_by_locale, repo_cfg) -> Map<locale, name>`, placed next to the walker in `indexing/`. It is used by every writer and by the rebuild, and it has ONE source: the reserved overlay pointer `/__node_name` in TRANSLATION_DATA (the translated node name), which gets history, fork, copy and delete for free. (The design originally also read a base-node property named by a repository-level `url_{locale}` pattern; the owner removed it on 2026-10-04.)

Selection is structural (RepositoryConfig is already loaded and replicated), with no NodeType resolution on the write path.

### Lookup

`/a/b/c` in locale L walks the segments with **one seek per segment** and stops at the first missing one. Per segment:
1. Try each locale in `get_fallback_chain(L)`.
2. If none matches, try the base name through PATH_INDEX, but only if that node has no translated name of its own in L (one reverse probe).
3. Otherwise answer `canonical_localized_path`, so the caller can issue a 301.

RLS is applied to the resolved node. Missing, forbidden and hidden-in-locale all return the same 404.

Moving or renaming an ancestor writes nothing here. Moving a node writes one forward and one reverse row per locale for the moved root only.

### Uniqueness

Per `(branch, ws, parent, locale)` over effective names, checked by a forward-key probe in the same batch, the way UNIQUE_INDEX does it.
- Enforcement is **off** per repository until a clean rebuild has reported zero collisions.
- Until then, collisions resolve deterministically (lowest `created_at`, then id) and are listed in the admin UI.
- Concurrent replicated claims: the highest `~rev` wins on every replica.

### Registration checklist

- `lib.rs` (`mod cf`, `all_column_families`) — **in the 12.0 release**;
- checkpoint ingestor: skip-and-warn on unknown CFs — **in the 12.0 release**;
- `branches/cf_registry.rs` (Copied, `RevisionLocator::Tail`);
- `storage/tenant_wipe.rs` (`TENANT_PREFIXED_CFS`; not test-enforced, so add a test that it is listed);
- `storage/repo_purge.rs`;
- `history_gc/layout.rs`;
- `tombstones/mod.rs`;
- optional `prefix_transform.rs` extractor, only after the Phase 15 iterator audit.

### Write funnels

All of them call one `localized_name::sync_node(batch, scope, node_id, parent_id, new_selector_output, rev)`:
- **create/update:** `put_node.rs`, `add_node.rs`, `crud/create/add.rs`, `crud/update.rs`, `batch/write_batch.rs`, `deep_create.rs`;
- **translation:** `transaction/context/translations/write.rs`, `repositories/translations/nodes.rs`;
- **move:** both `move_tree.rs` (root only);
- **delete:** `transaction/.../delete.rs`, `crud/delete/tombstone.rs`, `cascade/{single,tree}.rs`, `tombstones/index_tombstones.rs`;
- **copy:** `copy/{tree,single}.rs`, `cross_branch/{stage,translations,prune}.rs`;
- **merge:** `merge/apply.rs` (full put at M, never a delta; Phase 7 item 3);
- **replication apply:** `node_operations/{create_node,move_rename}.rs`, `applicator/{crdt_ops,legacy_node_ops,move_node_ops}.rs`, plus the Phase 11 translation arm;
- **restore:** **put_node** (`NodeService::restore_version` → put_node, `node_service/versioning.rs:227-264`). `jobs/handlers/restore_tree.rs` is not a funnel: it delegates to an executor the server wires as `None` (`main.rs:954-958`), so RESTORE TREE currently fails. If it is wired later, it must call `sync_node`;
- **config change:** see Rebuild and state.

**`sync_node` semantics under out-of-order apply.** It reads the reverse rows **bounded by the incoming revision** (newest ≤ rev), not the newest row. When it applies below the latest reverse row, it also writes a tombstone for its own forward key at the **next-newer reverse row's revision**, so the newer state stays authoritative. Otherwise an older replicated name write leaves a live forward key that no newer revision ever tombstones (a phantom claim). Tombstones follow `max(R, newest existing entry revision for that group)` for `versionable=false` nodes, because a name from an overlay at `r_t > R` is not masked by a tombstone at R.

### Rebuild and state

- Job `RebuildLocalizedNameIndex` per `(repo, branch, ws)`, through `JobRegistry` + `JobDataStore`, streaming per the repair discipline.
- It walks pre-order and writes at each node's current revision.
- **State key** `{tenant}\0{repo}\0{branch}\0{ws}\0lname_state` (tenant-first, and including repo, branch and workspace: INDEX_STATUS is `SkippedOnPurpose` on fork, `cf_registry.rs:266-267`, so a shorter key would let a fork or publish branch alias another branch's Ready flag). It records `built_from_rev` **and a fingerprint of `(default_language, supported locales, fallback chain)`**. Lookups fail closed on a fingerprint mismatch (the compound template).
- **Config changes flip the state to `NotBuilt` in the same write batch as the config change**, on the origin and in the replication apply arm for `UpdateRepository`, and enqueue the rebuild from both (the "local + remote" side of `node_handlers.rs:133/162`). A default-language change replicated from a peer otherwise leaves the state Ready and lookups serve stale names until the rebuild drains.
- **Per-branch rebuild required** after fork, after cross-branch publish target creation, and after a merge into a branch that is not Ready.
- The planner and the lookup are gated fail-closed: until the state is Ready with a matching fingerprint, and for any `max_revision < built_from_rev`, they **fall back to the row-level lookup** (the parent's children through the selector), so the migration window is never empty.
- Any in-process state cache registers with `derived_cache_registry`.
- Every node runs its own rebuild under a per-node lease at most; admin-triggered rebuilds use the fan-out endpoint.
- **Checkpoint.** The state record and the CF arrive from the peer; revalidate the state after ingest (fingerprint against the local RepositoryConfig, `built_from_rev` against local history), and treat a mismatch as `NotBuilt`. Across mixed versions, exclude the CF from checkpoints or version-gate it.

### Surfaces

All of them call **one** core function, `NodeService::resolve_localized_path(ws, locale, path, at, auth)`.
- **SQL:**
  - virtual columns `__node_name` and `__localized_path`, populated only when a locale is in scope and only when projected;
  - a new physical operator `LocalizedPathLookup` (in the style of `point_lookup.rs`) for `WHERE locale = 'fr' AND __localized_path = $1`;
  - a scalar `RESOLVE_PATH(workspace, locale, path) -> id`;
  - `extract_locale_predicate` accepts bound parameters (`predicates.rs:308`);
- **HTTP:** `GET /api/repository/{repo}/{branch}/head/{ws}/by-localized-path/{locale}/{*path}`, returning the translated node plus `canonical_path`, `canonical_localized_path` and `alternates{locale: path}` for hreflang. **`alternates` is filtered by per-locale visibility and RLS**: a locale where the node is hidden, or a path the caller cannot read, is omitted, so alternates cannot leak paths. Short-circuit the default language and cache RepositoryConfig.
- **WS:** `node_get_by_localized_path { locale, path }`, plus an optional `locale` on `node_get`/`list`.
- **SDK:** `ws.nodes().getByLocalizedPath(locale, path)` and `localizedPaths(id)`.
- **Functions:** `raisin.nodes.getByLocalizedPath`.
- **Docs:** `docs/website/docs/access/sql/localized-paths.md`.

### Tests

One per row of the maintenance matrix, asserting lookup and absence after the operation:
- `lookup_at_every_revision`;
- `ancestor_move_and_rename_needs_no_rewrite`;
- `carried_by_copy_cross_branch_copy_and_fork`;
- `pruned_by_cross_branch_prune`;
- `removed_on_delete_and_replicated_delete`;
- `rebuilt_on_replica`;
- `default_language_change_rekeys`, `default_language_change_falls_back_until_rebuilt`;
- `default_language_change_replicated_from_peer_falls_back`;
- `fork_is_not_ready_until_rebuilt`;
- `hidden_locale_hides`, `localized_alternates_hide_hidden_locales`;
- `sibling_uniqueness_when_enforced`;
- `fallback_to_row_scan_until_ready`;
- `rls_404_parity`;
- `restore_version_keeps_the_translated_name`;
- `versionable_false_with_translated_names`;
- `out_of_order_name_apply_leaves_no_phantom` (also in the interleaved-oplog oracle);
- `tenant_wipe_lists_cf`;
- `downgrade_to_registration_release_opens_db`.

Add the template to the 0b oracle.

### Rollback, gain, risk

**Rollback.** Turn the feature flag off and lookups use the fallback. The CF is **not** inert to older binaries: rollback is supported only down to the 12.0 registration release. Below it, the previous binary refuses to open the DB.

**Expected gain** (harness I). Localized URL resolution costs `depth × 1–2` seeks, independent of workspace size, instead of a property-index scan whose cost grows with edit history. Customers no longer need the `url_xx` hack.

**Risk.** High surface area (about 20 write sites). It is mitigated by the single `sync_node`, the per-site tests, the fail-closed fingerprinted gate and the registration-first rollout. Effort L, 2–3 weeks, plus one release of lead time for 12.0.

## Phase 13 — `REWRITE_LINKS()`: rich-text link rewriting in SQL — PLANNED

**Prerequisites.** Phases 3 and 4, which provide the shared batched, RLS-aware loader.

### Function

`REWRITE_LINKS(value [, options jsonb])` returns the same type it receives: TEXT in gives TEXT out, JSONB in gives JSONB out. It is non-deterministic.

**Accepted link forms:**
- HTML `<a href="raisin://{ws}/@{id}">`: the id form, stable across moves, preferred;
- `raisin://{ws}/{path}`: the path form, compatible with MCP;
- ProseMirror link marks carrying the existing `{"raisin:ref", "raisin:workspace"}` envelope.

All other hrefs are left byte-identical.

**Options:**
- `locale`: defaults to the row's resolution locale;
- `url`: a template over `{locale}`, `{path}`, `{rel}`, `{id}`, `{ws}`, and `{localized_path}` once Phase 12 is Ready;
- `strip`;
- `default_locale_prefix`;
- `broken`: one of `unlink | mark | keep_text`;
- `fields`;
- `keep_ref`.

### Semantics

- Missing, deleted, RLS-denied, hidden-in-locale and absent-on-this-branch targets are all "broken" and produce identical output.
- Publish awareness comes only from querying the publish branch.
- Output passes a scheme allowlist, and attributes are HTML-escaped.
- Id links resolve through the Phase 4/10 path rule at the snapshot, never through stored `raisin:path`, which goes stale after a move.

### Implementation

- New `raisin-core/src/services/link_resolver.rs` over the Phase 4 batch read, using `PropertiesMode::Skip` when RLS has no property conditions (and never the path-only skip under graph RLS, as in Phase 3).
- `raisin-sql-execution/src/physical_plan/eval/functions/links/{mod,html_scan,prosemirror,url_template}.rs`, using a minimal `<a` tokenizer (no new dependency).
- Signatures registered in `raisin-sql/src/analyzer/functions/builtins_hierarchy.rs`.
- Async dispatch in `async_eval.rs`.
- Cross-row chunking from Phase 4.
- Optional 13b: `links: Option<RichTextLinks>` on both `RichTextFieldConfig` structs (serde default), used only to select fields for `REWRITE_LINKS(properties)` through the `WalkCursor` selector. Run `cargo test -p raisin-models --lib --no-run` plus dependents.

### Tests and rollout

**Tests** (in `raisin-sql-execution/tests/all/rewrite_links_tests.rs`):
- an id link after a MOVE gets the new path;
- a path link after a move is broken;
- the locale prefix, and hidden in `fr` means broken;
- RLS-denied output equals missing output, including under graph RLS;
- deleted target;
- unpublished target is absent on the publish branch;
- `rev/{n}` uses the historical path;
- 50 rows sharing 3 targets cost 3 fetches;
- HTML edge cases: quotes, entities, uppercase `HREF`, `javascript:` untouched, malformed tags pass through;
- ProseMirror marks;
- `REWRITE_LINKS` on plain text with no links is byte-identical.

**Docs:** `docs/website/docs/access/sql/rich-text-links.md`.

**Migration.** None. It is read-only, and existing content passes through unchanged. It becomes useful once Studio's link picker writes the id form (decision D8).

**Rollback.** Remove the function.

**Expected gain.** Studio drops its client-side link resolution. One statement renders a page with its links at a fixed cost per distinct target.

**Risk.** Low.

## Phase 13a — Promotion stale-placement erasure + replica UNIQUE on a cold definitions cache — DONE (uncommitted)

**Owner request (2026-10-04).** The two gaps the Phase 11d review fixes deferred, plus the UNIQUE fixture they skipped. Nothing new is emitted or stored, so there is no later step to gate and no flag: every change is in how one node orders and checks the writes it already made.

**1. Promotion move-out / move-in.** PATH_INDEX and UNIQUE keys carry no node id, and every write of a promotion lands at one revision R, so a node vacating a path or a value and another node taking it write the SAME key; a WriteBatch applies in order, and the last write wins. Three faults, all fixed structurally in the one funnel (`repositories/nodes/queries/copy/cross_branch/`):
- *The stale-placement tombstone was staged per entry.* With A moving out of `/p` and B moving (or being created) into it, B staged first, A's `/p` tombstone landed after B's mapping and `/p` resolved to nothing on the origin. Staging is now TWO PASSES over the entries: the retire pass (`prepare.rs`, after the `delete_missing` prune) stages everything any entry ENDS — a displaced occupant, its own previous PATH_INDEX and ORDERED_CHILDREN placement, the UNIQUE claims it gives up (`UniqueHalf::Ends`) — for EVERY entry, then the put pass (`stage.rs`) writes every node (`UniqueHalf::Puts`). The outcome no longer depends on the order the source tree lists its nodes in.
- *A mover was taken for a displaced occupant.* Staged A-first, B's same-path check found A at `/p` in committed state and RETIRED it: A was deleted on every replica (a `Delete` change) and lost index entries on the origin. The displacement check now skips an occupant that is itself in the promoted set (`CopyScope::src_ids`): it is moving away, not displaced.
- *The commit-time correction erased claims.* Every promotion node is re-derived at commit (`NodeCommit`, `StagedDeltaCheck::always`), and the correction is APPENDED after the whole batch — so a pruned or displaced node's `end_claims_at`, or a giver's `write_unique_delta`, tombstoned the value its replacement had just put at R. This was live on the origin despite Phase 11d review fix 1 (the prune moved before the puts, the correction after them again); the new UNIQUE fixture shows it. `NodeCommit::revalidate` and the transaction's `revalidate_staged_index_writes` now collect every node's final claims first (`CommitClaims`, `StagedDeltaCheck::hold_claims`, from the definitions cache) and the correction never ends a claim another node of the same commit holds at that revision (`revalidate_final_held`).
- *Replicas.* PATH_INDEX was already protected (`path_owned_at` on the upsert side, the delete tombstoner's ownership check). UNIQUE was not: a replica applies one node per batch, so with the taker's upsert first the giver's `write_unique_delta` tombstoned the taker's claim. Every claim-ending tombstone now goes through one check, `unique_delta::end_claim`: skip when committed state already holds, AT the tombstone's exact revision, a live claim naming another node (`unique_guard::held_by_other_at`; see review fix 1), or when another node of the commit holds it (`CommitClaims`). `write_unique_delta` is now `write_unique_ends` + `write_unique_puts`; `end_claims_at` goes through the same check.
- *Checked, no hazard* (keyed by node id): NODE_PATH, ORDERED_CHILDREN (the key ends with the child id; the stale-slot tombstone moved to the retire pass anyway, beside the path), PROPERTY / REFERENCE / SPATIAL / COMPOUND (their stale tombstones stay with the node's put), and the localized name index (forward key carries the node id; `sync_node_final` is per node).

**2. Replica UNIQUE on a cold definitions cache.** Chosen: *end every claim the version owns, found from the index*, not "load the definitions before the batch" and not a repair marker. `unique_guard::owned_unique_names(db, ctx, node, at)` probes, for each top-level property of the version, the claim key `(node_type, name, hash)` and keeps the names whose newest entry at or before `at` is a live claim naming THIS node — one seek per top-level property, no NodeType read (so nothing near the `fulltext/batch.rs:152-161` deadlock), no cache, and exact: a claim exists only for a property some version declared `unique: true`, and only the owner's claim is ended. Loading the definitions would still be wrong when they changed (a dropped `unique: true` leaves claims nobody ends); a marker would leave the claim live until a repair that does not exist for UNIQUE (nothing rebuilds UNIQUE_INDEX). It is correct on every node, warm or cold, with no rollout.
- `apply_replicated_delete` (the `Delete` change and the decomposed `DeleteNodeSnapshot`, one body) ends the owned claims ALWAYS — it no longer consults the cache at all.
- The cold upsert (`compound_marker::cold_unique_delta`) CAN leave a stale claim: a claim written while warm, then an update applied cold, kept the old value claimed forever. It now diffs the replaced version's OWNED claims against the new version through the one writer (`write_unique_delta`, plus `end_claims_at` at the first successor out of order): a changed value is ended and the new value claimed (its property is known unique by the prior's claim). A retyped node keeps none (claims are keyed by type). A property whose prior held no claim here still gets none — that is a missing claim (the pre-Phase-8 replica behaviour), never a stale one.

**3. UNIQUE fixture.** `tests/all/promotion_unique_test.rs` (`account_type`: `test:Account` with `unique: true` `email`, about 70 lines), asserting the raw newest UNIQUE_INDEX entry (`claim_owner`) directly.

**Tests (green).** raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/`, each promotion case on the origin AND three replicas (the oplog as stored, decomposed, decomposed with deletes moved last):
- `promotion_placement_test::{a_move_in_staged_before_the_move_out_keeps_the_path, a_move_out_staged_before_the_move_in_keeps_both_nodes, a_node_created_into_a_vacated_path_keeps_it_in_both_orders, a_swap_of_two_paths_in_one_promotion}`: both paths resolve and their raw PATH_INDEX entries name the new owners, both nodes live at their new paths, the parent lists exactly both, and the promotion's `ApplyRevision` deletes nothing.
- `promotion_unique_test::{a_value_handed_over_in_one_promotion_names_its_new_owner_in_both_orders, a_displaced_nodes_claim_names_its_replacement, a_pruned_nodes_claim_names_its_replacement}` (UNIQUE for handover, displacement and prune) and `{a_replicated_delete_ends_the_claim_warm_or_cold, a_cold_replicated_update_moves_the_claim}` (replica delete and cold update, as stored and decomposed, the cache dropped with `defs::invalidate_database`).
- Mutation checks, each confirmed to fail the named tests: single-pass staging (move-in-first, create, swap); no mover filter (all four placement tests, via the deleted mover); no `CommitClaims` (displacement, prune and handover UNIQUE on the ORIGIN); no committed ownership check (handover on `replica-raw`); the old cache-only replica delete (cold delete); no cold delta (cold update).
- Runs: raisin-rocksdb `--test all` 667 passed, 13 ignored; `--lib` 1328 passed, 3 ignored; raisin-sql-execution `--test all mvcc_index_oracle` 9 passed (5 ignored debug).

**Review fixes (2026-10-05).** Two reviewers found seven defects; all were real and are fixed in the shared code paths:
1. *Claim-ending was not commutative.* `owned_by_other` skipped a tombstone whenever the newest claim at or before it named another node at ANY revision, so whether a peer wrote it depended on which concurrent peer ops it had applied: two replicas holding the same ops kept different claims. The committed check is now a same-revision KEY COLLISION only (`held_by_other_at`), the one case the hand-over needs; a tombstone over another node's older claim is what every peer writes, so they converge. `tombstone_unique_entries` (repository, cascade, prune, merge, replicated delete) is now a loop over `end_claim`, the one body.
2. *A replicated delete probed only the carried version.* With a concurrent update from another origin, this replica's version at the delete differs from the one the deleting origin saw, and the newer value's claim outlived the node here. `apply_replicated_delete` now probes the carried version AND the version stored here at or before the delete (`node_version_at_or_before`).
3. *`owned_unique_names` asked for the NEWEST entry,* so on a cold replica the giver of a promotion's swap or hand-over, applied after its taker, found the taker's entry at R and claimed nothing for its new value. It now asks whether THIS node has held the claim at or before the revision (`mvcc_read::any_at_or_before`), which also makes the replicated delete (2) independent of other nodes' entries; protection of the taker stays with `end_claim`.
4. *Merge resolution erased placements and claims* (two findings): `write_resolved_node` tombstoned every superseded old path unconditionally and every superseded claim against committed state only, at M, so it erased another resolution's mapping at the same M, a node the target created at the base path, and the source entries the merge copy replays below M afterwards. A resolution now asks the MERGED view (`merge::merged_view`: target at or before M, source at or before its HEAD, target wins a tie): an old path is ended only while that view still names the node (`indexing::key_owner`, the replica's rule); a claim the view gives to another live node is recorded as held at M (`CommitClaims`) and spared by the resolution's tombstoner AND its commit-time correction (`NodeCommit::hold_external`). The deletion resolution passes the same held claims.
5. *Promotion's stale-path tombstone had no ownership check on the origin,* while replicas used `path_owned_at`: on a target with a stranded duplicate the origin orphaned the path's owner and replicas kept it. `path_owned_at` is lifted into `indexing::key_owner` and is the one check for the replicated upsert, promotion (`tombstone_stale_placement`) and merge.
6. *The transaction delete hand-rolled its UNIQUE tombstones* (and the `unique: true` walk) with no ownership check. It now uses `extract_unique_property_names` + `tombstone_unique_entries`.
7. *(LOW)* The hold pass and the correction pass peeked the definitions cache separately, so a type warming in between ran a correction whose taker claims were never held. `CommitClaims` now remembers the types that were cold in the hold pass, and the correction skips UNIQUE for any of them (one snapshot per commit). The other LOW (the copied predicate in `tombstone_unique_entries`) is item 1.

Tests (raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/`): `unique_claim_convergence_test::{two_origins_converge_on_a_claim_whatever_order_the_ops_arrive (update, repository delete, transaction delete; raw entries equal on both peers), a_replicated_delete_ends_the_claim_of_a_concurrent_update_it_never_saw (warm and cold)}`, `promotion_unique_cold_test::{a_cold_replica_claims_both_values_of_a_swap, a_cold_replica_claims_the_givers_new_value_of_a_handover_in_both_orders}` (replicas cold from just before the promotion, via the new `replicas_with`), `promotion_placement_test::a_promoted_move_leaves_a_stranded_occupants_path_alone_on_origin_and_replicas`, `merge_placement_test::{a_resolution_keeps_the_path_and_claim_a_source_node_took_from_it, two_resolutions_at_one_merge_revision_keep_the_path_in_both_orders, a_resolution_leaves_a_node_the_target_created_at_its_base_path}`. Each was confirmed to fail with its fix reverted. Runs: raisin-rocksdb `--test all` 675 passed, 13 ignored; `--lib` 1328 passed, 3 ignored; raisin-sql-execution `--test all mvcc_index_oracle` 9 passed.

Known limits left (not regressions):
- The merged view decides whether to END a key, never re-asserts one. When the TARGET's own entry for the vacated path or value is newer than the source node's that took it, the resolution still tombstones (the target entry is this node's) and the source node's older entry stays masked; the full answer is re-asserting the other owner at M. Likewise a merge DELETION resolution still ends its old paths through the shared delete tombstoner, which checks the target only.
- PATH_INDEX ownership checks (`path_owned_at`, the delete tombstoner) remain cross-revision, as the findings asked; fix 1's commutativity argument applies to them in principle for concurrent multi-origin moves.

**Deviations.**
- The helpers the new tests share (`by_path`, `path_owner_raw`, `replicas`, …) are made `pub(crate)` in `cross_branch_displacement_test.rs` rather than copied; so are `merge_apply_funnel_test`'s `Env` and `node`.
- `CommitClaims` records a node's claims at its check's revision. An IN-PLACE write whose claim is placed at a newer revision (`claim_revision`) and a node a `NodeCommit` only `touch`es (no final version: fresh-id copies) are not recorded; neither occurs in a promotion, and a fresh copy taking a value its source still holds is refused by the uniqueness check anyway.
- Not changed: a multi-node TRANSACTION still stages each node's UNIQUE ends and puts in call order.
