A hand-over within one transaction in taker-first order is refused by `check_unique_constraints` (it reads the committed claim the giver still holds), so the hazard cannot be reached there; the commit correction is covered by `CommitClaims`.
- The displaced occupant's `Deleted` change is now reported in the retire pass, ahead of the copied nodes' changes (it used to sit just before its replacement's).

## Phase 13b — Release-build profiling: SQL point-lookup overhead, RESOLVE and localized-path hot spots — DONE (uncommitted)

**Owner request (2026-10-04).** Real speed for (a) a SQL point lookup by path, by id and by an indexed property, (b) RESOLVE over a page's references, (c) a localized path lookup. Earlier numbers were DEBUG builds (a point lookup ~200 µs vs ~29 µs for the storage get) and nobody had profiled the cause. Owner override for this phase only: a release build for the measurement harness, one target at a time, `target/release` deleted afterwards (done).

**Harness** (release, macOS, in-memory-resident RocksDB; p50/p95 over 400 runs after 10 warm-ups; `BENCH_REPS`, `BENCH_M`):
- `tests/all/index_read_bench_point.rs` — **P**: 2,000 page-sized docs; one row by path, by id and by `properties->>'slug'::String` through SQL (`SELECT *`, `SELECT id`, `LIMIT 1`, the `execute_batch` entry the transports use) and through the storage API (`get_by_path`, `get`, `find_by_property_with_limit` + `get`); plus **fresh SQL text** cases (another node's path every run), i.e. what a statement costs when no cache can help.
- `tests/all/index_read_bench_point_resolve.rs` — **R**: `RESOLVE(properties, 1|2)` over a page with 50 references (each target references one more node: 50 / 100 inlined) against the storage floor (the same nodes read with `get_many_for_read`, one batch per level, plus the page `get`); **L**: a localized path lookup at depth 3 and 6 (20 siblings per level, every chain node with a French `__node_name`, the index built and asserted `served_by = index`): `WHERE locale = 'fr' AND __localized_path = …` (`*` and `id`), `RESOLVE_PATH`, and `WHERE locale = 'fr' AND path = <canonical>`, against `LocalizedNameSource::resolve` + `get` and the canonical `get_by_path`.
- Profiling: `BENCH_HOT=<case> BENCH_HOT_SECS=<n>` loops one case after measuring it and prints `HOT <case> pid=…`; the profiles below are macOS `sample <pid> 7` (samply / cargo-flamegraph are not installed), call trees aggregated per frame (inclusive, recursion-deduplicated).
- Scenario I (`properties->>'url_fr'`) is obsolete — the `url_{locale}` source was removed in Phase 12 — and is replaced by L.

**Results** (release; µs p50 / p95; ratio = SQL p50 ÷ its storage counterpart's p50; "before" = this phase's starting code, "after" = with every fix below; "no cache" = after, with `RAISIN_SQL_PLAN_CACHE=0`):

| case | storage | before | after | after, no cache |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| by path, `SELECT id` | 6.0 | 53.6 / 54.5 (9.0×) | **10.3 / 10.5 (1.7×)** | 23.1 (3.7×) |
| by path, `SELECT *` | 6.0 | 111.3 / 114.9 (18.7×) | **19.7 / 20.3 (3.3×)** | 54.9 (8.8×) |
| by path, `SELECT *`, `execute_batch` | 6.0 | (= `execute`) | 19.8 / 20.2 (3.3×) | 55.3 |
| by path, fresh text, `SELECT id` | 6.0 | ≈53.6 (no cache existed) | 25.4 / 29.5 (4.3×) | 24.5 |
| by path, fresh text, `SELECT *` | 6.0 | ≈111 | 58.2 / 62.4 (9.8×) | 56.9 |
| by id, `SELECT id` | 5.2 | 52.0 / 53.2 (10.1×) | **9.2 / 9.4 (1.8×)** | 21.4 |
| by id, `SELECT *` | 5.2 | 109.4 / 113.2 (21.2×) | **18.7 / 19.2 (3.6×)** | 53.3 |
| by property, `SELECT *` | 7.0 | 123.5 / 131.1 (17.4×) | **27.1 / 31.3 (3.9×)** | 64.8 |
| by property, `SELECT *` `LIMIT 1` | 7.0 | 122.8 / 128.8 | 27.2 / 29.2 (3.9×) | 66.3 |
| RESOLVE depth 1 (50 inlined) | 313 | 721 / 758 (2.3×) | **672 / 701 (2.2×)** | 693 |
| RESOLVE depth 2 (100 inlined) | 542 | 1283 / 1533 (2.4×) | **1211 / 1268 (2.2×)** | 1241 |
| localized storage resolve + get, depth 3 | — | 33.6 | **28.9 / 30.6** | — |
| localized storage resolve + get, depth 6 | — | 53.3 / 56.0 | **43.2 / 47.2** | — |
| canonical `get_by_path`, depth 3 / 6 | — | 7.6 / 5.9 | 7.8 / 5.8 | — |
| `locale='fr' AND __localized_path`, `id`, depth 3 | 28.9 | 117.5 / 120.7 | **37.9 / 40.2 (1.3×)** | 54.2 |
| same, `*`, depth 3 | 28.9 | 174.9 / 179.9 | **47.4 / 48.7 (1.6×)** | 86.1 |
| same, `id`, depth 6 | 43.2 | 157.5 / 165.8 | **53.3 / 56.2 (1.2×)** | 69.6 |
| same, `*`, depth 6 | 43.2 | 215.4 / 222.7 | **62.7 / 65.9 (1.5×)** | 101.3 |
| `RESOLVE_PATH`, depth 3 / 6 | 28.9 / 43.2 | 95.7 / 115.1 | **41.3 / 56.9** | 59.6 / 75.7 |
| `locale='fr' AND path=<canonical>`, `*`, depth 3 / 6 | 7.8 / 5.8 | 119.5 / 116.7 | **26.1 / 24.0** | 63.5 / 62.0 |

Two runs agreed within ±3 %. Target "SQL point lookup within ~2–3× of the storage get": met for a repeated statement (`SELECT id` 1.7–1.8×, `SELECT *` 3.3–3.9×, the wide `*` projection is the remainder); a never-seen SQL text is 4× / 10× (see Remaining).

**Profile evidence and fixes** (each fix re-measured before the next; sample counts are the bench thread's):
1. *Optimizer change detection formatted the whole plan.* `Optimizer::optimize` compared `format!("{:?}", plan)` before and after every pass — every table schema rendered twice per pass. Baseline `SELECT *` by path, 6,410 samples: `Optimizer::optimize` 2,732 (43 %), of which `LogicalPlan as Debug` 1,524 (24 %). Now structural equality: `PartialEq` derived on `LogicalPlan`, `TypedExpr`/`Expr`, the analyzed statement types, `TableDef`/`ColumnDef`, `FunctionSignature`, `WindowFunction`, `CanonicalPredicate` (`raisin-sql`, derives only), and `previous = current.clone()`. A NaN literal can now never compare equal and runs to `max_passes` (10) — bounded, rare, passes are idempotent; Debug-equal-but-reordered `HashMap`s now compare equal (one pass fewer, never more).
2. *The built-in function registry was rebuilt per statement.* `Analyzer::with_catalog_arc` called `FunctionRegistry::default()` (several hundred signatures into hash maps): 621 samples (10 %). Now one process-wide `Arc<FunctionRegistry>` (`analyzer/mod.rs::builtin_functions`); nothing registers into an analyzer's registry.
3. *Projection round-tripped every object through JSON.* `project_row` evaluated a plain column to a `Literal` (`from_property_value`: `serde_json::to_value` of the map) and back (`PropertyValue::from_json`): 698 samples (11 %), the whole `properties` map plus every object/array property column of `SELECT *`. New `physical_plan/project_value.rs::projected_column_value{,_owned}` returns EXACTLY the round trip's answer: it keeps the stored value when the round trip provably returns it (scalars, plain arrays/objects with no classifying key — `raisin:ref`, `raisin:url`, `uuid`, `element_type`, `type` — and finite floats), rewrites a NESTED vector to the array the round trip makes (every node's materialized `$mixins` is an empty `Vector`, which forced the slow path on every `properties` map: 446 samples of the post-cache `SELECT *` profile), and runs the round trip for everything else (dates, decimals, references, …). An equivalence test pins fast == round trip over scalars, NaN/∞, dates, nested references/geometry/element/composite shapes and vectors. A projection whose expressions are all plain columns naming distinct input columns MOVES the values out of the input row (`project.rs::move_columns`) instead of cloning; the column lookup (`eval::core::column_index`, which `column_value` now wraps — one lookup) no longer allocates the qualified name, and `Row::get_by_unqualified` sits on the same allocation-free `index_of_unqualified`. The per-row `HashMap` column cache in `project_row` was dead after this (it cached only plain columns) and is removed, as are two clock reads per expression.
   After 1–3: `SELECT id` 53.6 → 24.4 µs, `SELECT *` 111 → 65 µs. The profile then (5,559 samples): optimizer still 2,144 (39 %; projection pruning 1,252, CSE 495), analyzer 866 (16 %), build 244 — ~60 % of the statement was a pure function of the SQL text and the catalog.
4. *Prepared-statement cache* (`engine/prepared.rs`; this is Phase 14 step 1, keyed on the final text rather than a parameter template). A statement's analysis and optimized LOGICAL plan, cached per `(catalog identity, single statement | one-statement batch, SQL text)` in a process-wide `moka` cache (TinyLFU admission, so one-off literal texts do not evict hot ones; 64 MiB weighted, ~2,000 statements; SQL over 8 KiB never cached). Cached: only `Query` statements with no uncorrelated subquery to bind (binding folds DATA into the statement). NOT cached: the physical plan (index build state, the branch's compound-index definitions, read revision, schema statistics all change without the text changing — it is planned for every execution), anything about the caller (RLS, auth, HEAD, the statement snapshot are applied after the cache), DML/DDL. Correctness of the key: the analyzer reads only the text, the catalog and the static function registry; constant folding folds only deterministic functions (`DEPTH`, `PARENT`, `PATH_STARTS_WITH`, `LOWER`, `UPPER`, `LENGTH`); nothing in a logical plan depends on time, data, caller or index state. The key holds the catalog's address and the entry a `Weak` to it — the allocation stays reserved while the entry lives, so no newer catalog can reuse a live key's address; a schema change yields a NEW catalog (`catalog_cache` rebuilds on the workspace event, its 60 s TTL and checkpoint ingest), so it misses; the whole cache is also registered with the derived-cache registry. `RAISIN_SQL_PLAN_CACHE=0` turns it off (the rollback switch; on by default — this is a cache, not a two-release rollout). A miss costs ~1–2 µs more than no cache at all (key + insert), visible in the fresh-text rows.
   One query path (the codebase's mirrored-funnel rule): `execute` (functions) and `execute_batch` (HTTP, WS, pgwire) each carried their own copy of logical planning → optimizer → index catalog → compound indexes → schema stats → HEAD read → execution context, and EXPLAIN a third. All three now call `engine/query_exec.rs` (`execute_query`, `plan_query`, `physical_plan_for`); the batch path's hand-built execution context is `build_execution_context`. Side effects of the merge: `execute` now evaluates a SELECT without FROM (it used to fail "FROM clause has no tables"; the batch path always could), and EXPLAIN loads compound-index definitions also for a query without a WHERE clause, as execution always did — EXPLAIN now shows the plan the query runs.
5. *Localized lookup decoded every candidate's properties.* `LocalizedNameSource::resolve` at depth 6 (5,045 samples): `NodeView::load` 3,663 (73 %), of which the full node decode 1,888 and the overlay read 1,470. A lookup verifies ids, names and paths, never properties: `NodeView` now reads through `reads::node_head_at` (`node_at` with `PropertiesMode::Skip`, one body behind both). Storage resolve depth 6: 53.3 → 43.2 µs.
6. *`LocalizedPathLookup` computed `__localized_path` again for the row it emitted.* The predicate column is projected, so `node_to_row` walked every ancestor through the lookup a second time: 2,098 samples, ~40 % of `SELECT id … AND __localized_path = …`. The operator emits only when the canonical localized path IS the requested one, so it passes it in (`OrderContext.localized_path`, now "values a scan already holds for the row"); the column is computed only when no scan supplied it. SQL `id` depth 3: 72 → 38 µs. Point lookups (path, id, localized) also move the node through translation into the row (`per_locale` + `node_to_row_owned`) instead of cloning it per locale.
7. *Projection pruning walked every projection twice and deep-cloned it* (`optimizer/projection/pruning.rs`): the read set is computed once and the expressions are moved, not cloned.

**Where the time goes now.** Cached `SELECT *` by path (5,614 samples): storage `get_by_path` 1,510 (27 %, incl. the `has_children` probe 245, which the SQL row never shows), `move_columns` 1,141 (20 %), `plan_query` 940 (17 %: physical planning 512 — the 30 wildcard projection expressions are cloned into the physical plan —, HEAD read 165, context 130), `node_to_row` 677 (12 %). RESOLVE depth 1: the batched storage reads are the floor (~6 µs per node, decode-dominated: `rmp` + the `PropertyValue` deserialize ladder), and the 2.2× above it is JSON: node → JSON (`Target::from_node`) 798, inlining 681, dropping `serde_json::Value`s 593, and the projection's `from_json` of the resolved document 439. The localized lookup is ~5 µs per segment: per segment one forward-claim prefix seek, one node-head read and one overlay read, each on a freshly created RocksDB iterator (~1/4 of it).

**Answer to the owner's question — is a localized path indexed "the same way as path"?** No, by design (§0, conflicts table): `PATH_INDEX` keys the FULL canonical path, so `WHERE path = …` is one seek. `LOCALIZED_NAME_INDEX` keys one segment per `(locale, parent_id, name)`, so `WHERE locale = 'fr' AND __localized_path = '/a/b/c'` walks one seek per segment and verifies each candidate (node live at the revision, parent, translated name, not hidden) — cost grows with depth (29 µs at depth 3, 43 µs at depth 6, vs 6–8 µs for the canonical path). A full localized-path index was rejected because a move, rename or ancestor-name edit would rewrite O(subtree × locales) keys. There is no `url_fr` property any more; `WHERE locale = 'fr' AND path = '<canonical>'` reads the canonical path with French content (24–26 µs SQL).

**Tests (green).**
- raisin-sql-execution `tests/all/plan_cache_tests.rs`: `a_schema_change_misses_the_cached_statement` (a new workspace → new catalog → the statement is analyzed again and the new table plans; the derived-cache registry drops the entry), `a_nodetype_change_reaches_a_cached_statement` (a compound index declared on the NodeType and built after the statement was cached: a statement answered from the cache plans `CompoundIndexScan`, same rows), `a_different_caller_is_filtered_on_a_cached_statement` (path / id / `CHILD_OF` statements cached by the system caller: a reader without access to the workspace gets nothing, a reader with access gets the system rows — through `execute` and `execute_batch`). The tests prove a HIT per call (`QueryEngine::prepared_physical_plan` returns whether that call was answered from the cache), not residency, which eviction under the full suite makes unreliable. Mutation check: a key without the catalog's identity fails the schema test.
- raisin-sql-execution lib `physical_plan::project_value::tests::{equals_the_round_trip, a_plain_property_map_is_not_converted}`.
- Benches: `index_read_bench_point::index_read_bench_p_point_lookups`, `index_read_bench_point_resolve::{index_read_bench_point_resolve_r_fifty_references, index_read_bench_point_resolve_l_localized_path}` (all `#[ignore]`; they assert row counts and `served_by = index`).
- Runs: raisin-sql-execution `--test all` 247 passed, only the 8 known failures (stable over three runs; the MVCC oracle included), `--lib` 939 passed; raisin-sql `--lib` 746 passed with the same 130 pre-existing failures (all in `analyzer::tests`, `logical_plan` tests, one `aggregate_spec` — the `FROM nodes` `TableNotFound` noted under Phase 12; optimizer tests all pass); raisin-rocksdb `--test all localized_name` 36 passed, `--lib localized` 5 passed; `cargo check` of raisin-transport-{http,ws,pgwire}, raisin-functions, raisin-server clean.

**Deviations.**
- No `samply`, `cargo flamegraph` or `perf` on this machine: profiles are macOS `sample`. The optional measurement against an APFS clone of `~/.raisindb` was skipped: `~/.raisindb` holds no database directory (only `bin`, `catalogs`, `plugins`, `server.log`); the running dev servers keep their data elsewhere, and nothing there was opened or copied.
- The prepared-statement cache keys the final SQL text, not a parameter template: parameters are substituted textually before analysis (Phase 12), and the optimizer's rewrites depend on literal values, so a template plan would differ from the literal one. A repeated statement (chrome, navigation, a hot page) is served from the cache; a never-seen literal is planned in full (Remaining).
- Diagnostics added for tests (`#[doc(hidden)]`): `plan_cache_contains`, `plan_cache_stats`, `invalidate_plan_cache`, `invalidate_compound_index_cache` (what the compound-definition cache's 30 s TTL does on its own) and `QueryEngine::prepared_physical_plan`.

**Remaining (measured, not done).**
- A never-seen SQL text: `SELECT *` 57 µs (9×), `SELECT id` 24 µs (4×); 40 % is still the optimizer's fixpoint loop, which always runs its passes at least twice (pruning and CSE with SipHash sets). Halving it needs either a proof that each pass is idempotent (then one confirmation-free pass) or passes that report "changed".
- Physical-plan caching (the 30 wildcard expressions are cloned per execution, ~2.5 µs) would need a key over compound definitions, index availability epochs, the read revision and schema stats — not attempted.
- Point lookups pay the `has_children` probe in `get`/`get_by_path` (~1 µs) for a column the SQL row never shows.
- RESOLVE: the JSON detour (node → `serde_json::Value` → inlined document → `PropertyValue::from_json`) is the 2.2× over the storage floor; producing `PropertyValue` directly would remove most of it. The floor itself is the per-node decode.
- Localized lookup: one snapshot-pinned iterator per CF for the whole walk (the Phase 4 `SnapshotRead`) instead of fresh iterators per segment, and a projected overlay decode (only `/__node_name` and the hidden flag are needed).

## Phase 13c — Localized-name uniqueness on every local translation write path — DONE (uncommitted)

**Owner request (2026-10-05).** Phase 12's sibling uniqueness for translated node names (`/__node_name`, enforced only when `localized_names.enforce_unique` is set AND the workspace build is `Ready` with zero collisions) is checked by the repository translation write, but the report was that the transaction translation write (`transaction/context/translations/write.rs`, `store_translation`) is not — and package install (`install_content/node_installer.rs`, `install_translation`) and SQL `UPDATE … FOR LOCALE … SET __node_name` go through it. Find every local write path of a node overlay, make all of them run the ONE check (`localized_name/unique/`) under the same conditions, never on replication apply or merge; verify the reserved value is normalized identically everywhere; keep the CLI validator change.

**What the audit found.** The transaction path WAS checked — at commit, by `stage_localized_names` under the branch lock (Phase 12 review fix 5) — but only against STORED state, which left three real holes:
1. *Two writes of one transaction never saw each other.* Two siblings named alike in one transaction (a package batch; two `UPDATE … FOR LOCALE` statements in one SQL transaction), or an overlay naming a node after a sibling the same transaction creates (`/site/a` and `/site/b` in one package batch, `b.node.fr.yaml` with `__node_name: a`), both passed: the batch is unreadable until written.
2. *A stored sibling the transaction renamed, moved or deleted still counted with its stored name* — a false conflict (give `a`'s name `x` to `b` while renaming `a` in the same commit).
3. *A collision surfaced only at commit*, so a package install failed its whole 100-entry batch and the install job, instead of rejecting the one overlay the way every other per-entry error (`is_per_node_error`) is rejected.

**Every local write path of a node-level overlay, and where it is checked:**

| entry point | funnel | check |
|---|---|---|
| HTTP `/translations` handlers, HTTP `raisin:cmd/translate`, `delete-translation`, `hide-in-locale`, `unhide-in-locale`, WS translation handlers, `TranslationService` (update, batch, hide, unhide, delete) | repository `repositories/translations/nodes.rs::store_translation` | `unique::NameCheck::staged` before the batch, and again under the branch lock at its commit step (review fix 1 below) |
| SQL `UPDATE … FOR LOCALE` / `TRANSLATE` (`dml_executor/translate.rs`, auto-commit and explicit transaction), package install `install_translation`, core copies (`node_service/copy_publish/mod.rs` ×2, `services/transaction/commit.rs`) | transaction `transaction/context/translations/write.rs::store_translation` | NEW: `check_overlay_unique` (`transaction/pending_names.rs`) BEFORE anything is staged; then again at commit (`commit/localized_names.rs`, under the branch lock, final view) |
| repository tree copy (`copy/tree.rs`, overlays via `stage_copied_translation`) | repository | `check_unique` of the copied root with the source's overlays (Phase 12, unchanged); `copy/single.rs` copies no overlay (its canonical name is checked by `add_impl`) |
| replication apply (`replication/application/translation_operations.rs`), merge (`merge/translations.rs`, `merge/node_translations.rs`), cross-branch promotion (`cross_branch/…`), history GC and repairs | — | never (trust model: the decision was made by another node or branch; the next build counts it, which suspends enforcement) |

The check is NOT placed in the one translation writer (`translation_write::stage_version`): replication apply, merge and promotion write through it too and must never refuse, and the repository and transaction need different views (stored vs. the transaction's own). So the ONE function `unique::check_unique_in` is called from each local entry point above.

**Implemented.**
- `localized_name/unique/` (was `unique.rs`): `check_unique_in(db, scope, node, parent, revision, overrides, pending)`; `check_unique` is it with `NoPending`. Per locale where the node is not hidden, its effective name (translated, else canonical) collides with (1) a sibling claiming it — stored claims PLUS the overlays the same transaction staged with that name — and, for a translated name, (2) the canonical name of a visible sibling without one, found through the transaction's paths first and the stored path index otherwise. EVERY sibling found is judged by its FINAL view (`pending::final_view`: the transaction's node/overlays where it wrote them, the stored ones otherwise; a deleted one is gone, a moved one is under another parent). `active(db, scope)` is the one enforcement gate (index enabled, `enforce_unique`, `Ready`, zero collisions). A parent the transaction itself creates no longer skips the check (staged siblings and the canonical path are still checked).
- `unique/pending.rs`: the `PendingWrites` trait (node, overlay, at_path, named) — the localized name module knows nothing about transactions; `NoPending` for every non-transaction caller.
- `transaction/pending_names.rs`: `TxPending` implements it over the read cache (`nodes`, `moved_nodes`, `paths`, `translations`, and the new `node_name_hints`); `check_overlay_unique` is the staging-time check; the commit's check shares `TxPending` (overrides, parent id) instead of its own copy.
- `ReadCache::node_name_hints` + `ReadCache::put_translation` (the transaction's overlay write records the name its overlay sets): (workspace, locale, name) → node ids, so "who in this transaction is named `x`" is one lookup, not a scan per write. Hints are never pruned when a later overlay replaces a name — every hint is judged by the final view (the index's own "a claim is a hint" rule).
- Selector: `overlay_node_name(overlay)` (the per-overlay half of `node_name_in`, without the default-language rule) so the hints use THE selector's normalization.
- Docs: `docs/website/docs/access/sql/localized-paths.md` (Uniqueness) names every path, the in-transaction rule and the replication/merge exemption.

**Normalization (item 2), verified.** No write path normalizes or interprets `__node_name` itself: the repository and HTTP/WS (JSON pointer `/__node_name`), SQL (`SET __node_name` → pointer `/__node_name`), and package install (`yaml_to_overlay`: top-level key → `/{key}`, `null` → `PropertyValue::Null`) all store the raw value. Every reader — index writer (`sync`), rebuild, lookup and fallback (`NodeView`), uniqueness (stored and staged), the transaction hints, the SQL `__node_name` column — goes through `indexing/localized_node_names.rs` (`node_name_in` → `overlay_node_name` → `normalize_node_name`: trim whitespace and `/`, last segment, empty or `\0` ignored, non-string ignored). Pinned by tests writing `" /chaise/ "` (transaction), `'/chaise/'` (SQL) and `/fr/site/a/` (package) and asserting the normalized claim or conflict.

**CLI (item 4), reviewed and kept.** `packages/raisindb-cli/src/wasm/translation-validator.ts` accepts the reserved key `__node_name` (string or `null`) instead of flagging it as an untranslatable schema field. Review change: the shape check moved out of the schema-aware pass into `checkNodeName`, which runs for every translation file — before, a node type the package has no schema for (a built-in `raisin:Folder`) skipped it, and a non-string value was stored and silently ignored by the server. Tests (`translation-validator.test.ts`): accepted on a local schema, `null` accepted, non-string rejected, non-string rejected without a schema.

**Tests (all green).**
- raisin-rocksdb lib `jobs::handlers::package_install::localized_name_install_tests::{package_overlay_colliding_with_a_sibling_is_refused_when_enforced, package_overlay_colliding_with_a_sibling_is_accepted_when_not_enforced}` — through `install_sorted_entries`; refused = that one entry in `content_errors`, the batch (every node and a non-colliding overlay) committed.
- raisin-rocksdb `tests/all/localized_name/translation_paths_tests::{transaction_overlay_colliding_with_a_sibling_is_refused_when_enforced, transaction_overlay_colliding_with_a_sibling_is_accepted_when_not_enforced, two_siblings_named_alike_in_one_transaction_are_refused, a_sibling_created_in_the_same_transaction_counts, a_name_given_up_in_the_same_transaction_is_free, the_commit_refuses_what_another_write_claimed_after_staging}`; `replication_tests::replicated_colliding_overlay_is_never_refused` (production receive path; the replica enforces and refuses the same name locally, and applies the peer's).
- raisin-sql-execution `tests/all/localized_name_uniqueness::{update_for_locale_node_name_colliding_with_a_sibling_is_refused_when_enforced, update_for_locale_node_name_colliding_with_a_sibling_is_accepted_when_not_enforced}`.
- Runs: raisin-rocksdb `--lib` 1330 passed; `--test all` 682 passed, 13 ignored (all Phase 12 `localized_name` tests included); raisin-sql-execution `--test all` 253 passed, only the 8 known failures; CLI `vitest run src/wasm/translation-validator.test.ts` 12 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Deviations.**
- No rollout flag: nothing here ships "one release before" anything — a stricter local write check, no format or op change.
- SQL `TRANSLATE` / `UPDATE … FOR LOCALE` filters one node per statement (`WHERE node_type = …` bulk is rejected as unsupported, a `CHILD_OF` filter does not parse), so "the same statement" is tested as two statements in one SQL transaction (`BEGIN`), which is the same transaction path.
- The staging-time check sees the transaction as it stands at that write: a SWAP of two siblings' names inside one transaction is refused at its first write and needs a temporary name, exactly as UNIQUE property values do (`check_unique_constraints` also checks at staging). The commit-time check judges the final view, so it alone would accept a swap; the staging check is what turns a package collision into one rejected entry.
- Not changed (beyond this phase's scope, translation writes): a transaction NODE write (`add_node` / `put_node`) whose canonical name equals a stored sibling's translated name is still refused only at commit — for a package, that fails the batch. Giving `add_node`/`put_node` the same staging-time `check_unique_in` call would make it one rejected entry.
- The plan text of Phase 12 still says `localized_name/unique.rs`; the module is now `localized_name/unique/` (`mod.rs`, `pending.rs`).

**Review fixes (2026-10-05, uncommitted).** Two reviewers' findings, each verified against the code and fixed in the shared path, with a regression test named after the failure (`tests/all/localized_name/unique_review_tests.rs` unless noted):
1. *Repository writes checked outside the branch lock* (HIGH, reported twice). `store_translation`, `add_impl`, `update_impl`, `move_tree` and `copy/tree` checked stored state and then wrote with no lock, so two of them — or one and a transaction commit between its check and its write — both stored a claim under a `Ready`, zero-collision state. Now each carries a `unique::NameCheck` (`unique/deferred.rs`; `None` unless enforced) in its `indexing::NodeCommit`, checked once at staging for a fast refusal and again UNDER `lock_branch_record`: `write_batch_with_head_as` runs it right after taking the lock it already takes; `NodeCommit::write` takes that lock (after the node locks, the one lock order) only when a check is carried, so unenforced writes stay lock-free. The translation write now goes through `NodeCommit::write` (it also locks its node). A conflict found there stays `Conflict` in move/copy. Test: `concurrent_repository_translations_of_one_name_cannot_both_land` (the first write paused between check and write with `pause_commit_of`).
2. *Reads bounded at the transaction's (or an in-place rewrite's) revision* (MEDIUM ×2, LOW). `check_unique_in` read the parent through `PATH_INDEX` and the node's stored overlays AS OF `revision` — allocated at a transaction's first write, or a `versionable=false` node's REUSED old revision — while every other read was the newest state. A parent created or renamed into place since did not resolve (every stored claim skipped; the commit's `sync_node_final` invalidated the workspace build on every in-place refresh), and an in-place node was judged by names it no longer had. Now `revision` decides only override precedence; the parent and the node's overlays are read at NEWEST, and `TxPending::parent_of` falls back to the NEWEST path index entry, the parent the commit hands both the check and `sync_node_final` (which keeps its revision bound for everything else, as replication needs). Tests: `parent_created_after_the_transaction_revision_still_guards_its_children`, `in_place_refresh_is_judged_by_its_current_name_not_its_reused_revision`, `in_place_refresh_under_a_renamed_ancestor_keeps_the_build_ready`.
3. *Unchanged names re-validated* (MEDIUM ×2). The check refused every locale's effective name, so a collision brought in by replication apply / merge (never refused by design) wedged every later local write of either node — a property-only update, another locale's overlay, a package re-install — until an operator renamed one. Now only names a write CHANGES are refused (`unique/stored.rs`: a locale whose stored parent path and effective name equal the new ones is skipped, as `UNIQUE_INDEX` checks only changed values). Tests: `stored_collision_does_not_wedge_later_writes_of_either_node`; `replication_tests::replicated_colliding_overlay_is_never_refused` now also writes both nodes locally after the apply.
4. *A sibling judged by a superseded staged overlay* (LOW). `pending::final_view` used a staged overlay unconditionally; the index writer keeps a NEWER stored version over it. Both now go through one precedence rule, `reads::overlay_over` (`overlays_at` calls it too), with the transaction revision from `PendingWrites::staged_at`. Test: `newer_stored_overlay_beats_a_staged_one_when_judging_a_sibling`.
5. *Quadratic overrides lookup* (LOW). `ReadCache::translations` is a `BTreeMap` and `ReadCache::translations_of` a range, so `TxPending::overrides` no longer scans every staged overlay per node (it ran per staged overlay and per commit target under the branch lock).

Deferred: marking a workspace `collisions > 0` (or invalidating it) when a replication / merge sync stores a second verified claimant. With fix 3 such a collision no longer blocks writes; lookups order it deterministically (newest claim wins) until a build counts it, and detecting it at apply time means a verified claim probe on every replicated name write. Mutation-checked: with the fixes reverted, every new test fails (and the extended replication test).

## Phase 13d — Native speed: cold SQL, parameterized plan cache, physical plan cache, RESOLVE without JSON, the small leftovers — DONE (uncommitted)

**Owner request (2026-10-05).** "The goal is to be natively super fast in raisindb; this is biting us in real projects." Fix every item of Phase 13b's *Remaining* list in the shared code paths — no second fast path around RLS, revision bounds, the planner's correctness gates or the caller identity — and re-measure 13b's table plus pgwire prepared execution. Owner override as in 13b: one release build of the harness at a time, `make prune-tests` first, ≥ 10 GB free, `target/release` deleted afterwards (done).

**Results** (release, macOS, same harness as 13b; µs p50; ratio = SQL ÷ its storage counterpart; "before" = this phase's starting code, measured with the same binary layout the same day):

| case | storage | before | after |
|---|---|---|---|
| by path, `SELECT id` | 5.9 | 10.3 (1.70×) | **8.7 (1.48×)** |
| by path, `SELECT *` | 5.9 | 19.7 (3.23×) | **14.9 (2.54×)** |
| by path, `SELECT *`, `execute_batch` | 5.9 | 19.8 | **14.8** |
| by id, `SELECT id` / `SELECT *` | 5.1 | 9.3 / 18.8 | **7.6 / 14.0** |
| by property, `SELECT *` (`LIMIT 1`) | 7.0 | 24.6 (24.8) | **23.0 (22.9)** |
| by path, fresh text, `SELECT id` | 5.9 | 24.6 (4.0×) | **21.1 (3.6×)** |
| by path, fresh text, `SELECT *` | 5.9 | 57.2 (9.4×) | **38.0 (6.5×)** |
| **prepared** `… WHERE path = $1`, new value every run, `SELECT id` (pgwire Execute / HTTP / WS `params`) | 5.9 | = fresh text, 24.6 | **12.5 (2.1×)** |
| **prepared**, new value every run, `SELECT *` | 5.9 | = fresh text, 57.2 | **25.1 (4.3×)** |
| no plan cache (`RAISIN_SQL_PLAN_CACHE=0`), `SELECT id` / `SELECT *` | 5.9 | 23.1 / 54.9 (13b) | **19.0 / 35.4** |
| RESOLVE depth 1 (50 inlined) | 306 | 655 (2.18×) | **460 (1.50×)** |
| RESOLVE depth 2 (100 inlined) | 503 | 1188 (2.36×) | **844 (1.68×)** |
| localized storage resolve + get, depth 3 / 6 | — | 28.8 / 42.9 | **26.1 / 35.0** |
| `locale='fr' AND __localized_path`, `id`, depth 3 / 6 | | 37.5 / 52.9 | **30.7 / 43.4** |
| same, `*`, depth 3 / 6 | | 47.0 / 62.3 | **36.9 / 49.5** |
| `RESOLVE_PATH`, depth 3 / 6 | | 40.5 / 56.4 | **34.1 / 44.8** |
| `locale='fr' AND path=<canonical>`, `*`, depth 3 / 6 | | 25.8 / 24.0 | **19.2 / 19.3** |

Targets: cold point lookup within ~2× of the warm one — **not reached** (`SELECT *` 38.0 vs 14.9 = 2.55×, `SELECT id` 21.1 vs 8.7 = 2.4×; the warm path got 25 % faster too, which raised the ratio while the cold path got 34 % faster); RESOLVE within ~1.3× of reading the nodes — **not reached** (1.50× / 1.68×). Both with profile evidence under *Not reached* below.

### 1. Cold SQL

Profile of a never-seen `SELECT *` by path with the cache off, before (6,040 samples): optimizer 35 % (projection pruning 15 %, CSE 10.6 %, the confirming round's plan clone/compare/drop 8 %), analysis 18 % (sqlparser 5.8 %), plan building 4.8 %.

- **One optimizer round** (`raisin-sql/src/optimizer/mod.rs`). The loop always ran a second, confirming round. Each pass is idempotent and no pass creates work for one that ran before it in the round (pruning adds pass-through columns only, CSE column references and projections only, the hierarchy rewrite canonical shapes that fold nothing and rewrite to themselves), so one round IS the fixpoint. Proof is by test, not by comment: a debug build still runs the confirming round and PANICS when a round changed anything — every SQL test in the workspace re-proves it (raisin-sql 750 lib tests, raisin-sql-execution 259 integration + 939 lib tests: zero non-fixpoint rounds). `RAISIN_SQL_OPTIMIZER_VERIFY=1` turns the check (logging) on in a release build, `=log` makes a debug build log instead of panic; `optimizer::non_fixpoint_rounds()` counts.
- **CSE skips what it cannot change**: a projection of plain columns/literals has no candidate (a leaf is never extracted); CSE's collision check compared `Debug` renderings, now structural `==`.
- **Projection pruning** passes requirement sets by value (it copied the 30-column set at every level, and the read set once more).
- **The analyzer looked the same workspace table up once per column reference** — and a workspace table is BUILT per lookup (~30 `ColumnDef`s). Per-scope memo (`AnalyzerContext::table_def_shared`); existence checks use the new `Catalog::has_workspace_table`, which does not build the table.
- Cold after: 57.2 → 38.0 µs (`SELECT *`), 24.6 → 21.1 µs (`SELECT id`); see *Not reached* for what is left.

### 2. Parameterized plan cache (`engine/prepared_params/`, `raisin-sql/src/template/`)

Statements with parameters are now prepared ONCE per text with placeholders. Key: `(catalog identity, single | batch, template SQL, each value's literal TYPE)`; value: the template's analysis and optimized logical plan. Every execution BINDS its values. The contract (`raisin_sql::template` module docs): binding must yield exactly what analyzing and optimizing the substituted text yields. It holds by construction in three parts, and is proved per template:

1. **Types.** A placeholder is analyzed with the type of the literal its value renders to (`template::param_literal` — the analyzer's own `literal_from_sql_value`, through the same dialect), so every type check runs as on the literal. The one coercion that re-spells a literal (Text → Path for a function argument) types the placeholder `Path`, and binding applies the same `coerce_text_to_path`.
2. **Positions** (`template::check`). A template is admitted only when every placeholder sits where its value steers nothing before physical planning: a comparison, `IN` or `BETWEEN` against a column or a JSON extraction of one, or the path argument of `CHILD_OF` / `DESCENDANT_OF` / `PATH_STARTS_WITH` / `REFERENCES`. Everything else is VALUE-DEPENDENT and planned per value from the substituted text, exactly as before. Placeholders the substitution would rewrite but the parser does not see (inside a string, a comment, a `GRAPH_TABLE` body) make the template value-dependent (`placeholders_are_tokens`).
3. **Value-driven optimizer passes** — constant folding and the hierarchy rewrite — are re-run on the bound plan (`Optimizer::rebind`); the physical planner always runs on bound literals.

The proof: a template's first execution plans the substituted text the ordinary way (that is what it runs), then builds the template, binds it to the same values and caches it ONLY if the bound analysis and the bound optimized plan are IDENTICAL to the text's; otherwise the text is remembered as value-dependent. A placeholder surviving binding is also a refusal.

**Every literal-dependent decision, and how it is handled:**

| decision | where | with a parameter | test |
|---|---|---|---|
| `extract_locale_predicate` (`locale = $n`, `locale IN (…)`) | analyzer | extracted into a locale MARKER, replaced at binding (Text only) | `a_locale_parameter_reads_its_own_locale` |
| `__revision = $n`, `__branch = $n` extraction | analyzer | value-dependent (per value) | `value_steered_positions_are_refused` |
| LIMIT / OFFSET `$n` | analyzer | not admitted (the template is refused) → per value | `compound_predicates_and_limit_bind_correctly` (LIMIT 1, 2, 3, 1) |
| Text → Path coercion of a function argument (`CHILD_OF($1)`) | analyzer | placeholder typed Path, bound by the same rule | `bound_templates_equal_their_substituted_text`, `one_template_serves_every_value` |
| analysis-time folding (`DEPTH($1)`, `PARENT($1)`, scalar folds), `@>` JSON literal parse, regex/LIKE pattern, INTERVAL, `JSON_GET` path, window literals, a placeholder in the SELECT list | analyzer | position not admitted → per value | `value_steered_positions_are_refused` |
| type checks (`path = $1` with a number) | analyzer | typed placeholder: same check, same error (from the text path) | `param_literal_is_the_analyzer_rule` |
| a negative number, a functions-runtime array (`ARRAY[…]`) | rendering | not one literal → per value | `param_literal_is_the_analyzer_rule` |
| constant folding; hierarchy canonicalization (`CHILD_OF`, `DESCENDANT_OF`, `PATH_STARTS_WITH`, `REFERENCES`, `DEPTH`/`PARENT` equality, **JsonPropertyEq**) | optimizer | re-run on the bound plan | `bound_templates_equal_their_substituted_text` (8 shapes incl. JSON property, IN, locale) |
| **compound index eligibility**, **LIMIT pushdown**, point lookup / property index / **LocalizedPathLookup** choice | physical planner | always planned on bound literals | `compound_predicates_and_limit_bind_correctly` (plan shows `CompoundIndexScan`), `a_locale_parameter_reads_its_own_locale` (`__localized_path = $2`) |

Every surface hits the same cache: HTTP `/api/sql` and WS `sql_query` with `params` (`execute_batch_with_params`; placeholder errors are still reported up front with the old status/text), **pgwire Parse/Bind/Execute** (`do_query` hands the engine the prepared statement's SQL text and the portal's values — one template per prepared statement for every Execute; the substituted text is only built for session commands and schema inference), and functions (`raisin.sql.query/execute`, `QueryContext`, transaction reads) through `execute_with_params` with the functions runtime's own literal formatter. A template keeps its last 4 bound statements by value, so a repeated execution with the same values reuses the bound statement and its physical plan.

Not in the key, and why that is safe: the caller (RLS, auth and HEAD are applied at execution, after the cache — `the_template_and_physical_caches_invalidate` runs a denied and an allowed reader through the system caller's template), the branch and index state (physical planning, §3). Invalidation: a schema change is a new catalog (new key); `RAISIN_SQL_PLAN_CACHE=0` and the derived-cache registry (`invalidate_plan_cache`) drop templates too.

### 3. Physical plan cache (`engine/physical_cache.rs`)

A prepared statement (final text, or a bound template) keeps its physical plan with what it was planned from. Reused only when tenant, repo, engine branch, FROM workspace, the compound-index definitions handed to the planner, the schema statistics and the statement's `__revision` pin are equal AND every spatial/compound AVAILABILITY answer the planner asked for is still the same — the questions are recorded while planning (`RecordingCatalog`) and asked again, fail-closed, on reuse; a build, rebuild or declaration change replans. Excluded, by construction: the branch HEAD (the execution context reads it per execution), the caller (RLS/auth live in the context), the statement snapshot and RESOLVE memo, and every statement that is not prepared (DML, statements with subqueries, texts over 8 KiB). `PhysicalPlan::Project` holds its expressions as `Arc<[ProjectionExpr]>`, so executing a cached plan no longer copies the wildcard projection (the ~2.5 µs of 13b).

### 4. RESOLVE without JSON

RESOLVE walks and builds `PropertyValue`s read straight from the stored records, through the ONE resolver (`reference_resolver`: `fetch`/`frontier` unchanged, `walk`/`memo` now on values, new `doc.rs`, `json_len.rs`); `resolve_values_many` is the API, `resolve_json` a thin wrapper. Its output is defined as before — the value whose JSON rendering is the JSON resolver's document — which holds because rendering is compositional, provided the walker sees a reference exactly where the JSON walker saw one (`doc::reference`: a reference, an object or an ELEMENT whose `raisin:ref` renders as a string) and can put a target wherever the JSON walker put one (`doc::make_walkable` re-spells the two places a typed value cannot hold a target: a composite item that is itself a reference, a resource `metadata` map that is one). The SQL projection gets what the JSON read back as: `PropertyValue::into_json_round_trip` / `json_round_trip_in_place` (`raisin-models`), THE "what JSON gives back" rule — exact `from_json(to_value(v))`, proven-unchanged subtrees kept in place, the real round trip only for a subtree a classifying key makes ambiguous. 13b's `projected_column_value` now uses the same rule (so a bare reference no longer sends a whole `properties` map through JSON). The budget's byte count is exact (`json_len`: the length `to_vec(to_value(v))` has, f32 widening and element key collisions included). Further cuts from the profile: a target's object is the node's own property map (no re-hash), a target inlined once is moved into place instead of copied twice, the round trip is in place.

Equivalence: `reference_resolver::tests::equivalence::values_resolve_exactly_as_json_did` runs the pre-13d JSON walk and inline verbatim (test-only copy) over the same fetch decisions and asserts the SQL-visible value AND the budget's occurrence and byte totals are identical, at depths 0–4, with and without `fields`, on a fixture with nested references, an element and a composite item that ARE references, arrays, a cycle, a path reference, a default-workspace reference, a missing and an RLS-denied target, a resource whose metadata is a reference, dates, decimals, a vector and NaN. Unit tests pin `into_json_round_trip` against the real round trip over every classifying key (`json_round_trip_tests.rs`) and `json_len` against `to_vec`.

### 5. `has_children` only where it is shown

`NodeRepository::get_for_read(scope, locator, max_revision, opts)` (default: `get`/`get_by_path`, then `opts` applied; RocksDB: `dispatch_get_as` / `get_by_path_impl_as` skip the probe and honour `PropertiesRead`). The SQL row executors that read single nodes go through ONE helper (`scan_executors::helpers::row_node`): point lookups by path and id, the localized lookup, the table-scan DFS, the property range scan, the index lookup join. Every API read (`get`, `get_by_path`, HTTP, WS, SDK) still carries `has_children` (decision D4 unchanged). Test: `point_read_shape_tests::sql_reads_skip_the_child_probe_and_api_reads_keep_it` (equal to `get` minus the field, by id and path, at HEAD and at a revision; with the probe asked for, equal to `get`).

### 6. Localized lookup

`LocalizedLookup::resolve` and `localized_path` read every segment through ONE `SnapshotRead` — one iterator per column family, pinned to one RocksDB snapshot — instead of fresh iterators per read: `VersionedRead` gained the `scan` primitive, and the grouped translation scan (`for_each_newest_in`), the node lifeline walk (`NodeLifeline::read_in`), the version read (`read_version_in`), the node head (`node_head_at_in`) and the claims (`claims_in`) run on any source (the `db` functions are wrappers — one implementation each). Overlays are decoded down to what a view consults (`decode_overlay_keeping(value, "/__node_name")`: the kept value through the same `PropertyValue` decode; `projected_tests.rs`). Storage lookup depth 6: 42.9 → 35.0 µs. The Phase 12 oracle template and all 36 `localized_name` integration tests stay green.

### Not reached, with profile evidence

- **Cold `SELECT *` at 2.55× of warm.** After this phase (5,948 samples, cache off): analysis 24.5 % (sqlparser 7.1 %), logical plan building 6.4 %, optimizer 12.4 % (pruning 10.9 %), physical planning 6 %, storage + row conversion ~35 %. The largest remaining single cost is `String::clone` (13 %), spread over analyzer → plan builder → pruning → physical planner: each stage copies the 30 `table.column` names of the wildcard. Halving it needs `Arc<str>` column names in `TypedExpr`/`ColumnDef` (a workspace-wide type change) or auto-parameterizing literals into the template cache; neither was attempted. For a parameterized statement the template cache answers instead (25.1 µs `SELECT *` with a new value every run, 12.5 µs `SELECT id`).
- **Prepared execution at 1.7× of a repeated text.** Binding clones the template's analysis and logical plan (~10 % of a prepared `SELECT *`) and every new value is physically planned (9 %); a physical plan with parameter slots would need a parameter-aware physical planner (the planner's index choices read literal values).
- **RESOLVE at 1.50× / 1.68× of the floor.** The floor reads the nodes; RESOLVE must also BUILD the document: copying each inlined value into place (Inliner 17 %), dropping the result (14 %) and the statement's memo (5.8 %), target construction (10.6 %, of which the exact budget byte count 4.9 %). Those are the document itself, not a detour.

### Tests (all green)

- raisin-sql lib: `template::tests::{bound_templates_equal_their_substituted_text, value_steered_positions_are_refused, param_literal_is_the_analyzer_rule, placeholders_inside_strings_are_not_tokens}` — 750 passed, the 130 pre-existing failures unchanged.
- raisin-sql-execution `tests/all/param_plan_cache_tests.rs`: `one_template_serves_every_value`, `a_locale_parameter_reads_its_own_locale`, `compound_predicates_and_limit_bind_correctly`, `the_batch_entry_point_shares_the_template`; `param_plan_cache_invalidation.rs`: `the_template_and_physical_caches_invalidate` (NodeType change, index availability change, different caller, schema change); `point_read_shape_tests`; the oracle for every row is the substituted text through `execute`. `--test all`: 259 passed, only the 8 known failures (MVCC oracle and the 13b plan-cache tests included; in one of five full runs `mvcc_index_oracle::stages::replay_witnesses_pass_out_of_order` failed under load and passed in the other four and three times alone — the oracle builds a fresh catalog per engine, so no cached statement is shared between its runs); `--lib` 939 passed (`project_value` tests updated to the shared rule).
- raisin-core lib: `reference_resolver` 11 passed (new `equivalence`, `json_len`); 308 total.
- raisin-models lib: `json_round_trip::tests::equals_the_json_round_trip`; 389 total.
- raisin-rocksdb: `--lib` 1331 passed (new `translation_read::projected_tests`); `--test all` 688 passed.
- raisin-transport-pgwire lib: `extended_query::params::tests::each_execute_hands_over_the_statement_text_and_its_values`. raisin-functions callbacks 85 passed. `cargo check -p raisin-server` clean.
- Bench: `index_read_bench_point` gained `sql_path_{star,id}_param` (the prepared/pgwire case).

### Deviations

- No "one release before" split: nothing here changes a stored format or a replicated op. `RAISIN_SQL_PLAN_CACHE=0` remains the one rollback switch — it turns off the final-text cache, the template cache and (because only cached statements carry a physical slot) the physical plan cache. New: `RAISIN_SQL_OPTIMIZER_VERIFY` (see §1).
- pgwire reuses ONE plan per prepared statement through the engine's process-wide template cache keyed by the statement text, not through state stored on the parsed statement. The pgwire unit test pins that Execute hands the engine the text and the values; the engine tests pin that every execution after the first is bound from the one template.
- The template key includes each value's literal TYPE (a `path = $1` bound once to text and once to a number are two templates), so every type check is the literal's.
- `resolve_json` (the JSON API, now a wrapper) reads a JSON number beyond `i64` back as a float.
- The projected overlay decode does not validate the entries it skips (it still validates the JSON).
- Single-node SQL reads outside the helpers named in §5 (spatial, vector, fulltext and search hits, neighbors, count, prefix-scan parent lookups, Cypher) still call `get` and pay the probe; they are not point lookups.
- Phase 14's step 1 (template-keyed cache) is done here; its step 2 is done in the form above (physical plan per bound statement, availability re-checked), not as a plan with parameter slots.

## Phase 14 — SQL AST/plan cache (gated on harness J) — PLANNED

- **Step 1** (only if parse plus analyze exceeds about 15% of point-query latency): cache the parsed AST keyed on the template SQL **before** parameter substitution, and bind parameters as AST literals. This gives pgwire Parse/Bind real reuse. *(Phase 13b measured parse + analyze + logical planning + optimization at ~60 % of a release-build point lookup and shipped the cache keyed on the FINAL text. Phase 13d shipped the template-keyed form: analysis and optimized logical plan per text with placeholders, values bound per execution — see Phase 13d.)*
- **Step 2** (physical plan with parameter slots): deferred. *(Phase 13d caches the physical plan per prepared or bound statement instead, availability re-checked on reuse.)* Planning depends on literal values (LIMIT pushdown, `__revision` stripping, the property literal), so a parameter-aware planner is needed first. If built, it is keyed on `(template, tenant, repo, branch, catalog epoch, availability epoch, auth shape)`, invalidated on `Event::Schema`, and registered with `derived_cache_registry`. Availability is re-checked at execution time (fail closed).

**Risk.** Medium. Step 1 is low.

## Phase 15 — Deferred, measure-gated, or rejected

- **Prefix extractors and prefix blooms** for NODES, NODE_PATH, PATH_INDEX, PROPERTY_INDEX and TRANSLATION_DATA, with an `exact_prefix_scan` helper (`prefix_same_as_start`). Only after an audit of every shorter-prefix iterator (fork copy, wipe, purge, GC, rebuild), and only if harness counters show blocks read on negative lookups. Meanwhile, drop the dead whole-key bloom on PROPERTY_INDEX (memory only; restart required, no data change).
- **Content-addressed decoded-blob cache** (key = hash of blob bytes, value = decoded StorageNode without path). Only if the post-Phase-4 harness shows decode dominating. Never a cache keyed by `(node, revision)` or by HEAD (`versionable=false`, replication below HEAD, and SST ingest all break it).
- **Lossy PROPERTY_INDEX compaction filter.** Premature; run-collapse covers the bloat. Revisit only for `__updated_at` tombstone churn, with a historical-revision planner gate and the Phase 9 watermark.
- **Materialized has_children (CHILD_PRESENCE CF).** Not recommended. It would be a 31st mirrored write path, counters do not survive merge, and a flag on the parent is not time-travel correct. Revisit only if the Phase 1 probe still profiles hot, and then only after funnelling all ORDERED_CHILDREN writes through one helper.
- **Rich-text links in the reference index** (extending `walk_references` with a memchr-gated `raisin://` parse, id form only). It needs a REFERENCE rebuild. Valuable for unpublish-impact and REFERENCES(), but not required for rewriting.
- **RESOLVE nested in CASE** (lifting async calls in the planner). Do it after Phase 4, when Studio asks for it.
- **Repository-layer translation `name_{lang}` legacy keys:** leave as they are.

## Operational test gates

These gate releases, not single phases. They cover the failure modes that unit and oracle tests cannot.

- **Downgrade open test, every release.** After the new binary has run (and written whatever it writes), open the DB with the previous release's CF list and read path. Required to pass for every release except one explicitly marked "downgrade unsupported below X" in its release notes (Phase 10b: below the release that ships it, Phase 11, Phase 12 below 12.0). Test: `downgrade_open_previous_release` in raisin-rocksdb `tests/all`.
- **Mixed-version replication test for every new op.** An older applier receiving an unknown OpType must skip-and-warn or buffer it, never stall the applier or wedge the peer. Test: `unknown_optype_is_skipped_not_stalled` (Phase 11), and the same harness for any later op.
- **Crash-mid-repair resume.** Kill each streaming repair, rebuild and backfill at a random batch boundary, restart, and assert that the final state equals an uninterrupted run and that the cursor resumed rather than restarted. Test: `repair_resumes_after_crash` per job.
- **Checkpoint bootstrap then repair.** Bootstrap a node from an unrepaired peer's checkpoint (corrupt ORDERED_CHILDREN, `\x00` PATH_INDEX tombstones, a stale Ready state record), assert reads are already correct (reader tolerance), run the repair, and assert the data is clean and state records were revalidated. Test: `bootstrap_from_unrepaired_peer_then_repair`.
- **Prod-snapshot dry run before Phases 2, 6, 9 and 12.** On a copy of a production snapshot: run every repair, the Phase 6 orphan detector, the Phase 9 collapse and the Phase 12 rebuild in dry-run mode, and record expected writes, expected reclaimed bytes, peak memory, duration and disk headroom. A phase does not ship until its dry run is recorded in the release notes.

## 6. Adaptive / dynamic indexing: verdict

**Premature, apart from a report-only advisor.**

- Every derived index is rebuilt on **every** replica and copied on every fork.
- Every new index adds a write funnel to a codebase whose top bug class is mirrored writers that drift.
- Correctness depends on fail-closed availability gates.

Auto-creating or dropping indexes would multiply all three costs with no human in the loop. Cracking, learned indexes, DBSP/IVM, the InnoDB change buffer and migrating to RocksDB user-defined timestamps are all poor fits (sota dossier). What *is* justified is the Phase 0 advisor counter: log, per predicate shape, the queries that fell back to a scan or a row-level fallback, aggregated in the admin console. An operator then declares compound or property indexes explicitly through the existing state record. Revisit automation only when that log shows recurring, high-volume shapes that operators keep failing to index.

## 7. Decisions the product owner must make

- **D1 — RESOLVE enforces RLS on targets.** DECIDED **yes**, always enforced, no legacy flag (shipped in Phase 3). Guest Studio sites need read grants on `assets` and `tags`, or delivery functions declare `execution_context: system`.
- **D2 — RESOLVE reads targets at the query revision.** Recommend **yes**. It is a correctness fix, and `rev/{n}` pages will show historical targets. Historical *translations* are trustworthy on replicas only after Phase 11.1 and 11.5.
- **D3 — RESOLVE budget defaults** (5k targets / 50k occurrences / 32 MB, failing loudly). DECIDED **yes** (shipped).
- **D4 — `has_children` on reads.** DECIDED: **kept on all reads**, made cheap by the Phase 1 probe (supersedes the earlier "absent from internal storage reads" recommendation). Sub-decision: should the chevron respect RLS (children the caller cannot see)? Recommend: not now. Document it as existence-only.
- **D5 — `embedding` excluded from `SELECT *`** (fetched only when named). DECIDED **yes** (shipped), with a release note.
- **D6 — Skip-unchanged writers give up accidental self-healing.** DECIDED **yes** (owner, 2026-10-04, Phase 7b): `index.skip_unchanged` is on by default, gated per branch on this node's `property_index` rebuild, which queues itself; the verify job stays admin-triggered.
- **D7 — Run-collapse GC enabled by default under KEEP_ALL.** **Reversed: no.** Collapse is not lossless while historical-revision inserts are possible. It stays off by default, under KEEP_ALL and otherwise, until the causal-stability watermark exists and every Phase 2 repair has completed on that branch; then it is admin-triggered. Retention-based GC stays opt-in. Pin merge bases first.
- **D8 — Internal link grammar `raisin://{ws}/@{id}` and a Studio link-picker change.** Recommend **yes**. URL policy stays in Studio through the REWRITE_LINKS template.
- **D9 — Localized name model.** Decided (owner, 2026-10-04): the translated node name, overlay `/__node_name`, and nothing else — no `url_{locale}` pattern. Per-sibling uniqueness enforced per repository only after a clean rebuild. A canonical-name URL in a locale where the node has its own translated name answers with a 301 hint, not a 404.
- **D10 — Translation replication op rollout across two releases.** Recommend **yes**. Release 1 applies the new op and adds the `Unknown` OpType catch-all, release 2 emits it. Downgrade below release 1 after the flag is set is unsupported.
- **D11 — Repair jobs run automatically on upgrade, or admin-triggered?** **Admin-triggered, permanently**, with a prominent console banner and per-node status. The earlier "automatic in the next minor release" is dropped: it would make every node run streaming repairs at boot simultaneously. **Exception (owner, 2026-10-03):** the `node_path` backfill (Phase 10b) is enqueued automatically, after boot, one branch at a time (a job chain), for each branch whose state is not `done` — there is one small deployment and a local database, and neither should need an admin call to converge. `RAISIN_NODE_PATH_AUTO_BACKFILL=0` turns it off. **Second exception (owner, 2026-10-04, Phase 7b):** with `index.skip_unchanged` on (the default), the `property_index` rebuild is enqueued automatically the same way — the chain after boot, plus a targeted job for a fork once its index copy is done, a new branch, a merge target and every branch after a checkpoint ingest; `RAISIN_PROPERTY_INDEX_AUTO_REBUILD=0` turns it off.

| # | Phase | Status | Gain | Risk | Effort | Hard prerequisites |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Harness | DONE | enabler | none | S–M | — |
| 1 | has_children / log / seek | DONE (items 6–9 open) | very high | low | M | — |
| 3 | RESOLVE correct + frontier + memo | DONE | very high (+ security) | medium (behaviour) | M | — |
| 6 | Bounded property reader + residual re-check + orphan report | IN PROGRESS | high + correctness | medium | M–L | prod-snapshot dry run (orphan report) |
| 2 | Proven bug fixes, merge funnel, compound lease, then repairs | PLANNED | correctness, replication apply O(1) | medium | M–L | writer fixes before repairs; prod-snapshot dry run |
| 0b | MVCC oracle with independent reference model | DONE (uncommitted) | guard (+11 bug fixes) | none | L | before 7–9 |
| 5 | Tree readers know-before-recurse | PLANNED | medium | low | S–M | — |
| 10 | Path read rule, NODE_PATH writer + backfill | DONE (uncommitted) | medium + correctness | medium | M | 2.8 |
| 10b | One record format everywhere (gate removed; merge + replication on the one writer; automatic backfill) | DONE (uncommitted) | correctness (+ one format to maintain) | medium | M | 10 |
| 4 | Batched snapshot reads | DONE (uncommitted) | high (measured: 7–14 % in-memory, debug) | medium | M | 10 (before or with) |
| 7 | Delta writers (skip unchanged) | DONE (uncommitted; `index.skip_unchanged` default on since 7b) | very high (writes) | medium-high | M–L | 6, 0b, 2.8, 2.10, `get_last_order_label` rewrite, full rebuild on every node |
| 7b | Commit-order-independent index deltas (per-node commit lock; flag on by default; automatic rebuild) | DONE (uncommitted) | correctness; makes 7's saving the default | medium | M | 7, 8 |
| 8 | Compound reader, derived tombstones, replica + merge writes | DONE (uncommitted; compound state v2 forces one rebuild per index) | high (writes) | medium | M | 2.5, 2.11 |
| 9 | Run-collapse GC (`collapse_runs`, flag `history_gc_collapse_runs` default off; refused in cluster mode) | DONE (uncommitted) | high (space, fork) | medium-high | M | all Phase 2 repairs complete per CF/branch, causal-stability watermark, 7.6, 8.2; prod-snapshot dry run |
| 11 | Translation substrate + `Unknown` OpType | PLANNED | correctness | medium | M | two-release rollout |
| 11d | Remove legacy replication node ops (promotion prune as an `ApplyRevision` delete; compactor, keep-path writer and defaulted-workspace scan gone) | DONE (uncommitted) | less code; one node-op shape on the wire | low | M | 11b (`Unknown` skip proven) |
| 13 | REWRITE_LINKS | PLANNED | feature | low | M | 3, 4 |
| 12.0 | localized_name_index CF registration + unknown-CF skip in ingest | DONE (uncommitted; same release as 12, owner decision) | enabler | low | S | — |
| 12 | Localized name index + surfaces | DONE (uncommitted; on by default, `RAISIN_LOCALIZED_NAME_INDEX=0` turns it off) | feature + perf | high surface | L | 11, 2.8, 7.8, 12.0; prod-snapshot dry run owed |
| 13b | Release profiling: optimizer change detection, shared function registry, projection without JSON round trips, prepared-statement cache (`RAISIN_SQL_PLAN_CACHE=0` turns it off), one query path, localized lookup without property decode | DONE (uncommitted) | point lookup 5–6× faster (1.7–3.9× of the storage get for a repeated statement); localized SQL lookup ~3× faster | low–medium | M | — |
| 13d | Native speed: one optimizer round, parameterized plan cache (pgwire/HTTP/WS/functions), physical plan cache, RESOLVE over stored values, no `has_children` on SQL point reads, localized lookup on one pinned iterator set | DONE (uncommitted) | point lookup 1.5–2.5× of the storage get; prepared statements 2–4×; RESOLVE 1.5–1.7× of its floor | medium | L | 13b |
| 14 | AST cache | step 1 done in 13d (template-keyed); step 2 in 13d's form | measure-gated | low–medium | M | harness J |
| 15 | Deferred items | — | — | — | — | — |

Notes on the order:
- **2.8 (decode-correct, bounded `load_latest_node`) lands before Phases 7 and 10**; it is the only baseline reader for 7, 8 and 12.
- **Phase 10 lands before or together with Phase 4.** Its read rule was to ship one release before its writer; Phase 10b dropped that split by owner decision (no deployment needs the rolling upgrade).
- **Phase 9 hard-depends on the Phase 2 repairs** for each CF and branch, and on the watermark.
- **Phase 12's CF registration (12.0) ships one release before any writer.**
- Every repair, backfill and rebuild streams with bounded batches, a resumable cursor and a disk precheck, stays admin-triggered, and fans out through the admin endpoint with per-node state.
- Phase 13 is listed before Phase 12 because it is read-only and lower risk. Its `{localized_path}` template variable lights up once Phase 12 reaches Ready.
